- **Why did hw151c still found nothing with S6 on (read 2026-10-11, reviewed on Fable 09:30)?** S6 did its part - growth drew 0 ("0 to grow on" in 41 kit lines) - but the kit never banked: the stock sat 0-16k because the fleets away (28-36k a month) and the navy charge (15-22k) draw before the feed, and `ThreatFuel.canFound` needs a supplies `surplus` (stock covering `buildDays` of demand) while the chest is full. The chest read full all run (89k -> 137k FP of a 42k horizon, no transition line) **wrongly**: `ThreatOffensive.cheapestProngCost` tests a prong against FP and fuel only, and c's fund could sail nothing - 39 passes "nothing the means pay", 15 "the best prong is out on the supplies away" - so by the hw70 rule ("a fund that fields nothing is poor, not full") it should have read poor, the expansion tithe would have gone on and the kit alone would have founded. The deadlock: supplies 0 -> no prong sails -> fund full -> chest full -> founding needs a surplus -> no spread -> production stays 88k (28 of 31 hives already forges, "runs dry, wants a spare" every month, +0 building, 905k fuel idle). Demand 142k a month trailing. The losing hold fired 24 passes in c (losing 0.32-0.34, paid 29k from the fund to Ogre and Yama in 23 payments) and 9 in b (0.30-0.32); it is not the cause. b on the same build: away 17-26k, stock 53k -> 145k, chest "spent" at d~3,900, founded 33 (38 -> 66 hives). `game-runs-2.md` hw151.
- **F1 built: a chest full only if the supplies away keep a prong (user, 2026-10-11 09:20, "do what you recommend").** `ThreatOffensive.cheapestProngCost` now records the cheapest priced prong whose passage the fuel pays AND whose supplies away the free stock and the spare keep (the campaign's own first-prong test); none such, the chest reads poor (`ThreatStance.chestFull` false), the expansion tithe runs and `ThreatFuel.canFound` needs the kit alone. No knob: it is the hw70 rule ("a fund that fields nothing is poor, not full") applied to supplies. Trial hw152 from the hw148 b/c snapshots against hw149 (71 / 33) and hw151 (63 / 30). Not built, the user's: F2 (the kit as a `ThreatFuel.reserve` ahead of fleets away and the navy), F3 (recycle garrisons held above want faster when the spare is short). -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "The chest".
- **How do I count the hive garrison builder's swarms in a run log (2026-10-13)?** `Garrison fleet fabricated at X (n, floor f, N FP, ...)` lines, one per `maintainColonyGarrisons` build (`Garrison swarm fabricated into a standing fleet` = the same builder growing a fleet; `Posture: X fabricated a` = `fabricateFor`, the reinforcement builder - a different path). Window by the `Clock: day ... war N` lines. hw177c w2,863-3,807: 463 / 113k FP; hw180a: 718 / 172k.
- **Why did hw177c sit at 47-49 hives for 900 days on 347k fuel (2026-10-13)?** hw151c's deadlock with F1 satisfied: the chest read full legitimately (fund 45k of a 44.6k horizon, cheapest prong 7.4k the supplies keep), so `ThreatFuel.canFound` asked a supplies `surplus` (stock >= `buildDays` of demand) that a 1-10k stock spending all of +80-100k/mo on fleets (52k -> 113k FP) never meets; 49 seedings held. A held seeding books its own kit as supplies demand monthly (`ThreatFuel.held`), so 49 held inflated the trailing demand to 182,562/mo against 101,250 made ("runs dry, wants a spare" - phantom demand the planner answers with producers): a flow test (K1) locks the same way and is withdrawn (Fable's review). Order for the user (`game-runs-2.md` s51): K3 first - why the full chest spent only ~7 strikes a 300-day bin (a chest that spends reopens the founding by the existing rule); then, if the founding is touched, K4 - leave the held foundings' own bookings out of the demand the surplus test reads; K2 (= F2) fixes only the spent-chest arm. **K3 read: the spent arm dominated** - the chest read spent ~730 of the last ~1,000 days (state lines print on change) and every offensive pass read "0 free" supplies (fund 55k -> 72k unspent); the last month's supplies went feed 40.0k / navy 32.6k / away 24.5k / build 5.0k / found 0 of 101k made - the navy's upkeep leaves neither a kit nor a prong's trip free. K2 is the fix for most of the stall, K4 for the full spells. The navy fit fired and was satisfied (451 recycles, last w3,615 "spare 4950 of 5000 a month wanted, 44 send(s) held") - its want (`fitWant`) is one kit a month as a flow, never banked; whether it should want the held sends' kits is the user's call.
- **hw152 (F1, 2026-10-11 09:52): c 40 hives, b 59 against hw149 33 / 71 and hw151 30 / 63 - c is unfrozen.** c founded 17 (0 in hw149 and hw151), turned 33 -> 40 from ~d4,500 with 6 sends held at the end (37-43 all through hw151c); churn unchanged (29 eradicated / 32 landings). The chest read spent at once, flapped full twice on 15-31 FP prongs the supplies could keep (a wrinkle: a trivial prong re-gates founding for a month), the fund banked 104k unspent with 1.4M fuel - the fund still cannot buy supplies. b 59, below hw149's 71 (b's spread started later, stock 180k -> 4k founding 37 -> 59). Not won yet: by the loop c's snapshots (`...hw148cd3620hw152cd4836`) stay the test bed. `game-runs-2.md` hw152.
- **The chest's prong floor (user, 2026-10-11 10:00, "just do recc and continue"):** a prong counts toward a full chest only if it costs at least a month's saving of the fund (`ThreatColonyManager.strikeFundPerMonth`, about 3.5k in c) - hw152c's chest flipped full twice on prongs of 15 and 31 FP and re-gated the foundings for the month. In `ThreatOffensive.cheapestProngCost`, no knob. Trial hw153 on the hw148 snapshots against hw152 (59 / 40).
- **hw153 (prong floor, 2026-10-11 10:26): c 51, b 53 (hw152 40 / 59; hw149 33 / 71) - the chest stayed spent and c turned round 34 -> 51, founding 44 (17 / 0 before).** The swarm is unfrozen on this save: foundings 44 / 72 against 47 / 68 eradicated in 1,200 days - the frontier churn (a landing a month, almost every one a hive lost) is now the whole war, and the spread out-founds it by a half. Still open, the user's: the fund idles at 150-190k FP with 1.1-1.4M fuel while supplies bind every prong (the fund cannot buy supplies); the churn itself (handover 9c's S2 found within a core garrison's reach, S7 strike the siege bases). `game-runs-2.md` hw153.
- **A failing run becomes the checkpoint (user, 2026-10-10 19:00).** "We should always switch to specific checkpoint when we have a failing run so we can try different strategies until threat win. Then if they win we go back to random seed general testing." A batch game the swarm loses is the test bed: strategies are tried from that game's own saves (the situation that beat it, not a fresh war from the pre-war checkpoint) until the Threat wins it; then testing returns to random seeds. -> `testing-harness.md`
- **Batches from a pre-war checkpoint and stopped at war day 3,800 (user, 2026-10-10 18:55, "need to speed things up").** A 4,700-day batch is 50 minutes at ~100 game days a minute; every game on the OceanPena seed is identical to day 1,500, the war opens at 2,000-2,100, and both losing games read by 3,800. ck1 on the OceanPena seed (made 19:05, last save at war day 1,883, the war opened at 2,005) is from the current build (the opening bar and the pass gate are pre-war changes; remake it after any other); batches run `-Bases` from it with `-Days` to reach 3,800 (~32 minutes: the war runs ~68 days a minute, hw146; 18 was the whole-run average). hw145 (the fixed hold) was stopped at ~day 1,000 for it and reruns from ck1 as hw146. -> `testing-harness.md`
- **hw144 (losing hold, first cut; 2026-10-10 18:42): 81 / 9 / 68 hives** on 216k / 11k / 79k FP, humans 12 / 28 / 33 colonies; found by 8 of 8 factions in all three; b lost under 498k FP of sieges, c held under 525k - the navy per hive against the strongest attacker's median siege decides (a 2.7k, c 1.2-2.7k, b 0.8k vs 1,200). -> `game-runs-2.md` hw144, handover 45
- **Why did hw144b's navy per hive fall 2.3k -> 0.8k with the losing hold firing (2026-10-10 18:30)?** The first cut priced "a month's fund" every monthly pass, which is the fund's whole income, so 8.3k FP of 13 passes reached the hives (the fund read 545-753 at the passes); the strikes did shrink (33 after the hold, 0.6-2.9k each, 31 of them drawing garrison spare). The siege exchange d3,300-3,750 was 1:1 (336 fights, 66k Threat FP lost, 62k human) on a 10k FP/mo forge income, and half the fights found no Threat fleet over the world. Fixed before hw145: the hives are paid first, the campaign prices from what is left. -> `strikes-staged.md` section 4
- **Losing holds the navy (user, 2026-10-10 17:50, "You can try it" - D1 after hw143a).** From losing pressure `offensiveHoldLosing` 0.3 the offensive prices only a month's fund and the rest pays the pressed hives' garrisons (`ThreatOffensive.pass`, `payDefence(keep)`); bends the 2026-10-07 "losing spends" rule. The line a hive fight is decided on: navy per hive against the strongest attacker's median siege (hw143: a 2.0k -> 1.0k against 1,625 lost; b 1.6k held; c 2.2k+ grew). First run hw144. -> `strikes-staged.md` section 4
- **Did the opening bar (`strikeStagedOpenFP` 40000) work (hw143, 2026-10-10)?** Yes: opened at war day 1,947-2,036 with 18-23 hives and a 32-38k navy, nobody had seen a hive before any opening, 0 / 34 / 36 hives at the end (hw142 same seed 0 / 0 / 0). The day-2,000 rule is gone (a collapsed d2,700-3,650 under Hegemony's 108k campaign while the offensive, losing at 0.3-0.6 and so striking sooner by the 2026-10-07 rule, spent 39k FP from d3,000). -> `game-runs-2.md` hw143, handover 44
- **No encirclement of the core (user, 2026-10-10 16:50, "encirclement would spread them thin and make reinforcements harder").** After the opening bar: the swarm takes as long as it needs to open the war (nothing wakes the humans but a strike - `mobiliseAtPhase` 0, strikes give no lead, a hive found before the war draws no siege), but it does NOT claim a ring of staging hives around the known core worlds - losing games were found by 4-5 factions, winners by one, and a ring sits where the eyes are. Open on readiness is the fund bar (`strikeStagedOpenFP`), not a positional test. Built the same hour: attacked first means open now - the bar holds only while nobody has mobilised and nobody has struck at the hive (`IncursionManager.openingWaits`: `ThreatWarState.warFactionIds` empty and `ThreatAlarm.alarm` 0 - raids, fronts and the player's own fights feed the alarm); the offensive prices no prong while it holds. -> `strikes-staged.md`
- **Open the war later; the two inert fixes off (user, 2026-10-10 16:30, "why don't we just raise the minimum strike fp bar so it doesn't attack until it has more worlds", after hw142).** hw142 lost 3 of 3 with defence first on: the fund (73-98k FP a game) built swarms at the hives under siege one at a time into the siege (hw142a: 270 fabrications, 37k FP, at the paid hives), and the offensive froze 37-38 passes while the coalition grew (hw142a faced 107 sieges / 118k FP in war days 2,500-3,000 with no strike at their bases) - harm, not help. `threatinc_defenceFirst` false and `threatinc_consolidateLosing` 0 (code kept); `threatinc_strikeStagedOpenFP` 40000: the war's first strike waits until the fund holds 40k (from ~12-22k at the day ~1,300 opening), so the answer that lands 190-420 days later meets the navy the unpressed games reach by then. Trial hw143. -> `handover-2026-10-08-morning.md` 43
- **The player is outside the opening (user, 2026-10-11, "the threat should respect its opening threshold for the core worlds, but not for the player"; BUILT, untested in game).** The 40k bar (`strikeStagedOpenFP`) and the 8k floor (`strikeStagedMinFP`) hold for NPC worlds only: a player world is open at every phase (`warOpen`), and from phase 2 is struck the pass the fund pays a strike sized to its defence, once a scout or the hive's eyes have seen it (`strikeSeen`) - no grudge needed, `playerGraceDays` 120 between player strikes. While the bar holds `ThreatOffensive.pass` campaigns against the player's worlds alone, and a pre-opening strike at the player sweeps no NPC world in the system (it would mobilise that faction). `openingWaits` reads NPC wars and NPC grudge only (`ThreatWarState.npcAtWar`, `ThreatAlarm.npcAlarm`): neither the player's Mobilise button nor the player's raids open the war on the core (an NPC never attacks before it is struck at default settings - `mobiliseAtPhase` 0 - so the swarm's own strike is in practice the only opening). Grudge (+0.5 a raid or tactical pass, +2 a stratum, +10 an eradication - fleet battles earn none; fades 20% a month) still weights the player's worlds x(1 + 0.2 x grudge) and an eradication draws `IncursionManager.retaliate`. Scouting Swarms are not aimed at the attacker: from phase 2 every capable hive scouts unknown inhabited systems in its fuel reach, nearest first (`ThreatSwarmScouts.launchAll`). -> `strikes-staged.md` "Size"
- **The defence is paid before the offensive, under pressure read now (user, 2026-10-10 15:15, "C1", after hw141b).** Ten games on the OceanPena seed are identical to war day 1,500 and decided by 2,000 by how many factions find and press the swarm (losers 24-41 sieges from 4-5 factions against a 14-24k navy; winners 0-7, navy tripled by 2,500); the consolidation fired and the games wiped anyway, and through the campaign the offensive launched 13-50 strikes (17-85k FP) while the 365-day losing trend read 0.05-0.36. C1: while any hive faces a force (present, or seen over it within the window) beyond what stands for it, the strike fund and the launch stock feed that defence first and no strike leaves a region whose hives cannot stand what is coming - pressure read daily from the rally's force readings, never the trend. Options C2 (consolidation on the same pressure) and C3 (later opening) in handover 41. -> `handover-2026-10-08-morning.md` 41
- **A losing swarm consolidates on its keystones (user, 2026-10-10 noon, "if you recommend A still let's try that", after hw138b).** hw138b: 30 hives -> 6 with 260-290k fuel in stock - supplies-bound at its peak, garrisons of 127 FP ground by six factions' 511 daily sieges at 5:1, 0 regional rallies in the collapse window (only-if-enough: "1,311 FP could stand against 1,677"), the offensive held on an empty fund. Of the options (handover 39: A consolidate, B the fund to relief first, C rally regardless) the user took A: losing at `consolidateLosing` 0.5 the garrisons mass on as many keystones as can each stand the largest force seen over a hive in 120 days, the keystones the hives whose input cover the economy would miss most; the rest keep their patrols. Built as `ThreatPosture.consolidate` (wants and needs overridden, the pressure pass moves the swarms); first run hw139. -> `swarm-defence.md` "Consolidation"
- **The economy plans for redundancy - a balance across inputs (user, 2026-10-10, after the hw137c fuel-lock read; replaced four rules the same day).** "There needs to be a balance across all resource types. So that way, it doesn't really matter what's going on with wars unless they hit all of the volatiles points at once ... it's just redundancy is what we're going for"; planned on potential ("assuming all colonies it goes for are size 8"), never on blockades ("that's for the war planner"). `ThreatColonyManager.mineableNeeds`: cover = full-strength worlds' worth per input (each live hive's size-8 output capped at a size-8 consumer's draw, ore 10 / rare ore 8 / volatiles 8, summed over the draw); behind = (lead - cover) / lead; `needBonus` = 30 x (min(draw, output) x behind + rise over the hive's best size-8 source, up to the largest consumer's want, x consumers) - the user: "something that increases overall supply is worth a lot if we've never found a planet that rich". Weights the claim (`systemNeedScore`), the first colony and the order a held system fills. Every save reads ore leading 20-40 worlds, volatiles 4.5-11 (strongest pull), rare ore 10-24. Built 2026-10-10, untested. -> `hive-economy.md` "Where a colony lands"
- **Launch by fleet points (user, 2026-10-09 20:30, "go" on option A after hw132-hw134).** A quiet colony launches what it holds above its reserve's FP (`held - ThreatPosture.minimumFP`), fleets fitted largest first as the pressed path already did, never its last fleet (`ThreatColonyManager.ownAvailableForLaunch`); the fleet count minus `garrisonReserve` (a swarm count from the size table) gated it before, and under the hull pool's FP want no quiet colony ever had a swarm to spare before the war. First run hw135a/b/c (OceanPena, day 0). hw135 stayed locked (one founding fleet of ~745 FP a colony, never its last fleet): second cut 6210e81c - `launchOrder` (the fleets a launch may take, fitted largest first, skipping one too big, read by the count and the muster alike) and `splitLaunchSwarm` (a swarm's worth carved off the largest fleet when nothing fits, `ThreatFleetComposer.splitOff`); hw136. hw136: the navy fit recycled each piece the poll after (smallest on station; bound by sends held on FUEL): third cut 6ae245db - `fitWant` counts only sends held on supplies (`ThreatFuel.heldOnSuppliesThisMonth`), `fitFloor` holds a forge colony's `stockFP`, and its stock fleets (`launchStockFleets`) are never the fit's victim; hw137: first claims day 760-791, 17 / 16 / 17 hives at the opening (hw132: 4 / 11 / 4), end 49 / 62 / 2 hives vs 35 / 32 / 32 human colonies - the lock is gone, the seed contested (`game-runs-2.md` 43, handover 37). -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "The fleet count locks"
- **The carry removed; a new-seed game from the start (user, 2026-10-09 17:00, after hw130/hw131).** The belt guard fixed the freeze (overdue reads 1 / 3 / 0, no carry), so the stopgap that carried an overdue reinforcement to 1,500 units from its target goes (`ThreatColonyManager.overdue` now only re-orders and logs - the read stays as the alarm). On the swarm winning both sectors (old 251 hives / 2 colonies, new 219 / 344 vs 0 / 1): "can you do a brand new test with new seed from game start" - a freshly generated sector, run from day 0 rather than a checkpoint. Read hw132 (`game-runs-2.md` 43): the swarm loses all three games (hives 0 / 3 / 0 at day ~3,200, humans 43-45 colonies); in a and c it claimed nothing before the war (4 hives at the opening, day 1,365) - ck5 and ck2 opened at 32 and 38. -> `swarm-defence.md` "The engine, decompiled"
- **The noon questions answered (user, 2026-10-09 afternoon, after hw119/hw120).** Old sector (ck2, 89 / 49 / 44 / 151 / 65 hives on one build): build **regional relief** - a besieged hive draws garrison swarms from hives within fuel reach, sized to the siege it can see, sent only if the pooled force beats it (the humans' coalition relief mirrored; "nothing sails that cannot win" holds). **counterGap kept** (the holding front may feed the counter-attack line - a recorded exception to the 2026-09-08 "the front is never stripped"). **The carry stays, and the freeze's cause is still hunted** (`ThreatColonyManager.overdue`; next: decompile `CampaignFleet.advance` / `ModularFleetAI`, and hw119's tiny source-hive bodies, radius 45-49). **beltSafe removed** (`ThreatFleetComposer.beltSafe`: hw111 had refuted it - frozen swarms held with the key set; the reason given the user, "belt impacts run only in the player's location", came from the broken frame pulse and was wrong). Older items: **structures stay paid before foundings** (`affordStructure` from raw stock, then a founding above `ThreatFuel.free`); **no StarLord save heal** (dropped). Defaults taken: `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` split under the 40 KB rule; the four human-side shapes of hw60 stay unbuilt (humans must not win unattended). -> `handover-2026-10-09-noon.md` 1
- **The swarm keeps no navy it cannot supply and use (user, 2026-10-08, after hw73a: fleets 109k FP on 78k supplies a month, stock 0 a year, 29-31 sends held).** "if it cant strike cause it cant afford to cause its maintaining too many fleets then yeah it shouldnt maintain more than it can supply and use". The fund hoarding (300k+ FP on the new sector): "dont care for now they won anyway". Built as the navy fits the spare (`ThreatColonyManager.fitNavyToSpare`, knob `navyFitsSpare`, `swarm-navy-fit.md`): swarms above the floor recycled into the bank until the spare covers a held send, no transfer on a short flow to a world nobody attacks; trial hw77 (ck2) + hw78 (ck5). Read (hw77/hw78, 17:30): swarm stronger on both sectors - old sector 55 hives (hw75a 35), humans 36 (38); new 191 / 177 hives (162 / 132), humans 9 / 4; ~880k FP recycled a game but 2,161 swarms fabricated alongside (churn through the bank); ck5 still holds 50 sends a month on a positive spare (the build spend drains the stock first - unconfirmed). User (2026-10-08, 17:50): "the two wrinkles need to be addressed, not another strategy layer". Churn fixed: the nexus builds to the fit's floor while the fit binds (`ThreatColonyManager.fitBinding` / `fitFloor`, swarm-navy-fit.md "The builder respects the fit"); the ck5 holds were read as the queue beyond a fully spent income (foundings 5-6 a month, structures paid from the raw stock before foundings), not a defect - trial hw79 (ck2) + hw80 (ck5). Read (hw79/hw80, 19:40): the nexus churn gone (same hive-month built-then-recycled 66, was 4,019; a first gate on the fit binding NOW still churned - the feed records the spare before the fit recycles - so the floor holds a month after the last recycle, `fitActive`); old sector 92 hives / 23 human worlds (was 55 / 36), new 177 / 87 hives, 4 / 4 worlds. Next loop: the pressure pass fabricates for receivers short of the POSTURE want (4,330, 1,060 recycled at the same hive) - its want becomes the fit floor while the fit is active, hw81/hw82. Built: `ThreatPosture.wantFP` returns `ThreatColonyManager.fitFloor` (patrols or need, plus the staged strike) for a colony nobody attacks while `fitActive` - one want for the nexus, the pressure pass and the launch gates (swarm-navy-fit.md "The fit's floor is the colony's want"). hw81a mid-run (20:05): recycling a third of hw79a's, but the pass still fabricated for a quiet receiver whose want moved with the pressure (Gream, nine a month, recycled on arrival) - so while the fit is active nothing is fabricated for a receiver nobody attacks, and Read (hw81/hw82, 20:20): recycled 1,241 (was 2,284), nexus 1,199, but the pass fabricated 5,200 and 395 were recycled at the receiver (Gream, unattacked, its want moving with the pressure); old sector 70 hives / 22 worlds, new 185 / 180 hives, 2 / 3 worlds. the fit skips a colony recentlyReceived (swarm-navy-fit.md "Nothing fabricated for a quiet hive"); hw83/hw84. Read (hw83/hw84, 23:10): loop three closed (pairs 252, 3 a month), but 4,501 swarms / 470k FP fabricated for hives under attack and fed in a swarm at a time, 40 hives eradicated (old sector 53 hives / 25 worlds); the pass's enough gate follows (below). hw85/hw86 (f23c009d, hw83's build with the academy guard, read 00:05): old sector 61 hives / 31 worlds, 5,441 fabricated for attacked hives, 18 eradicated; new 133 / 156 hives vs 7 / 3 worlds, no fatal.
- **Trial the recommended human fixes (user, 2026-10-08, "trial your recommended human fix, either one or some together i dont mind").** After hw71/hw72 (the swarm overruns both sectors, humans down to 3-10 colonies). First: yards at home (`ThreatFrontlines.homeYards`, knob `homeYards`, `hull-pool.md` 3) - one change a batch; coalition relief and Starve sized to the pools stay unbuilt until it is read. Read (hw73/hw74, 15:00): the old sector holds (humans 28 of 50, hives 50 -> 34, yards 5-7k FP a month, Hegemony keeps all 10 worlds), the new sector still falls (humans 5 / 4, hives 91 / 84) because the swarm takes the yard worlds within three years - a yard never outlives its world. Second (user, 2026-10-08, "next human change sounds good"): coalition relief (`ThreatCoalition.jointRelief`, knob `coalitionRelief`, `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Coalition relief") - the owner and its partners relieve a world together when none is enough alone; trial hw75 (ck2) + hw76 (ck5). Partners = every war faction not hostile (the trade rule), after a first run found 0 partners on the Favourable gate. Read (hw75/hw76, 16:35): the old sector trends human (humans 38 of 50, hives 52 -> 27 -> 35, 103 joint reliefs sent), the new sector still falls (humans 3 / 6, hives 162 / 132; the coalition fields 0.26-0.29 of the armies there).
- **A full chest must be fieldable (user, 2026-10-08, "do recc fix for swarm first", after hw70).** The chest reads full only if the strike fund holds a horizon's saving AND covers the cheapest priced prong whose passage the fuel in stock pays (`ThreatOffensive.cheapestProngCost`, read in `ThreatStance.evaluate`; the ck4 remake read full at 8,700 against a 7,968 FP prong with no fuel - out on the fuel is not hoarding); a fund that fields nothing is poor, and the expansion that grows its income goes on. hw70 (`game-runs-2.md` 43): ck3's pre-war chest read full at 1,834 of 1,800 FP with the cheapest prong at 8,244, held the spread 1,400 days, the swarm opened at 13 hives and the humans won a game. Tested on both sectors at once from hw71 (ck2) + hw72 (ck4, ck3 remade on this build).
- **Sustenance first - the forge is the income, the navy the expense (user, 2026-10-08, "1 sounds good").** After hw68a's collapse (`facts` "why does it collapse rather than settle"): the hive worlds' break-even sustenance is paid before the fleets away and the navy at home, from the whole stock; a navy the supplies cannot keep loses swarms smallest-first, never forges. The user's reading, confirmed: "the navy will be smaller but only temporarily until the new world forges are at capacity ... its an investment rather than max navy now future output is compromised". Built as `ThreatColonyUpkeep.sustenanceDue` (the hold `paySupplies` / `payNavySupplies` / `ThreatReach.freeStock` leave), knob `sustenanceFirst`; the counter-stroke knob off by default for the clean test (hw69 against hw67). **hw69 (2026-10-08): confirmed** - the swarm wins all three games (hives 67 / 50 / 58, eradicated 1 / 9 / 11, income 102k / 110k / 92k a month); hw69b ran its stock to 0 at hw68a's collapse point and lost 14 swarms instead of any forge size. Not built, the user's to pick after hw69: founding paced to the spare after the navy; a campaign's away-burn fitting the spare rather than the stock.
- **The humans must not win an unattended run (user, 2026-10-08).** "Remember the player isnt involved in these runs. if the humans win the mod isnt working correctly." The overnight session read hw66/67 (the swarm beaten down in hw66a / hw67c, 42 / 30 hives eradicated) as a good "contested" result; by this criterion those games are failures to solve with swarm strategy - never by undoing the human-side coherence fixes (bases bound by hulls, relief only if enough). First answer built: the counter-stroke (`IncursionManager.counterStrike`, hw68; `strikes-staged.md` 6).
- **The front is never stripped and is drawn by geography; patrols hold the corridor (user, 2026-10-07, after hw59).** "Frontline worlds should never be stripped, whether humans know about them or not - how would threat know if humans know anyway, they could be attacked at any moment. Like any war the point of the frontline is to be strong. The threat patrols should traverse the space between the front and human colonies once they are found to prevent scouts getting through." SUPERSEDES the weighted no-hard-never-take gather of the same day for front colonies. hw59a: one 10,460 FP strike at Sentinel Forward Base took 52 fleets from all 15 colonies, besieged systems included, and nine of them were eradicated in the months after (18 of 21 falls followed a strip; hw58a 2 of 31). Built: `ThreatPosture.frontline` = any exposure (within `postureExposureFarLY` 30 of a human world the swarm has seen, or hostiles seen lately); `IncursionManager.stagedSpares` offers a front colony only what it holds above its want (tier 2 is the rear alone); `ThreatSwarmPatrols.planRoute` sends a front system's Patrol Swarms through the systems nearest the line from home to the nearest known human world (`corridorLY`) instead of the ring around home. Before a human world is known there is no front. -> `strikes-staged.md` 2, `information-by-ship.md` 3.
- **Overnight 2026-10-07/08 (user):** "solve problems like we talked about with strategy, not just twisting knobs back and forth. and obviously still bugfix" - every fix this night names the premise it changes; knobs only where a measured value is plainly wrong.
- **Defence in depth and defeat in detail (user, 2026-10-07, "yes lets build two suggested to start").** After hw58-59 (garrisons 6.5k of 15-19k wanted, campaigns of 300-5,700 FP spread over four factions, strikes razing nothing) and the user: "you always suggest a knob to turn, but you never come up with strategic ideas". Two shape changes, both the swarm reading only what it has seen: (1) `ThreatPosture.exposure` - a quiet hive system wants its base x exposure, 1 within `postureExposureNearLY` (10) of a human world the swarm knows or while hostiles were seen in it within `postureExposureSeenDays` (180), 0 (one swarm a colony) at `postureExposureFarLY` (30) and beyond or with no human world known; the rear's garrison and output flow to the hives the humans have found. (2) `ThreatOffensive.chooseFocus` - the campaign masses on one faction, every known system of it (its prongs share its one navy), until its worlds answer the strikes with under `offensiveBrokenAnswer` (0.5) of what they were sized for (`ThreatSwarmIntel.answerShare`) or nothing known of it is left; then the faction the means cover most completely with the most value. Spoiling blows while losing stay. The ranges, days and share are my defaults. Changes the pre-war phase (rear hives hold one swarm): ck1 remade as ck2. -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "Exposure", `strikes-staged.md` 5.
- **Where a strike takes its swarms: the safest worlds first, then the biggest surplus, never a hard never-take (user, 2026-10-07).** SUPERSEDED the same evening for the front (next entry but one): a front colony is never taken below its want. On hw58a (three frontline systems milked to 1-2 fleets, a rear world sitting on 11): "priority pull from safest, then from those highest above posture threshold ... there should be no hard never take, just weighted ... prefer not to strip a world clean". Built as `IncursionManager.stagedSpares` two tiers (every colony's fleets above its posture want before any below it, down to the last fleet) ordered by `threatinc_spareGatherDistanceShare` (0.55) of distance from the nearest known human world and the rest surplus. -> `strikes-staged.md` 2
- **Test from a pre-war checkpoint, three games at once (user, 2026-10-07).** "Why do we have to wait 15 mins every time, why not just have a save from right before the threat attack? Most of the issues we are tracking happen after then anyway" Three at once: my first speed figures were wrong (the 216 days a minute was the EMPTY pre-war phase of one game; at war a game runs 90-130 a minute alone or one of three), so after one sequential batch the user: "no yeah do 3 at once". `sbs.ps1 -Checkpoint ckN` makes `save_XckN` from the last save before the first launch; batches are one `sbs.ps1 -Tags hwNNa,hwNNb,hwNNc -Bases "hwNNa=save_XckN;..."` run (~30 min to war day 3800), the checkpoint remade after any pre-war change. -> `testing-harness.md` "A checkpoint save from before the war"
- **One hull economy from vanilla's own figures, every side (user, 2026-10-06).** On learning NPC war fleets are paid in supplies and fuel with no hull production (`ThreatSoftening.payableFP`) and the player's in an `aidBaseFP` allowance: "if they do they bloody shouldnt, and there should be parity with the threat. FP require heavy industry". Then: "use all vanilla calculations for everything? Each world/forward base has its own fleet size and num patrols spawned, and we can take that FP % and quality calc to determine how much that contributes to the pool that they can use for staging? should apply to player, threat and human factions. we should also factor in quality. as per vanilla average quality should be the best producer so if theres a pristine nanoforge somewhere that effects all spawned fleets". Agreed design: a market's standing hulls = vanilla's patrol counts (`PATROL_NUM_*_MOD`, from its military structure and size) x vanilla's patrol FP by weight, through `Misc.getAdjustedStrength` (quality, fleet size %, officer doctrine); a faction's pool is the sum, held while a fleet is out, back when home; a lost fleet is rebuilt only from Heavy Industry / Orbital Works `ships` output x `fabFPPerShipUnit` - no Heavy Industry, no replacement. Player, NPC factions and hives alike; `aidBaseFP` goes. -> `hull-pool.md`
- **Human and Threat build logic identical (user, 2026-10-06).** "build to bridge the shortage, and have the ability to rebuild, i.e. decommision industry or structure in favour of new structure to address shortage, but this shouldnt be instant" - the hive's stock plan read per faction (`ThreatFactionStock`), hulls as a third stock; after hw31 built fuel plants first on million-fuel hoards. -> `frontlines.md` "The faction's stock plan"
- **Standing upkeep in supplies, both sides (user, 2026-10-06, "Go", after hw33-35).** "we don't want the game to be decided by who reaches the compound threshold first": every FP a side holds - human standing hulls in the pool, swarm garrisons - pays vanilla-rate maintenance a month whether it moves or not, from the reserves above their floors and holds / the hive stock; unpaid, hulls are lost at the rate the shortfall says. Navies settle where production pays them (closed economy, no cap); a beaten side's small navy is cheap to keep, a big one dear. Built 2026-10-06 (`ThreatHulls.maintain`, `ThreatColonyManager.payGarrisonSupplies`): rate = each side's own measured maintenance per FP (`ThreatReach.suppliesPerFP`) x knob `standingUpkeepMult` (1; 0 off); a hull-month unpaid is a hull lost - off a faction's built navy then its debt, the hive's smallest swarm on station. Not the player's faction (it keeps the old rule, as its fleets do in `ThreatUpkeep`) - raised with the user. First run hw36.
- **The hive's navy above its patrols pays supplies (user, 2026-10-07).** "Might as well charge them supplies if threat has surplus anyway": garrison FP above `ThreatPosture.minimumFP` x the swarm's rate x `standingUpkeepMult` from the hive stock (`ThreatColonyManager.payNavySupplies`), the swarm lost smallest-first once a month is owed, never below the patrols. The patrols stay vanilla's. Parity with `ThreatHulls.builtAtHome`.
- **The swarm saves and strikes everything at once; losing, the near only (user, 2026-10-07).** "Why would they attack the same amount every month when attacking all at once would be a million times more effective ... they should go through periods where they are saving and then do a multi pronged attack on all known systems. Of course if they are losing ... they might want to focus on nearby bases only ... It depends on their resources vs the known resources of their enemy. So dynamic strategy." Built as `ThreatOffensive` (`strikes-staged.md` 2); `strikeMinSize` kept at the user's word; the horizon (12 / 3 months) and the near range (10 ly) are my defaults, untested.
- **Spend the navy on the offensive; "losing" is a trend, not a one-off (user, 2026-10-07, handover).** "option 1 spend it yes": the offensive's prongs take the hive's spare Defense Swarms above the patrol table first, the fund building only what is missing (`strikeStagedGarrisons`, with the fixes `handover-2026-10-07-offensive.md` 2 lists). And "losing should not mean one lost opening or hives falling, it should be more nuanced ... likely more a series of worlds falling not just a one off": a trend over a year, a series of windows, weighed against the swarm's means, in degrees - the design to confirm is in the same handover. Both built 2026-10-07 for hw48 (`strikes-staged.md` 3-4): prongs take the spare up to their need, a pass shares it, held prongs earmark theirs, the navy charge they stop paying offsets their supplies away; losing is a 0-1 pressure over 365 days, the larger of hives fallen below the year's peak (none for one, full at half the peak) and the net exchange against the year's fabrication (full at 1x), 0 with no war, consolidating no longer counted - it shrinks the reach from all known toward 10 ly and the horizon from 12 toward 3 months by degree. The shape was mine; the user confirmed it 2026-10-07 ("yes proceed with tests") and hw48 tests it.
- **Innate patrols, tangible actions (user, 2026-10-06, after hw37, "cut it").** Every world on both sides keeps its innate patrols by vanilla's systems (the Military Base / Nexus demand, shortages shrinking fleet size and quality - the standing-hulls figure reads that), and nothing the mod does charges them; the mod's actions - voyages, fleets out, structures, and a navy BUILT beyond vanilla's table - draw on the tangible war stocks. So: standing upkeep on a faction's `built` hulls only, the hive's garrison charge and growth gate removed, fuel unchanged. Parity with the player's systems. Overnight batches to tune (hw38+).
- **Navies bound by supplies, both sides (user, 2026-10-06, "Go", after hw36).** hw36a: the swarm's garrison is grown from forge FP to the posture want, so the standing charge took 100% of its supplies income every day (76k/mo, 43 forges) and never shrank the navy - its strikes starved instead, no war in 3,750 days; hw36b/c humans paid 90-100% and lost 9-33k FP to starvation. Fix: a hive grows no swarm its supplies surplus (`ThreatFuel.perMonth` - trailing demand) cannot keep, a faction's yards bank no built hull its surplus (`ThreatFactionStock`) cannot keep (losses still rebuild first), a hive's first swarm always; unpaid standing upkeep is booked as demand on both sides; a held strike books its supplies shortfall as demand like its fuel (`ThreatFuel.held`). Rate knob stays 1.0. First run hw37.
- **Humans trade through the war: allied aid convoys, yards first, no deadlock on one world (user, 2026-10-06, after hw33b).** "trade shouldnt stop because they are at war. if heg is short another faction that has surplus can send supplies right. should be an actual physical supply convoy like the player can send" - NOT paying from another faction's stockpile: a faction whose stock plan reads short (`ThreatFactionStock`) is sent a real convoy by a war faction whose plan reads surplus, like the player's aid missions; and a faction with hulls short reserves its yard's 5,000 supplies before upkeep and hunts draw the stock down ("yes both"). Also the wave fix: a seeding wave alive past its deadline is lost and re-sent, and the first fuel plant ignores the volatiles gate (hw34b: a bootstrap wave stalled for ten years, the hive never had fuel).
- **Ship hulls move like any good (user, 2026-10-07, after hw50).** "vanilla sends convoys all the time with ship hulls, marines, heavy armaments etc": the allied aid convoy carries `ships` units to a hull-short faction, credited to its hull pool (`ThreatHulls`) at `fabFPPerShipUnit` as its own yards build, the sender's pool debited; and the player's aid missions ask for whatever a colony is short of - hulls, armaments, marines - "not just supplies or fuel". hw50c: Tri-Tachyon sat on 8,262 free FP while the Persean League's sieges waited on 850. Built for hw52 (`ThreatCoalition.aidHulls`, `ThreatHulls.hullStatus/spareUnits/give/receive`, `ThreatReserves.AID_COMMODITIES`, knob `hullAidKeepFraction` 0.5). -> `player-aid.md` 4-5, `hull-pool.md`
- **A convoy lands anywhere; hull contracts sail from the player's yards, priced as warships (user, 2026-10-10).** Nova Maxios (size 5, no Waystation) posted a 4-unit hull contract the player could not meet: the port hand-over reads the `ships` commodity, vanilla's `meta` good that is never in a hold, and the board's convoy refused hulls and any colony without a Waystation ("No Waystation: nothing sails from here or lands here"). "In reality that doesnt make sense and should be removed. obviously ships can still get there just accessibility is less. aid should allow me to deliver hulls but ... 400fp of aid for $1000 is retarded." Built: the recipient's Waystation gate is gone everywhere (`ThreatConvoys.stageDonor`, `ThreatCoalition.allyAid`, `ThreatAidRequests.tick`, `ThreatFactionStock.aidTarget`; a Waystation still gates sailing and staging FROM a colony, "Links are bases from founding" below); the Aid button sends hulls out of the player's built navy from their hull port (`ThreatAid.quoteHulls`, `ThreatHulls.sendableUnits`); a unit of hulls was valued at `unitFP` x the game's hull table (~9,600 credits an FP) for a day - superseded below. -> `player-aid.md` 4 "Aid X with N ship hulls"
- **Hulls are priced as the ships good, not as warships (user, 2026-10-11, "thats too much").** Nova Maxios's 4-unit contract paid 3,674,000 credits at the hull table. Now a unit of hulls is vanilla's econ unit of the ships good - 200 items (`ThreatHulls.itemsPerUnit`) at the receiving market's price, like every other aid good (`ThreatAid.valueAt`): about 20,000 credits a unit, 80,000 for that contract; `threatinc_hullCreditsPerFP` > 0 overrides with flat credits an FP; an open hull contract follows the current price both ways (`ThreatAidMissionIntel.advanceImpl`). **Why 400 FP could sail from a 153 FP colony:** the colonies table's FP cell is that colony's standing patrols (`ThreatHulls.standingFP(market)`, Haven 153); the Total row is the faction's free / standing pool, which adds the navy the player's yards have built since mobilising (`ThreatHulls.built`, 1,239 FP there) - the hull convoy draws on that built navy (`sendableUnits`, 12 units), not on any colony's patrols. The Aid prompt now quotes the built navy (FP and units), and the column is **Fleet** (the user: "no abbreviations here"), the built navy on the hull port's row (`ThreatFactionView.fleetShare`, Arkoman 1,239) so the rows sum to the Total. Every other board, tooltip, prompt, notice and intel string that said "FP" now says "fleet points" ("Swarm FP" columns and labels are "Swarm fleet"); log lines keep FP. -> `player-aid.md` 4, `hull-pool.md`
- **The player sends from the same pool as the NPCs (user, 2026-10-11, "undo and keep player inline with npcs").** Built-only for the player (`patrolsSail`, aed3f41d) lived an hour and is reverted: for every faction the pool is a cap on what can be at sea at once - patrol strength plus the built navy, less fleets out (committed at spawn, released when they settle home) and losses (a debt only Heavy Industry / Orbital Works output repays, before it grows the navy). Hull aid still sends the built navy only (`sendableUnits`). The home patrols are never thinned; that is a defensive freebie, not an offensive one, and the war runs were calibrated on it. -> `hull-pool.md`
- **Yards build a navy, no caps (user, 2026-10-06).** "it a wartime economy why would they stop producing ... i never wanted artifical caps nywhere" - a mobilised faction's shipyard output beyond its losses adds to its standing hulls (`ThreatHulls.built`), the player's too; the strategy layer is to choose between kinds of strike and fleet composition, never to cap force. -> `hull-pool.md` 2

- **The humans play to win; no artificial constraints (user, 2026-10-04, about 15:30).** "The question isn't should we allow the humans to attack more, the question should be what is stopping the humans from attacking more? The humans should play to win just like the threat should. There should be no artificial constraints. If there is a shortage that should be identified and questioned." A rule that stops a faction with the means from attacking is a fault to remove, not a knob to tune; a shortage is traced to what spends the stock before it is accepted. -> `game-runs-humans.md` 2, memory `humans-play-to-win`
- **The humans are left alone for now; the swarm's artificial constraints go next (user, 2026-10-04, about 18:00).** After hw6a-hw6c: "This is a great milestone for humans. We should commit and push and leave them alone for a bit. Now yeah agree we need to remove artificial constraints on the swarm like we just did for humans." The human council is not tuned further. The swarm gets the same question the humans got: what stops it defending and attacking with the means it has; a rule that does is a fault to remove, a shortage is traced. Seven rules removed the same evening, each with a knob that puts it back (68d019a; game-tested the same evening in hw7a-hw7c: all seven work, and the swarm is still exterminated in 3 of 3, m65-90 - what is left is its means, 2.6-4.5 to 1 against, and where it puts them); massing a system's defence, the hive's cost on the ground, a recall of strikes when home is attacked and the Hive Mind design stay the user's call. -> `game-runs-humans.md` 3, `game-runs.md` 4 and 5, memory `swarm-plays-to-win`
- **The council's restraints go: four changes (user, 2026-10-04, about 17:00).** "Ok proceed with recommended." (1) An invasion starts on any hive the marines cover, under every strategy, Hold included, easiest first (`ThreatPlays.invade`). (2) The siege is paid before the escort, and the escort is sized to the swarm reported at the target (`ThreatPlays.strike`, `escort`). (3) Bombing takes the worlds it can pay, sails with an escort, and books only those worlds (`ThreatPlays.saturate`). (4) Sieges are one a world, so several run in one system, and the one-play-per-3,000-FP cap is gone (`ThreatPlays.besiege`). Relief of a faction's own worlds still comes first (2026-09-27). Held back: dropping the strategy layer, unless factions still sit idle. -> `war-council-invasions.md`, `game-runs-humans.md` 3
- **The game is the test; the simulator is frozen (user, 2026-10-04 afternoon).** "I did not realise it was recreating game logic with separate simulator only code. Complete waste of time." `tools/warsim` stays at round 33 and is not calibrated to the game again; no simulator-only copy of mod logic is written. A fault is diagnosed from the game's log and dumps, fixed in the mod, and tested by game runs, two or three a setting (one setting has given 26 and 42 worlds lost). A mismatch between the simulator and the game is a simulator fault and never holds a game run. Supersedes the same morning's "simulator first, test-driven" and "no UAT while the simulated war shows problems". -> `war-sim.md`, memory `game-is-the-test`

- **How a run is scored** (2026-10-02) Humans: forward bases set up and held, at its simplest the number held at the end of the run. Threat: colonies founded plus size levels gained during the run, weighted up the scale (7 to 8 counts more than 2 to 3), so a run from an established save scores too. Both: worlds destroyed (hives by humans; human colonies and forward bases by the Threat), so neither side scores by sitting passive. The weights are to be played with; start on spread alone and watch what emerges. Offline simulator approved the same day, with the shared rules package, two more planner runs and monthly dumps. -> `war-sim.md` 7, memory `run-scoring`
- **What makes a run good? (user, 2026-10-02)** Dynamic and emergent, not deterministic: variation between runs is fine as long as the average is good. Judge a run by its whole outcome, never by its deviation from an expected run. Best: both sides winning and losing ground back and forth. Good: one side annihilating the other. Bad: neither side attacking. Worst: a stalemate, two sides failing to change the situation for years. So a balance change is scored over many seeds on how much the map changes hands and how long the longest dead stretch is, beside the two sides' scores. -> `war-sim.md` 7
- **Lead measure: destruction, not ownership switches (user, 2026-10-02).** Judge liveliness by successful colony destruction on both sides - hives killed by the humans, worlds and forward bases destroyed by the swarm, per year - and by `mutual`, the smaller of the two rates (zero when either side sits passive). Reversals and contested systems are secondary. The real runs already read this way: planner wars destroy on both sides (pd9a 24 hives against 8 worlds + 14 bases, pd10a 45 against 19 + 58), council wars only on the swarm's (0-2 hives). -> `war-sim.md` 7
- **The data decides balance (user, 2026-10-02).** "the decision isnt mine. the run results drive the decision." Balance rules and knob values are chosen by the simulator: rank the trial cells by the side's OWN score from "How a run is scored" (humans: forward bases held + hives killed; Threat: foundings + weighted size-ups + worlds and bases destroyed), subject to the other side's score not falling below the base's p10 and the quiet/stalemate shares not rising - never by `mutual`, which would train the factions not to defend (the user, later the same day); `mutual`, turnover and dead years are diagnostics of the war's health; build the winner into the mod, confirm with a 5-minute mid-war game check, record in facts.md. The user is asked only about what the game is (new mechanics, removed systems), never about numbers or rule variants. -> `war-sim.md` 7
- **The Threat may win the NPC-only war (user, 2026-10-02).** "It's ok for threat to win overall given these tests dont include player participation." The simulator has no player, who is the humans' missing weight: a cell is never rejected, and nothing is tuned toward parity, because the swarm ends the run ahead. The ranking rule's guards stay as written (the other side above the base's p10, quiet/stalemate shares not rising) - they keep the war alive, not even. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16d
- **The swarm's home worlds are a setting, not luck** (2026-10-02, after run tr1a's three-planet home) "enforce min 4 tbh it shouldn't be random maybe should be config as the more they start with harder game is. 3 can be allowed also as non default for easier playthrough." Built as `threatinc_homeWorlds` (default 4, 3-8): the home system has at least that many colonisable planets and the opening lands on exactly that many; the rest of the system is taken later, paid for (`tryExpandInSystem`). -> `ThreatIncConfig.homeWorlds`, `ThreatColonyManager.pickChainPlanets`, `IncursionManager.pickOGSystem`, `war-sim-rounds.md` 18
- **`strikeMinSize` stays 4** (2026-10-02) Asked whether to build round 20's 6 (clear for the Threat, the war a year later): "Let's see how it goes without strike min size assuming that makes it easier for humans." Read as: not built, the knob stays 4. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16d
- **No radar; presence is knowledge (user, 2026-10-02).** Radar goes entirely, both sides (`radarRangeLY`, `swarmRadarRangeLY`, `ThreatIntel.inRadar`, `ThreatSwarmIntel.inRadar`). A faction's picture of a system is real time while any of its ships is in the system, and stands and ages from the day the last one leaves; the same for the swarm. Sizing against a report is planning; reading the live garrison is the hack. Amended 2026-10-04 for attack forces in flight: the hive picket, next entry. -> `attack-planner.md`, `threat-fog.md`
- **The hives keep a picket (user, 2026-10-04).** "Yeah give same picket don't worry about changing in flight behaviour otherwise." A human attack force in hyperspace within `swarmPicketLY` (4, the humans' `strikeDetectLY`) of a live hive is seen, exact; this amends "No radar" for forces in flight only - no base, world or convoy is seen from hyperspace, and nothing in flight changes (routes do not meet, blinkers stay). Every hive pickets, not Bastion worlds only. Built 2026-10-04, game-tested the same night (hw8a-hw8c): it first sees 60-64% of sieges, about five days out (`game-runs.md` 6). Not decided: the 10-day contact memory. -> `ThreatSwarmIntel.sees(where, hyper)`, `inPicket`, `threat-fog.md` 4 "The hive picket"
- **Strikes come home when convenient (user, 2026-10-04).** "Ok can they recall if there are nearby fleets but i don't think they should recall a strike that is already mid siege or close to it's target. So yeah should be about to deviate those that are convenient." A hive system attacked and short of its need calls in the strikes within `postureRecallLY` (10) that are still preparing or travelling out and nearer it than their target, nearest first, whichever hive launched them; each comes whole and its fleets join the garrisons of the worlds shortest. A strike in its payload, with a guard left over a landing, nearer its target, or returning flies on. Built 2026-10-04, game-tested the same night (hw8a-hw8c): works, and it recalls nearly every strike launched after contact a median 3-4 days later (`game-runs.md` 6; the fact "Why is the swarm no longer exterminated in hw8"). My readings, not the user's words: "nearby" = 10 ly, "close to its target" = nearer it than the hive, a gap under one swarm calls nothing. -> `ThreatPosture.recallStrikes`, `ThreatStrikeFGI.recallable` / `recallTo`, `swarm-defence.md` "Strikes come home"
- **Mass the defence (user, 2026-10-04).** "If you recommend that then do it and run another test", to my recommendation after hw8: the posture pass sends a system's spare swarms straight to its short worlds, and a launch musters only what the system holds above its need. Built 2f0b270: `massWithin` (a system's own spare, the launch's measure, to its worlds short of their need), then the strike recall, then `massFromNeighbours` (the spare of hive systems within `postureRecallLY` not calling themselves), and the launch hold `strikeCapFP` (no strike from a system still short or past one in range; an attacked system launches only above its need). Game-tested hw9a-hw9c the same night: the swarm is exterminated in 3 of 3 (m91, m105, m124), so the knob `postureMass` is OFF (false = hw8's launch-then-recall) until the user rules on the mend (`game-runs.md` 7). My calls, not the user's words: neighbours give, each world's reserve count stays home, the call is read per world (a sibling's reserve is not over the world attacked), a recall stays whole. -> `swarm-defence.md` "The defence is massed", `ThreatColonyManager.spareFleets`
- **A sighting tells the system, not the world, for both sides (user, 2026-10-05).** "Also humans shouldn't know the exact world just the system. When any human faction gets attacked they all mobilise anyway it doesn't matter who specifically is attacked", and of the swarm: "I find it strange that knowing the system isn't enough ... Why don't they just defend the jump point then." So the mend of the massing is NOT a sighting that carries the target world (my `game-runs.md` 7 (a), rejected): the system is to defend as one force on a system-level sighting. BUILT 2026-10-05 on both sides (`eb7a1f1`), game-tested hw10a-hw10c: the humans' side changed nothing measurable (`game-runs.md` 8). Humans: `ThreatFrontlines.boundFor` reads a seen strike as bound for every market of its system (guards, help requests, the faction view) and `IncursionManager.struckWorlds` mobilises every faction holding a world there (knob `strikeSeenBySystem`; before phase 3 a faction elsewhere still does not mobilise - only from phase 3 do all, `mobiliseAtPhase`). -> `frontlines.md` "A seen strike tells its system"
- **The system defends as one, the middle version (user, 2026-10-05).** Offered a new off-screen battle on entry to the system or "keep the battle at the world, but have the gathered force count in it from the first day, as if it had intercepted, and send it there": "Alright let's go with simplicity and the middle version". BUILT 2026-10-05 (`eb7a1f1`), game-tested hw10a-hw10c (works as built, outcome as hw8: `game-runs.md` 8): a force over a hive world draws the spare swarms of its system's other worlds, sized to the force x `systemDefenceMargin` 1.25 (`ThreatPosture.rally`), and an unspawned siege the swarm saw coming fights the swarms bound for the world from its first day (`ThreatPosture.defenders`, `ThreatPurgeFGI.seenComing`). Knob `systemDefence`; `postureMass` stays off. My calls, not the user's words: sized to the force rather than every spare swarm, the reserve count stays home, hunts and the player's fleet draw the rally too, an unseen siege gets no first-day force. -> `swarm-defence.md` "The system defends as one"
- **Staged strikes; the recall goes; the closest known target first (user, 2026-10-05).** To "let the swarm stage strikes as the humans do: built for the purpose from the whole hive's bank at a staging world, sized to the target, the garrisons stay home" and "should recall come out with it": "Yes and yes. Also they should prioritise the closest known targets including forward bases." BUILT the same day, not yet run: `threatinc_strikeStaged` true (false = the old muster of a system's spare swarms, and the recall), `strikeStagedMargin` 1.5 x the defence last seen at the world, at least two swarms (`IncursionManager.stagedSizes`); the bill is pooled from every hive colony holding its garrison (`ThreatColonyManager.poolHiveBanks`, no haul charged); one strike a pass, the closest payable target of the whole hive, a front of its own short of troops first (`tryStagedStrike`, `pickStagedTarget` - no weighting by worth or stance); `ThreatPosture.recallStrikes` returns at once. Why: a human siege is the faction's pooled means on one target, a Threat strike was one system's leftovers (600 FP against a forward base's 3,000) and its own garrison, so it was recalled. Supersedes the recall and partial-recall entries while on.
- **Information travels by ship; nothing is known instantly (user, 2026-10-05).** "Let's make communication require being in the same system... No more instant knowledge transfer of any form. Once mobilised both sides have patrols which can also serve as scouts that essentially traverse the perimeter of their owned systems and expand outwards assuming no imminent threat that requires consolidation. The budget and number of patrols should be dynamic and dependent on the state of the war. E.g. discovery phase many low fp patrols. But if patrols keep getting lost less with more fp... Basically information is power and should not be instant." A scout's find counts only once it is carried into a system of its own side (not within 10 ly); the distance picket (`swarmPicketLY`) and the humans' `strikeDetectLY` are instant knowledge and go, replaced by patrols on both sides that see where they are, can be destroyed, and are sized by losses. Sieges and hunts need not reveal (they sail only to known hives). Scope, answered the same day: a find carried into a friendly system is shared with every faction at once ("comm relays work when inside a friendly system... literally how market info is shared"); the player's finds are shared instantly; a faction knows at once when one of its own worlds is attacked. A patrol that sees an incoming force (siege, bombers) meets it physically: by speed and strength it is destroyed, escapes to report, or catches and destroys the force ("bombers full of fuel ships and really slow... threat patrol easily catches and eliminates"). BUILT the same day (`strikeLeads`, `carriedIntel`, `patrolsEnabled`; `ThreatScoutRoute.carry` / `meetAbstract`, `ThreatScouts.launchPatrols`, `ThreatSwarmPatrols`), not yet run in a game. -> `information-by-ship.md` Supersedes "The hives keep a picket" when built.
- **A strike gives no lead (user, 2026-10-05).** "Strikes give no lead or indication of anything, it just causes mobilisation. The only way humans can find threat is by sending out fleets or scouts that enter a system with threat worlds in it. So they must manually search"; a find is still shared with everyone. Before, the struck faction's lead party sailed to the origin and its neighbours: the home cluster was found 0-5 months after the first strike in six runs of six (hw15, hw17), sweeps (10 ly of a military world, every 60 days) finding nothing by then. `ThreatScouts.addLead` gated on `threatinc_strikeLeads` false; a colony beside a hive and an army on it still reveal (`revealNeighbours`). Untested; to watch: whether sweeps at 10 ly find anything at all. -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Finding the hive"
- **A recalled strike sends home only what the system is short (user, 2026-10-05).** "Yeah do that", to recalling the fleets that cover the shortfall and letting the rest sail on, after hw15 (90-94% of strikes recalled whole, 3,353 FP for 218 FP short; 0-1 human worlds lost) and hw16 (recall off: the swarm dies). `ThreatStrikeFGI.recallTo(shortBy, needFP)`, `recallUnspawned`, `buildFirst`; `ThreatPosture.recallStrikes`; knob `threatinc_postureRecallPartial` true, false = whole. Game-tested hw17: works as built, changes little (the shortfall is at least the strike in 29 of 38 recalls); all three extinct m92-m109 at 81-100% found, so not a test against hw15. -> `game-runs-2.md` 15
- **The Nexus is no target of tactical bombardment and gives no defence bonus (user, 2026-10-05).** "Take nexus off target list and remove defence bonus it gives", after the trace that a siege's tactical bombardment puts the Nexus down within a month and the hive's FP income with it, at vanilla's price for a military target (0.6-8k fuel a world). Saturation covers the whole planet and still reaches it; the user set aside pricing tactical fuel by size ("tac bombs should be scaled to the defence numbers", which 0.04 x FP a day already matches: defence / 365 a day down). `ThreatGroundFronts` HIVE `fortifications` (`nexusBombardable`, false), `nexusDefenseBonus` 0.5 -> 0 (LunaLib store migrated, version 14): a hive's ground defence is a third lower. Game-tested hw14a-hw14c: no run extinct (4, 29 and 52 hives at m120; found at m48 67% / 72% / 25%, lower than hw11-hw13's 78-100%, so not separated from the found share), income since found 268-705k FP against 27-318k. -> `game-runs-2.md` 12 **Build `d53ecf9` put the gate in the wrong list** (HIVE `keyStructures`, what a holding front suppresses), so hw14 ran with tactical bombardment still on the Nexus; corrected the same day to `fortifications` (`c16e063`) and game-tested hw15a-hw15c: the Nexus stays up until an army lands, no run extinct, two of three run away (19, 100, 120 hives at m120; income since found 540k-1,050k FP; human worlds lost 0-1). -> `game-runs-2.md` 13
- **A system rallies only when it is enough (user, 2026-10-05, after hw11).** "Like humans turn back if world is too well guarded, if threat fleets that can be gathered aren't strong enough to defend then they shouldn't bother." `ThreatPosture.rally` sends nothing unless the swarms at the world, those bound for it and the system's spare together outweigh the force (`systemDefenceOnlyIfEnough`, on; log `Posture: no rally to X`). Built, untested. The ordinary transfers (`Posture: X sent`) are not under it. Since 2026-10-08 (hw83a: Nomios under a 4,450 FP siege sent 91 swarms of 40-90 FP in three months, each hunted on arrival; 4,501 fabricated for attacked hives a game) the pressure pass is: an attacked receiver is sent nothing, transfer or fabrication, unless what stands for it (`ThreatPosture.standsFor`) plus what the pass would send it this poll (its accept - deficit, band and a swarm - or less if donors' spare `pressureSpare` and idle banks `fabricableFor` hold less) would outweigh the force over it (`forceOver`); log `Posture: no transfer to X - N FP over it, M FP could stand (...)`. hw87/hw88 gated on the banks alone (60k "could" stand) refused 49 times, fabricated 4,489 - what the pass would send counts, hw89/hw90.
- **What does the swarm do when it is losing (2026-10-10)?** Consolidates: at `ThreatStance.losingPressure` >= `consolidateLosing` (0.5) `ThreatPosture.consolidate` picks keystones - held FP / the stand, the `consolidateStandPercentile` (0.75) of the forces seen over hives in `consolidateWindowDays` (120) at the defence margin (hw140c, 14:30: sized to the largest force, one 4.4k flotilla, it conceded 23 of 24 hives and fell; keystones are sticky and their bank never scraps the stand), ranked by the share of an input's cover they carry (`mineableNeeds`) - and every other hive is conceded: a keystone wants and needs its stand (held from rallies, launches and the fit), a conceded hive wants its patrols alone, and the pressure pass moves the swarms (keystones pass the flow gate, conceded hives are never the fit's victim). Why: hw138b lost 33.8k -> 7.9k FP in five months at 27-29 hives, each garrison too small to stand and the region never able to outweigh one siege (0 rallies against 62 siege fights). Log `Posture: consolidating - ...` / `consolidation ends`. -> `swarm-defence.md` "Consolidation"
- **Does a besieged hive get help from other systems (2026-10-09)?** Yes, since hw121: regional relief. When its own system's spare cannot outweigh the force, `ThreatPosture.rally` pools the spare swarms of hives in other systems (`regionalPool`: nearest first, fuel-paid passage, each colony within its own need, front colonies never below want, a system fighting for its own worlds gives none) plus those already on the way (`boundFromAfar`), and sends them only if the whole outweighs the force. Knob `regionalRelief`. hw121 (three old-sector games): hives 102 / 97 / 152 at the end against 44-151 before, eradicated 18-26 against 10-48 - the old sector's floor rose from 44 to 97; read with `tools/test-harness/reads/regread.pl`. hw123a (fourth old-sector game): 78 hives / 13 colonies - floor 78; hw125a 111 / 24 (+6). With the belt guard (every reinforcement arriving, hw131a): 251 hives / 2 colonies, 3 hives eradicated - the earlier relief samples were taken with 6-18% of reinforcements frozen. -> `swarm-defence.md` "Regional relief"
- **Why do human hunts beat hives whose garrison outweighs them (hw89a, 2026-10-09)?** They met one swarm at a time: garrison swarms orbit 400-700 units out, up to 1,400 apart, outside the engine's 500-unit battle-join range (settings `battleJoinRange`), and a swarm chasing a hunter is caught alone coming back under the 700-unit leash - 487 hunt battles, 89% against a single swarm (171 FP vs 478, 839 FP of garrison reported standing by), 303 of them 600+ units out. Since 2026-10-09 `ThreatGarrisonMuster.advance` (per frame) joins the hive's other swarms on station and the reinforcements bound for it to a garrison swarm's battle within 1,500 units of the world (`BattleAPI.join`); player battles keep the engine's range. -> `swarm-defence.md` "The garrison fights as one"; hw91/hw92. Read (hw91/hw92, 02:25): single-swarm hunt battles 27% (was 89%), 559 musters, hunts completed 155 / stood down 144 (was 257 / 67); old sector 66 hives / 21 worlds, 39 hives eradicated by sieges that outweighed their garrison; new sector 175 / 115 hives vs 7 / 3 worlds. -> `swarm-defence.md` "The system defends as one", `game-runs-2.md` 9
- **Why does the pass fabricate dozens of tiny swarms for one attacked hive (hw93a, 2026-10-09)?** It built the receiver's cheapest garrison row - a size-1 hive's LOW swarm, 52-97 FP - one a dispatch: Culann, wanting 4,582 FP under a human siege, was fabricated 57 of them in one poll, 151 reinforcements were in flight at a time (20k FP, 15.5k supplies a month) and the old sector's swarm ran its stock to 0 with 128k FP in the fund. Now `ThreatColonyManager.rowFor` picks the dearest row of any size's table that the receiver's deficit, the fabricator's idle bank and the fuel pay for (the cheapest only when nothing more fits). -> `swarm-defence.md` "Only when it is enough"; hw97/hw98. Read (hw97/hw98, 04:50): fabricated 2,792 at a mean of 195 FP, 27% under 100 (was 4,350 / 110 / 55%), 111 in flight (181); old sector 42 hives / 43 worlds with 9 ground victories (20-39 before) - one sample, the fund ran 25-39k; new sector 145 / 168 hives vs 6 / 1 worlds.
- **Why does a swarm siege stand for years without taking the world (hw93a Eldfell, 2026-10-09)?** Its Defend fleets fed the front to the hold line only (`frontHoldFraction` 0.17 of the defender), and the world's counter-attack (its garrison plus a share of its marines) outweighs that line: battered every cadence (odds under 2, never overrun), fed +240 troops back to the line, braced again - 77 months, 45 counter-attacks, 82 First Strike fleets over it at 11k supplies a month, 31 fronts open at the end. Now `ThreatGroundFronts.counterGap` feeds a swarm front to where it repels the counter-attack out of cover (with `fabricateHoldMargin`), and `defendFabricates` keeps feeding until it does. Bends the 2026-09-08 rule "only while the front cannot hold" - the user's call. -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Fabricating troops from the fleet"; hw99/hw100. Read (hw99/hw100, 05:35): old sector 80 hives / 29 worlds (hw97a 42 / 43), 26 ground victories (9), hull break-ups 229 (68); new 167 / 216 hives vs 1 / 3. Still idling: a front holding the hold line but short of the counter line got no bombardment (`defendBombards` asked hold-or-overrun) and no feed while orbit paid - Nortia 150 vs 263 braced 41 times; 4084b5d1 bombards for it first (hw101/hw102). Read (hw101/hw102, 06:20): old sector 36 hives / 22 worlds (71 at m40; 50 eradicated with the fund at 189k FP and 139k supplies in stock - the rally refuses what cannot win), new sector 214 / 76 hives vs 3 / 22 worlds (hw102b the new sector's first contested game: 106 First Strike fleets on Defend, counter-attacks battered 107). Second sample (hw103/hw104, 07:15): old sector 77 hives / 27 colonies (53 eradicated, 281 reinforcements of 80k FP in flight at the end), new sector 169 / 154 hives vs 3 / 5. With overdue reinforcements ordered on (hw105/hw106, 07:58): old sector 92 hives / 15 colonies, the night's best (137 reinforcements of 21.6k FP in flight at the end, 82 of them 90+ days out), new sector 244 / 192 hives vs 5 / 8. Same rules with the read (hw107/hw108, 08:40): old sector 65 hives / 36 colonies (44 eradicated - the swing), new sector 170 / 162 vs 1 / 4. hw111/hw112 (09:30): old sector 89 hives / 21 colonies, new sector 154 / 203 hives vs 0 / 0 - the humans wiped out. hw113/hw114 (10:15): old sector 49 hives / 33 colonies (the swing back), new sector 164 / 146 vs 5 / 1. hw115/hw116 (11:00): old sector 44 / 32 (+25 forward bases), new sector 202 / 144 vs 4 / 4. hw117/hw118 (11:50): old sector 151 / 6 on the same rules, new sector 191 / 103 vs 3 / 3 - four old-sector samples on one build span 44 to 151 hives: the opening decides, not the build. hw119/hw120 (12:50): old sector 65 / 30 (+10 forward bases), new sector 203 / 160 vs 2 / 2.
- **Why do hives fall with the fund full (hw101a, 2026-10-09)?** Reinforcements that never arrive: in every run read, 92-177 reinforcement swarms (18-34k FP) sat 120+ days inside one system - most in the big fabricators' systems (Thule, Beta Cormoran; new sector Alpha Laphirial) - while their targets fell. Zipacna (Gull Motley) was sent a 724 FP swarm from Beta Cormoran I on war day 2690; it stood in Beta Cormoran until Zipacna fell on day 3418 to a 520-625 FP siege, the rally finding 196 FP to stand against it while the system read 3,065 FP held, +2,730 inbound (`ownedFleetFP` counts reinforcements bound for a colony as held, so the pass reads no deficit and sends nothing more). Since 2026-10-09 `ThreatColonyManager.overdue` (the arrivals poll) logs one out longer than `reinforcementOverdueDays` (90) where it stands and orders it on again; the dump's REINFORCEMENT records carry `days` and `battle`. -> `swarm-defence.md` "Overdue reinforcements are ordered on"; cause read from hw105/hw106. hw105a read (07:25): a fresh order does not move them (48 swarms ordered on 3+ times); they move at burn 9 with the order active and no battle yet never close - three swarms for Blue stood 990 days in Blue's own system, their distance to Blue a 540-day sinusoid (the planet's orbit seen from a fleet circling one spot). Most arrive (hw105a 1,916 of 2,231 sends). 095cce7a (hw107/hw108) reads everything (heading, speed, fleeing, tactical target, terrain, nearest hostile) and escalates: order afresh, then a fresh fleet AI, then carried to 1,500 units from the target (stopgap; it works - carried swarms arrive within days) -> `swarm-defence.md` "Overdue reinforcements". CAUSE (hw107/hw108, 08:40): vanilla's asteroid belts - `AsteroidBeltTerrainPlugin` knocks a fleet that is not slow-moving off course each time its impact timeout runs out, heavier for a big fleet, and a 16-25 ship swarm at burn 9 never gets out of its hive's belt (214 swarms at the same unit of position 90 days apart; 1,240 of 1,497 full-speed reads in a belt or ring). The plugin skips a fleet holding `$asteroidImpactTimeout`: `ThreatFleetComposer.beltSafe` set it on every mod fleet, both sides, hw111-hw120 (removed 2026-10-09 afternoon). REFUTED (hw111/hw112, 09:30): with the key set, 137 swarms still froze at the same unit a span apart, all at full speed in a belt or ring with the heading unchanged - the engine is not advancing them; the belt is where they were built. The crawlers (261 reads, speed 44) are `slow true` = vanilla's sneak burn (min burn x the sneak multiplier), the mod never calls `goSlowOneFrame`. The frame pulse (hw113-hw120) was a broken instrument: it tested a negative day (`ThreatPosture.today()` is negative on the sector clock), so every read said `pulse never ran` - CORRECTED 2026-10-09 by the decompile: `BaseCampaignEntity.advance` runs an entity's scripts in any location; the 10:15 reading "scripts run only in the player's location" was wrong. The crawlers arrive (39 of 39 read twice had moved 10,000+ units). The carry split (hw115/hw116, 11:00): 68 of 774 overdue swarms had moved 0 units since their send - never left the spot (all in the hive's belt or ring); a zeroed velocity freed 11 of 21, a one-unit nudge 2 of 16, the carry always. hw117/hw118 (11:50): a cleared avoid list and a 500-unit hop free almost none (46 of 650 overdue swarms never moved from their send spot); only the carry does, and carried swarms arrive within days. hw119/hw120 (12:50): the carry at the second read (177 / 154 / 80 carried); every frozen pair holds its velocity vector to the unit and its facing in 101 of 106 - a dead stop at full burn, nothing integrates its position; neither a reversed facing nor a small source body marks them.
- **What moves a campaign fleet off-screen, and can the engine skip one (decompiled 2026-10-09)?** `BaseLocation.advance` moves every entity in its `CampaignEntity` list by its velocity each tick, unconditionally; non-current locations tick once in 60 iterations at 60 x the time (never zero). Nothing in the core stops a listed fleet at full burn - no sleep flag, per-fleet throttle or separate list. Frozen swarms ARE advanced (hw122/hw123: in the engine's list, the pulse running ~450 frames a span), but inside their own advance they stand at one fixed point and read 6.0 s of velocity further on: the movement module's stored location (`SmoothMovementModule.loc`) never integrates; hw124/hw125: they get normal 2 s frames but flip between two points 6.0 s of velocity apart on alternate calls; CAUSE (hw126/hw127, 67 of 67 in asteroid belts or fields, listed in one location): vanilla's go-slow brake - TacticalModule asks every frame in DANGEROUS_UNLESS_GO_SLOW terrain, `CampaignFleet.doGoSlow` brakes at full acceleration for the whole frame, and on a 2 s background frame that reverses the fleet at full speed, then back. Guarded by `ThreatFleetComposer.beltGuard` on every mod fleet (hw130/hw131; hw128's first cut scaled `getVelocity()`, the entity's copy - a fleet's real velocity is `getVelocityFromMovementModule()`, written only by `setVelocity(x, y)`) - confirmed: overdue reads by war day ~3,200 1 / 1 / 0 against 67-111 before; `swarm-defence.md` "The engine, decompiled". The carry frees because 2-3 ticks cover its last 800 units. `ThreatPosture.today()` is negative - never test a day `< 0`. -> `swarm-defence.md` "The engine, decompiled"
- **Council plays sized by the planner (user, 2026-10-02).** The council decides where and when; a play's siege is sized as the planner sizes one - the strongest world in the faction's report x `npcSiegeOrbitMargin` (`IncursionManager.siegeSizesFor`; there is no trust term, trust only sets the planner's prong count) - and a recon play goes first where there is no report. Built 2026-10-02 (`ThreatPlays.strike` / `saturate` / `stage`; `Play.sizeBy` waits 2x passage + 15 days for the report). Replaces share-of-means sizing, which left the council killing 0-2 hives a run (simulator round 11: the humans' kill rate alone sets whether the war is one-sided). -> `war-council.md`

- **No arbitrary ceilings** (2026-09-29) No hard caps on force sizes, fleet counts or FP maxima; limits come from real resources. A cap found is removed without asking. -> memory `no-arbitrary-ceilings`, `design-theory.md` 2a
- **Closed economy** (2026-09-29) Every fleet, either side, is paid from finite production, banked and spent; strength is moved, never conjured. -> memory `no-arbitrary-ceilings`
- **No nerfing a side's strategy** (2026-09-29) Do not nerf the Threat to help humans (pausing swarm transfers was rejected); fix stuck AI by finding the cap, not adding a rule. -> memory `no-arbitrary-ceilings`
- **Razing never desizes** (2026-10-01) Razing a hive disrupts (structures, unrest, growth halt); size falls only through starvation, and a hive dies only to ground troops. -> memory `razing-disrupts-only`
- **Off-screen fights cost both sides** (2026-10-01) Abstract fights also cost the defenders' garrisons, not only the attacker. -> memory `razing-disrupts-only`
- **Hive stockpile outlets** (2026-10-01) Stop the shortage ratchet that overbuilds plants, convert surplus plants to what is short, and buy a hive military tier with idle FP. Paying structures from any stock at price parity was declined. -> memory `razing-disrupts-only`
- **Human reach is billed, with a stance** (2026-10-01) Humans get the same billed reach (no radius) and a per-faction stance; forward bases pay vanilla build cost and time. Structure upkeep was declined "for now". -> memory `razing-disrupts-only`
- **Disruption runs on the per-day engine** (2026-10-01) Saturation, tactical bombing and sieges all disrupt through rate x condition x shield toward wear days. Never a flat 300-day "pay once" path (the hive "wreck" of cd56a30 was rejected); ask before anything that skips it. -> memory `per-day-disruption-engine`
- **Bombing squadron** (2026-10-01) Over a hive the flotilla holds orbit and only a squadron saturates, picked by cheapest fuel + supplies per day down. Tune the cost model, not a fixed squadron size. -> memory `bombing-squadron`
- **No logistics radius** (2026-10-01) Reach is what the donor's fuel pays; convoys are gated on voyage fuel only, supplies size the escort, pools pay a per-unit haul. Never reintroduce a radius, free pooling or a supplies gate. -> memory `no-logistics-radius`
- **A faction mobilises only when attacked; nobody knows of the swarm before the first attack (user, 2026-10-05).** "They only mobilise when they are attacked. There should be no core worlds in reach crap. No one knows anything until the first attack." `threatinc_mobiliseAtPhase` 0 (migration 15 moves a stored 3), the "Core Worlds in Reach" notice removed. Reverses: **Every faction mobilises at phase 3 (user, 2026-10-02).** Answered "yes" to round 27's trial: `IncursionManager.mobiliseAtPhase`, knob `threatinc_mobiliseAtPhase` 3 (0 = only when struck); one "Sector Mobilised" notice; the player's faction still by choice. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 9, 11
