- **A strike waiting on fuel books fuel demand (user, 2026-10-02).** Answered "yes": `pickStrikeTarget` books what the stock is short of the cheapest passage of a world it would take but for fuel, once a SHORT_DAYS a source (`ThreatFuel.heldShort`), knob `threatinc_strikeWaitBooksFuel` true. Until 2026-10-03 it booked the whole passage, which grows with the garrison: hw4d's planner overbuilt plants (3.5M fuel in stock at month 125, strikes still waiting). -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 7, 11, 12
- **Every faction guards its siege, on screen or off (user, 2026-10-02).** Only spawned strikes held the orbit over their landing - "not intended". An unspawned strike now builds its first fleet over the landing on Defend (`ThreatStrikeFGI.guardUnspawned`; since 2026-10-04 its share of all its fleets, `strikeGuardWhole`, `guardShare`); NPC sieges already left `coverFP`. The simulator's `warsim_strikeDefends` is true by default to match. -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "The swarm guards its unspawned landings too"
- **The humans' p10 guard stays (user, 2026-10-02 late).** Asked whether the ranking may drop "the other side above the base's p10" for swarm levers: "keep it for now". -> `war-sim.md` 7
- **5 troops a broken-up FP (user, 2026-10-03).** "i quite like the feature in general so maybe we switch it to 5": `threatinc_fabricateTroopsPerFP` 10 -> 5, guards and beachheads, LunaLib store migrated (`LunaConfigBridge` version 13). Game test owed: hw4n at 5 stalled on fuel and tested nothing; the simulator puts 5 near 10 (a guard spends twice the hulls for the same troops). Also asked: look at the fuel stall. -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 16-17
- **Relief holds the orbit until the invaders are beaten, and bombards them while its side is losing (user, 2026-10-03).** "Most important thing is they hold space so threat can't come back and start bombing or fabricating again"; bombard "but not to the extent where they would run out of fuel and have to leave". Only the swarm breaks up hulls; humans bombard. `threatinc_reliefStays`, `threatinc_reliefBombardPer30Days` 0.60 -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Relief"
- **Relief sails from any distance (user, 2026-10-03).** "Why wouldn't a far away base be allowed to send relief? Yeah definitely let them": `pickReliefBase` has no radius, the voyage costs fuel and time. Tested in hw4r: median 2.6 ly (0), 15 worlds lost by month 126 (34), until the supplies ran out -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Relief", `war-sim-real-runs.md` 21
- **Supplies bank like fuel (user, 2026-10-04).** "Yeah keep them in line then": `threatinc_reserveWartimeSuppliesShare` (default 1, LunaLib; 0 = the old surplus rule) banks that share of a war faction's peacetime supplies demand on top of its surplus, at 1 its whole availability, as `reserveWartimeFuel` does fuel, against the late-war supplies famine. The import cap (`productionShare`) binds only what a faction buys in: a producer's own output counts whole, so at 1 Hegemony, Persean, the Church and Tri-Tachyon bank 12-21 units against a cap of about 10. Tested overnight 2026-10-04, the value is open (below). -> `economy-coherence.md` "Wartime supplies", `war-sim-real-runs.md` 22
- **NPC offense levers are the user's call** (2026-10-01) Offer, do not apply: unpaid-upkeep consequence, convoy upkeep exemption, links swapping fuel plant for Heavy Industry, a conserved fuel pool. The user wants multipronged attacks and ~300 FP bomber raids on unguarded systems. -> memory `npc-offense-fuel-bound`
- **Attack planner** (2026-10-01) NPCs plan from what they saw (reports that age, radar from forward bases, no remote exact reads, the player included), send several prongs so one is likely to land, divert or go home when outmatched on arrival, and size per world, not on the system's sum (at 1.5x again since h50a/h51a: Siege sizing). Built 2026-10-01 (session e7a0fee3; build notes and deviations: `attack-planner.md` section 11). User answered section 9 on 2026-10-01 ("1 yes 2 yes 3 no 4 whatever recommended 5 yes"): allies pool reports as made and the player gets Cooperative factions' reports (2a); sieges do not divert in v1, an outmatched siege goes home (3); off-screen sieges resolve a day at a time, built with the planner (4); test on an ng7a clone (5). Fogging the Threat's own remote reads is a separate, later change (1, confirmed "separate change"). -> `attack-planner.md`, memory `attack-planner-design`
- **Hunts strike elsewhere when outmatched** (2026-09-30) "this is how we get emergent strategic gameplay". Built as `ThreatSoftening.divert`. Hunts are sized on the garrison now, not a forecast: "what the threat does is dynamic".
- **Hunts spend to the reserve floor** (2026-09-30) Base and donor stock down to the floor, like sorties.
- **The Threat pays for travel** (2026-09-30) "lets try the threat pays for travel", chosen over shrinking Threat fleets (`ThreatFuel`).
- **Threat colonising costs real materials** (2026-09-30) A seed carries what a human forward base costs (1,500 supplies + 800 fuel), extended 2026-10-01 by the founding kit.
- **NPC fleets use supplies like vanilla** (2026-09-30) Maintenance only, no repair or CR: "its too much complexity, for now".
- **Links are bases from founding** (2026-09-30) "why would a base need patrol hq or heavy industry to be used for staging?" Links with a Waystation stage and launch; core worlds still need a military building. Forward-base garrison upkeep stays (it is the real cost of a front).
- **Links build fuel, swap one way** (2026-09-30) Links build Fuel Production while fuel is the shortage; a fuel plant may become Heavy Industry when supplies are short, rate-limited and never back ("as long as 2 cant flip flop").
- **Hive size cap 8** (2026-09-30) "we need a cap there put it back to max 8" (`colonyMaxSize`). Marines and armaments bank at vanilla's stockpile rate.
- **The war is asymmetric and strategic** (2026-09-30) Parity is false ("the threat gets years to spread"); the war must be won by cutting production, so the Threat cannot "magic fuel production on every colony".
- **Structures cost supplies, both sides** (2026-09-30) No credits ("theres nothing for the player to target"), no metals ("feels like an overcomplication").
- **Size upkeep, and shrink when unpaid** (2026-09-30) Growth costs exponential upkeep and tiers shrink when it is not met, so targeted economy strikes matter. Design from the logs first: "give it a lot of thought before blindly building".
- **Siege sizing** (2026-09-29) Whole-system sizing came from one-world sieges that "would always leave". [replaced 2026-10-01: the attack planner sizes each siege on the strongest world it takes, from reports (`npcSiegeOrbitSystem` false). At 1.0x, h50a and h51a sent home 22-25 sieges a run, most on day 1 facing a median 1.4x their FP (the call-off is at 1.0x its own); the user chose 1.5x per world the same day (`npcSiegeOrbitMargin` 1.5, LunaLib migration 9)]
- **Emergent posture and stance** (2026-09-29) The Threat goes defensive under many attacks and thins out to spread when not, and presses weak known systems: "So adaptive". Built as `ThreatPosture`, `ThreatStance`.
- **Sector fuel economy** (2026-09-30) The user wants a closed fuel economy where Sindria matters as top exporter; "not sure what the solution is yet". A conserved pool is parked as their call.
- **Fresh game is the real test** (2026-09-30) "ultimately we will have to fresh with a brand new game"; the lt save's head start is unfair. Test saves must survive: enough supplies and credits that no accident report stops the clock.
- **Commits** (2026-10-01) Commit when the user says so (they pick "Commit now" when offered); push only when asked - on 2026-09-29 they asked for pushes to test on another machine. Every commit includes `jars/ThreatInc.jar` (two once left it out). Leave the `*.stackdump` files untracked.
- **Standing goal** (2026-09-30) "dynamic gameplay with emergent strategies ... no artificial caps and no infinite sources. Everything should be strategic".
- **No cheats, fog for everyone** (2026-10-01) "shouldnt be any cheats, like heg flotilla magically becomes aware that there are more defense swarms at its target"; "Neither the player nor heg should really know the exact number of defense swarms anywhere". Knowledge comes from eyes, forward-base radar in range, and battles. -> `attack-planner.md`
- **Redundancy over certainty** (2026-10-01) "hit in multiple places one is likely to suceed"; an outmatched fleet should "run or divert to another system". The 1.5x margin may go: "fleets that size are proving impossible to send". [The system's sum went; 1.5x per world stayed: Siege sizing]
- **Strategy, not FP matching** (2026-10-01, after h52a) "There shouldn't be any sizing based on enemy FP. There should be strategic plays": a massive joint strike invasion fleet, or decoy attacks with bombers and scouts, then an invasion or another coordinated play depending on the damage done. No deterministic reactive behaviour ("an RTS game where the AI only ever reacts ... so boring and so easy to manipulate"). Relative strength drives it, read from known worlds, their spread and sizes, never fleet FP. "Where is the grand strategic thinking controller": there is none yet; the attack planner re-plans on news (h50a: 95% of 1,466 plans). Design: `war-council.md` (decisions answered: War council decisions). -> memory `strategic-controller`
- **War council decisions** (2026-10-01) `war-council.md` section 11 answered: all four plays in v1 (hammer with recon in force, starve then invade, feint and strike in the close-range form, bombers of opportunity as a bounded share); the coalition hammer is built last in the same build; the council sets the faction stance (Hold CONSOLIDATE; Starve, Roll back, Decapitate PRESS; EXPAND when no play runs); a booked off-screen siege counts as attack FP from dispatch. Taken as written, not objected to: swarm scale from known worlds, sizes, tiers and spread only, no swarm FP; a hammer gets 60% of the means and its muster disbands below half its share; the section 7 personalities, as data; temperature 1 with learning; the Threat's AI unchanged. -> memory `strategic-controller`
- **NPC pressure on big hives must exist** (2026-10-01) If saturation is unaffordable so is tactical bombing, "so only sieging disrupts anything via troops, whichs ounds like a problem".
- **Short test runs** (2026-09-30) About 5 minutes of fast-forward (12-15 game months at 64x): probe one chunk, then about 3 of 110 s. Longer only when a slow trend is the question. -> memory `short-test-runs`
- **Early-war victories** (2026-09-25) Humans "should at least have some victories at the beginning"; the Threat winning comfortably every run is the problem. -> `testing-handover-2026-09-24.md` "Run 7"
- **Board text: less is more** (2026-09-05) The board shows what is true now, never how it works; see CLAUDE.md "UI text and layout". Orders are floating buttons (2026-09-06); notifications go through `ThreatNotice` (2026-09-26); never fluoro green. -> `CLAUDE.md`
- **Board fog of war** (2026-09-25) An unfound hive system is not listed and counts toward no totals; with nothing found the board is off the intel list. -> `war-board.md` "Data model"
- **Rare inputs count in vitality** (Sept 2026) Rare ore and rare metals count in the growth-input average whenever a rare economy runs: "a shortage is a shortage". -> `war-board.md` "Vitality and needs"
- **Raid disruption durations are vanilla's** (Sept 2026) A 90-day floor was added and reverted the same day. -> `war-board.md` "Missions and sieges"
- **Asymmetric, no cheating either side; the swarm attacks** (2026-10-01) "there should be no cheating by either faction we want asymmetric strategy the threat aren't some all knowing all seeing deity their goal should be to spread and purge so when you get to their strategy controller it should be far more offence oriented". The swarm's own strategy controller (not built yet) leads with offence: spread and purge, not defence. -> memory `threat-fog`
- **Big changes get a long new-game test** (2026-10-01) "testing from established save from old code isn't fair test": after the swarm's fog, a long run from a new game (overnight), not a clone of an old save. Short runs stay the default for small changes. -> memory `short-test-runs`
- **The swarm gets fog too** (2026-10-01, after h53d) Asked "why does threat not have fog of war?", then "commit first then do threat fog". Built without a question round, taking the mirror of the human side by default: radar on Bastion and Command worlds only at the humans' 10 ly; a sighted attack counts at its real target; no arrival break-off for blind strikes; a contact ends when its force ends. Each is open to the user. The review (same night) added one more default: with Swarm Scouting off, each system holding a strikeable world is seeded once per save, so a world founded later there waits for eyes or radar. -> `threat-fog.md` sections 2 and 4, memory `threat-fog`
- **Starve fits outmatched** (2026-10-01, after h53b) Offered four answers to "outmatched factions held for a year", the user chose: Starve fits outmatched, scoring about as high as Hold (2.5, x1.3 with a rich cluster in reach). Not chosen: counting every mobilised faction in the ratio, lowering the even threshold, or keeping Hold. `ThreatWarCouncil.scores`. -> memory `strategic-controller`

## Siege sizing and targeting (NPC)

- **How big must an NPC siege be, and why are there 10-20k FP fleets?** Defense Swarm FP faced x `npcSiegeOrbitMargin` (1.5), no fleet cap; fleets are at most difficulty 10 (250 FP). FP faced = max(strongest target world, the SUM of every garrison in the system while `npcSiegeOrbitSystem` is on, swarms met by its last called-off siege within `siegeMetMemoryDays` 90). Player sieges need 0. `IncursionManager.siegeOrbitNeeded` / `siegeOrbitWeighed` / `siegeFleetSizes` -> `ground-war-runs.md` "Overnight 2026-09-29"
- **Are there really 20k FP fleets?** No fleet that size sails: 18-26k is what an NPC believes a core siege needs. Fleets grow in whole fleets of 6-10 points x 25 FP (mean 1.84x the need), and the median NPC fuel pool is 28k against 0.4 fuel per FP per ly. h48a: all 7 launches hit 1-2-hive systems needing under 4.3k. `IncursionManager.siegeFleetGoal`
- **Why are big multi-hive core systems never sieged?** The whole-system sum x 1.5 dwarfs any one world (h48a: cores 12-17k FP). The siege waits and posts a swarm bounty instead. Since 2026-10-01 sieges size per world at 1.5x from the faction's report (`siegeOrbitWeighed`), and raids bomb the worlds a siege does not take. Under the planner a system no siege of the faction can take still posts the bounty, through the launch's gates or `IncursionManager.orbitBounty` once the plan's chance is met. `IncursionManager.systemSwarms`, `ThreatSwarmBountyIntel.post` -> `attack-planner.md` "Why: what h48a showed"
- **Do a system's garrisons converge on a besieged world?** No, so the whole-system sizing rests on a false premise. A world's reserve never leaves it: what moves is FP above 1.25x a colony's want and one swarm, plus, for a system under attack, one fleet at a time from a quiet colony at its want; in a core the want is the reserve (Pelephanar: want 11,032, need 200-466; Bastion/Command x2/x3 the reserve). A core world's own orbit holds 5-21% of its system, 0.9-2.5k. With `postureMass` on (built 2026-10-04, off since hw9) the fleets above each world's reserve COUNT converge on a world short of its need (`ThreatPosture.massWithin`, `swarm-defence.md`); the reserve itself still never leaves. Since 2026-10-05 (`systemDefence`, game-tested hw10) the fleets above each sibling's reserve count are sent to a world a force is over, sized to that force x 1.25, and fight a siege seen coming from its first day (`ThreatPosture.rally`, `defenders`). `ThreatPosture.wantFP`, `ThreatColonyManager.redistributeByPressure` -> `attack-planner.md` "Why: what h48a showed"
- **Where are the openings?** Surplus moves as real fleets, the same system's colonies first, then the nearest, landing 0-15 days after the 5-day pass that sends it. A quiet colony gives a system under attack one fleet at a time, never below its reserve, and refills from its bank between (1-5 days while the bank pays and its Core and Nexus stand); a fresh seed holds one swarm of 63-67 FP. No faction strikes these. -> `attack-planner.md` "Why: what h48a showed"
- **What does it take to raid (bomb) a hive?** The larger of two: the guns need F >= D/29 for a day to buy a day down (42 FP at size 2, 435 at size 7, 498 at size 8), and the orbit must be uncontested, so more than 2x the swarms over that world (130 for a fresh seed, 2-5k for a core world). So 300 FP reaches fresh seeds, stripped and soft hives, never a core. A 300 FP raid 12 ly out and back costs about 2,900 fuel and 360 supplies. -> `attack-planner.md` "4. Raids"
- **How does an off-screen siege resolve?** A siege expedition (`ThreatPurgeFGI`; an NPC's, and the player's, which never calls off) runs a day at a time since 2026-10-01 (knob `abstractSiegeDaily`): `advanceDaily` takes it on arrival; each day over each world it weighs `ThreatGroundFronts.hostileFleetsNear`, goes home outweighed (`callOff`), fights a day (`ThreatAbstractBattle.foughtDay`, both sides lose; since 2026-10-02 its play's hunts at the world fight beside it and share the loss, and it waits up to 3 days for hunts still coming, `ThreatSoftening.playFleetsNear`), bombards a day (`abstractSiegeStep`) while the orbit is its own, then lands through vanilla's passes (`endWorld`); every toll is route damage. The swarm's strike (`ThreatStrikeFGI`, ground doctrine) runs the same way since 2026-10-17 (`ThreatStrikeFGI.advanceDaily`, the purge's copy without the call-off, the hunts or the hive's rally: the swarm fights to vanilla's abort line), under the same knob; its bombardment doctrines keep their one go -> `ground-war-code-paths.md` section 8
- **What does the Threat read about humans remotely?** Since the swarm's fog (`threatinc_swarmFogOfWar`, 2026-10-01), only what its eyes (a hive, Threat fleet, strike route or Threat front in the system), radar (Bastion and Command worlds, `swarmRadarRangeLY`) and Scouting Swarms saw: `ThreatSwarmIntel` contacts (attacks) and places (staging, guards, reach, defence), aged by `intelHalfLifeDays`. Every remote read, by symbol, and what stayed live: `threat-fog.md` section 1. With the fog off, as before: exactly and live: each staging base's fuel and supplies (as siege FP against the hive it stages for, so the swarm reinforces before anything sails), the FP of every NPC attack and order from dispatch, at any distance (a siege from dispatch too since 2026-10-01, the user's decision 10: while its route is abstract it counts by its flotilla, `abstractNow`, until its fleets spawn, `ThreatPosture.countsAbstract`, knob `threatSeesBookedSieges`; before, an off-screen daily siege added no attack FP: Gamma Sonora under a 4,900 FP daily siege read "attacks 0"), forward guards, convoy FP and routes (`ThreatRaiders`), a target's defenders and station. `ThreatPosture.siegeCapacityFP` -> `attack-planner.md` "9. Decisions for the user"
- **What is a siege's fuel bill made of?** About 85% passage (FP/25 x ly x 10) and 15% tactical ordnance (0.04 x FP x days, at most 120), billed for the worst single target, not summed over the system. Razing prices (100k-1M) are separate and chosen only where cheaper than landing. Marines are sized after bombardment (`raidStrNeededAt`). `IncursionManager.expeditionFuel` / `siegeOrdnance`
- **How are NPC siege fleets paid at spawn?** The game's real built FP is compared with the estimate: extra drawn from home then donors, overpay refunded, warships trimmed if the reserve cannot pay (flagship and transports never cut); supplies come back per fleet on arrival home. Fuel and ordnance stay at the estimate. `ThreatPurgeFGI.setLedger` / `settleLedger`
- **What does "Raze or siege of X from Y" in the log mean?** Only the weighing, printed for every base considered; not a launch.
- **When do NPCs pick siege targets?** Since 2026-10-01 the attack planner picks them (`attackPlanner`; the monthly `tryPurgeBombardments` pass returns at once). Each faction plans every `planIntervalDays` 7 days, and on news: a prong resolved, a raid over, a report that moved by half, `huntThinned`. Pressed system first, then the landing, then the reported orbit; bases cheapest first; one new siege per system per plan. Sieges are added until P(at least one lands) >= `planConfidence` 0.8, where each prong's P is its report's trust at arrival. `purgeCooldownDays` 240 per colony and the `siegeAllowed` stance gate still apply. The Threat never reads NPC targets. `ThreatAttackPlanner.plan` -> `attack-planner.md` sections 2 and 11. Before the planner: the slow tick (`tickDays` 30), easiest first, one launch per system per pass, no plan.
- **Which worlds does one siege take?** `siegeTargets`: the whole system if the base can take and pay its orbit; else the most of the easiest it can afford; else the first it can take alone; else the easiest alone (convoys stage toward it). Excludes worlds on cooldown, booked (`bookedWorlds`), held by another faction's army, or whose own front finishes first. `IncursionManager.siegeTargets` -> `strategy-reserves-sieges.md` "Reserves"
- **Do humans attack one target at a time?** No. Since 2026-10-01 a faction's plan spreads sieges over several systems until P(at least one lands) >= 0.8, and raids bomb the worlds no siege books. One siege takes one system's worlds in turn with its whole flotilla; a faction can run several sieges per system on unbooked worlds, and in many systems at once. Limits: one new siege per system per plan, one hunt per faction per system, one Support or Defend per world, and one raid per faction per world, kept apart from Support. `IncursionManager.bookedWorlds`, `ThreatSoftening.hunting`, `ThreatFleetOrders.hasSupport` / `hasRaid` -> `attack-planner.md` section 11, `strategy-reserves-sieges.md` "Reserves"
- **Is a siege's trip reserved at launch?** No. `siegeCanPay` prices deposit + supplies for `siegeTripDays`, then `ThreatUpkeep` pays as it goes, so two sieges admitted in one pass can count the same stock; an over-committed one starves home (`outOfSupplies`). Unspawned expeditions are billed monthly (`chargeAbstract`). -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "The factions' reach and stance"
- **Why does an NPC siege turn home without landing?** No front down and hostile swarms >= `siegeBreakOffRatio` (1.0) x its live FP over any target (unspawned: the same test in vanilla's `WarSimScript` units). It calls off, remembers the swarms for 90 days and posts a bounty. It never diverts (targets fixed at launch); player sieges never break off. `ThreatPurgeFGI.breaksOff` / `breaksOffAbstract` / `callOff` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Bombardment v2"
- **Can a siege expedition be redirected?** No. `ThreatPurgeFGI` targets (`where`/`allowedTargets`) are fixed at launch; it is one route, abstract until the player is within 1.6 ly, and `spawnFleets` deletes the route for good. It ends by return, `callOff`, `outOfSupplies`, `abortPurgesAgainst` or its last fleet detaching. Per-fleet moves exist (`recallFleet`, `detachToHunt` -> `ThreatFleetOrders.adoptHunt`). Retargeting an abstract siege means route and ledger surgery with no vanilla precedent. -> `attack-planner.md` "3. On arrival"
- **Is there an NPC bomb-only fleet that softens targets?** Yes since 2026-10-01: the RAID, a Support order flagged `raid` (`ThreatFleetOrders.dispatchRaid`, sent by `ThreatAttackPlanner`), bombs a hive with no front of its own and leaves on the contest, the commander's stop or a third lost (`raidOver`). Before it, no (the user remembered one). NPC orders are guard, support, defend, hunt; bombing happens only as a siege's squadron, tactical slices while a siege waits to land, or a Support fleet (`dispatchOrbit`, triggered by `ThreatConvoys.supportFor` only with an owned front and a refused orbit) whose `tickSupport` -> `supportSlice` bombs while the orbit is clear; `supportLost` stands it down unless `navyHoldsOver`. Hunts fight swarms and never bomb. -> `attack-planner.md` "4. Raids" and section 11
- **Why have NPCs never saturated a hive?** h48a: the squadron cut planned fuel a median 9x, yet 0 saturations and the runtime path never ran - pools cannot pay even the flotilla's passage, and the verdict is all-or-nothing (priced with `Float.MAX_VALUE` fuel, then gated on spare). `ThreatGroundFronts.squadronPlan`
- **What can redirect a fleet?** NPC: only `ThreatSoftening.divert` (a hunt outmatched at muster or moving on; no `sectorKnows` check), `ThreatFleetOrders.divertRaid` (a raid turned back in its first two days at the world, to its fallback, 2026-10-01) and `ThreatResponseIntel.retarget` (target hive dead -> nearest found hive). Sieges can only abort; Threat strikes are never redirected. Only the player's Support/Defend/Hunt reassign fleets (`nearestReassignable`, `takeOver`, `adoptHunt`). -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Hunting forces"
- **How far does a siege fleet roam?** Hunts only within `siegeHuntRange` 1500 of a contested world, recalled beyond `siegeLeashRange` 6000 or out of the system; `ALLOW_LONG_PURSUIT` is stripped. `ThreatFleetOrders.siegeLeash` -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "NPC fronts"
- **When does a faction mobilise and stand down?** NPC: when a Threat strike with one of its worlds in the sweep is detected (`IncursionManager.onStrikeDetected` -> `ThreatWarState.recordStrike`; every faction in the sweep since 2026-10-02, before that the first target's alone); never by phase (`mobiliseAtPhase` 0 since 2026-10-05, the user: nobody knows of the swarm before the first attack); `warExcludedFactions` (pirates) never. Player: by button. Stands down only if `warModeStandDownDays` > 0, the last strike is that old, and no found hive is in reach (`hiveInReach`). Default 0: a faction never stands down - in sl2 (2026-10-06) Hegemony and the League flew patrols and kept the war footing five years after the last hive died. -> `strategy-reserves-sieges.md` "War mode"
- **How does a faction's stance work?** `ThreatFactionStance.evaluate` every 7 days. CONSOLIDATE: pressed (struck within 60 d or a Threat front on its world) and losing or outweighed; no new links, sieges only at systems facing it. PRESS: not pressed, force >= `stancePressRatio` 1.5 x the swarm facing it, a siege payable; its system weighed first. Else EXPAND. Held `stanceDwellDays` 30 except into CONSOLIDATE. Priority only, never a cap. Until 2026-10-01 a `> 0` guard on the negative clock timestamp read every strike as "never", so pressed meant a Threat front only (123 of 123 logged pressed lines); fixed to `!= 0`. `lastStruckTimestamp` is the mobilisation time until a first strike. -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "The factions' reach and stance"

- **Where is the NPC war code's call graph?** The siege pass and its gates, targets, orbit sizing, the launch, the stance, orders and the Support stand-down, hunts, the coalition call and what is saved, by symbol. -> `strategy-code-paths.md`
- **Which gates does calling `launchSiegeExpedition` directly skip?** The pass's own: `sectorKnows`, `reliefOwed`, `siegeAllowed`, `anySiegeReady`, the `siegeTargets` filters (booked, held by another army, cooldown), `siegeDifficulty` / `siegeSizesFor`, and the launch notice and log. Inside the launch stay the orbit gate and the provisions gate (both post the bounty), marines and armaments; a postponement stamps no cooldown. -> `strategy-code-paths.md` "Injecting a planned siege"
- **Who must agree on `siegeOrbitNeeded`?** Five readers: the launch's orbit gate, `siegeFleetGoal`, `siegeAffordable`, `ThreatFleetOrders.dispatchOrbit`, `ThreatConvoys.supportFor`. -> `strategy-code-paths.md` "Orbit sizing"
- **Why does an NPC Support over a hive without its own front go home at once?** `ThreatFleetOrders.poll`'s `supportLost`: `ThreatGroundFronts.navyHoldsOver` is false wherever the faction owns no front, so it stands down within half a day. A raid order must be exempt. -> `strategy-code-paths.md` "Orders"
- **Is the siege cooldown per world?** Yes (`onPurgeCooldown` over `lastPurgeTimes`; the launch stamps every target), but the pass weighs each system once and launches at most one siege per system per pass. -> `strategy-code-paths.md` "Cooldown"

## War council (NPC strategy)

- **What decides an NPC's attacks while `warCouncil` is on?** `ThreatWarCouncil` picks a strategy and a focus cluster each review; `ThreatPlays.plan` starts plays from them. The attack planner (`ThreatAttackPlanner.active`) and the monthly siege pass (`tryPurgeBombardments`) stand aside. `war-council.md` s16. Since 2026-10-04 the invasion line (`ThreatPlays.invade`, weekly) also invades every hive the pools pay a landing on, whatever the strategy. -> `war-council-invasions.md`
- **What happens when the humans attack without restraint (hw6a-hw6c, 2026-10-04)?** They exterminate the swarm in 3 of 3 runs, 24-34 months after finding it (extinct m66-76): 49-50 landing sieges and 22-28 escorts a run against a swarm of 17-18 hives, 9-16k FP and 2.5-3.5k FP a month; 19-20 landings, 1-2 overrun, 18 hives eradicated, 0-2 human worlds lost. The swarm starts the war with doomed landings (7-12 of 10-17 overrun), leaves outlying hives under 25-121 FP, loses a hive to 300-2,300 marines, and goes CONSOLIDATE (no founding) once pressed. The user's answer: remove the swarm's own restraints (next fact). -> `game-runs-humans.md` 3
- **What stopped the swarm defending and attacking with what it had (hw6, read 2026-10-04)?** Seven rules, all removed in 68d019a (game-tested in hw7: each works as built), each with a knob: a depot's capacity read as an attack (`postureStagedShare` 0); systems written off, the home system and its 12.9k FP included (`postureTriage` off); the launch stock never given to the defence, and the need split by size tables while the orbit is fought at the world - Alpha Laphirial II met 1,925 FP with 1,432 in a system holding 12.9k (`postureNeedAtAttack`: `ThreatPosture.overWorlds`, `siegesOver`, `alarm`, `redistributeByPressure`); pressed forges home (`posturePressedForgesHome` off); CONSOLIDATE stopping claims and strikes (`stanceConsolidateSpreadShare` 1, `stanceConsolidateStrikes`); an unspawned strike leaving one fleet a landing - 291 FP of 4,219 over Chicomoztoc (`strikeGuardWhole`, `ThreatStrikeFGI.guardShare`). Not rules: the 8-10k FP away were two strikes sent before it was found; income fell to 0 because Cores and Nexuses were down. -> `game-runs.md` 4, `swarm-defence.md` "The swarm's restraints removed"
- **Why does the swarm still lose with its rules gone (hw7a-hw7c, 2026-10-04)?** Extinct m90, m67, m65 (hw6: m65-75). Four things, none a rule of the seven: it is outbuilt 2.6-4.5 to 1 (after contact 48-111k FP of sieges and 72-122k FP of escorts against 10-17k FP held and 27-68k built); it trades at 0.7 because every garrison is outnumbered at its own world (1.4 Threat FP lost a human FP in all six runs; a siege's first day is a median 0.9-1.4k FP against 163-356) - the home system's 6,920 FP on eight worlds met five sieges of 0.7-4.6k one world at a time, a sibling gives only down to its reserve (`ThreatPosture.minimumFP`) and a fleet at a time (`ThreatColonyManager.redistributeByPressure`, `pressureFleet`); the home system launched 6,938 of its 12.9k FP at Eochu Bres a week before 10.4k FP of sieges, two months in flight, arrived (the posture reads a siege once it is in the system; nothing recalls a strike); and a hive falls a median 77-91 days after a landing of 851-1,208 marines. Read with `tools/test-harness/digest/swarm.pl` and `hives.pl`. -> `game-runs.md` 5
- **What stops a governed faction invading a hive (since 2026-10-04)?** No report of the system, marines (`IncursionManager.siegeMarinesPooled` against the landing), fuel, supplies or armaments (`siegeCanPay`), relief owed, a siege already booked on the world, another faction's front on it, or its own front about to finish it (`frontFinishesFirst`). No strategy, focus, play cap or muster. -> `war-council-invasions.md` 2
- **How big is a play's escort?** `ThreatPlays.escort`: `councilEscortMargin` 4 x the fleet points reported in the system, at least its sieges' own FP, at most `councilHammerShare` 0.6 x `playPayableFP`; sent after the sieges are paid, held until they near. Before 2026-10-04 `toMuster` sent the 60% share first (median 5.3-7.5k FP against 344-444 reported). -> `war-council-invasions.md` 3
- **How does a play skip the enemy-FP gates?** Through its `playId`. `launchSiegeExpedition`'s 10-argument overload drops the orbit gate, the orbit term and the Coalition Call; `ThreatSoftening.sendPlay` builds a held `Force` (`hold`) paid from `playPayableFP`; `ThreatPurgeFGI.friendsNear` counts the play's hunts against a break-off. With `playId` null every path is unchanged. `war-council.md` s16.
- **Who sets the stance of a governed faction?** The council (`ThreatFactionStance.set`; `refresh` skips governed factions): PRESS at its system whenever a major play runs, Hold included (2026-10-04); with none, Hold is CONSOLIDATE and the others EXPAND (`ThreatWarCouncil.setStance`).
- **How does a play stage?** `ThreatConvoys.stageForPlay` overrides `stagingHive` and `siegeStock`'s wants for that base until `clearPlayStaging` (the siege sails or the play ends). A feint's strike base stages against the decoy.
- **How does a play learn which raids were driven off?** `ThreatPlays.raidEnded`, called from `ThreatFleetOrders.endRaid`, `standDown` and the destroyed path, books each `raidId` once.
- **What ends a held play force?** `ThreatSoftening.release` (its play lets it go), or a stand-down: the play ending (`end` stands down forces that never went in), its play missing, or `warCouncil` off (`advanceForce`).
- **Which base sends a play's squadron?** `ThreatPlays.squadronBase`: the nearest base of the faction that reaches the world and pays the squadron whole (`sortieReachFP`). A raid pays from one base with no donors, and the nearest base (`pickBase`) is often an empty forward base. In h53b a bombing campaign with 35k fuel a month sent nothing for two months.
- **How long does a hammer wait at the muster?** Since 2026-10-04 there is no muster ahead of the strike: the strike is tried each day of the prepare and sails the day one siege is paid; with none paid on its day it waits in MUSTER up to `councilStrikeWaitDays` 30, then fails (`ThreatPlays.strike`). `councilMusterDays` only serves an older save's muster. In h53b a 30-day muster 39 ly out filled 0 of 750 FP. -> `war-council-invasions.md` 3
- **What did the first council runs show (h53a-b, 2026-10-01)?** While few hives were known, both factions read ahead (Hegemony 2.0, Persean 1.7) and drew Decapitate. Hegemony#3's hammer ran its whole cycle: 2.5k FP of hunts, a 5.6k FP siege, the Nexus down 55 world-days, orbit held 38 days, no landing; success. Once the swarm reached 16 hives (weight ~95 against single-faction weights of 66-71), both read outmatched (0.66-0.76) and held for the rest of the run, the Hegemony on 338k fuel. One faction's weight against the whole known swarm puts every faction in Hold once the swarm passes about 16 hives.
- **Where does a bombing campaign's squadron go?** Only to a Nexus that `ThreatPlays.bombable` allows: the faction's reports show no swarm over it, or its flotilla holds the orbit. The least guarded goes first, and the saturation siege sails from `richestBase` (the cluster's base with the largest `siegeCapacityFP`). In h53c, squadrons sent alone into 600-3500 FP of swarms were all driven off, and the core's saturation share from the nearest outpost was about 500 FP, too small to sail.
- **Why did the human council do so little in pd2a (1 hive killed in 8.6 years)?** Hold won 70% of months: pressed (true 85% of the time) and outmatched each added +2 to Hold and pressed halved Starve, defeating "Starve about as high as Hold"; Hold never starts a hammer. Bombers of opportunity failed 48 of 55 on one-day "unguarded" snapshots of colonies still being garrisoned, and never read what they learned. Both fixed 2026-10-01 (`ThreatWarCouncil.scores` counts pressure once; `ThreatPlays.opportunity` weighs `learned` and rests a world that drove a squadron off, `REPULSED_FLAG`). Still open for the user: Starve's orbit is circular, hammer sieges are small beside their hunts. pd4a (with the fixes): HOLD 58%, STARVE 38%, no repeated bombers, but still 1 hive killed; then human supplies ran dry (17k a month to 0) and a siege fought alone beside its own hunts. pd6a (hunts fight beside the siege): unexercised, the hunts were let go after the siege had landed (fixed: `ThreatPlays.siegeNear`, live ETA) or stood down out of supplies. pd8a: hunts beside the siege on 27 of 42 fight days, but 4 sieges, 0 landed. -> `war-council-runs.md` 1-3
- **Does the swarm run away in a new game?** In pd2a (fog on) it went from 5 to 139 hives in about 103 months (doubling every ~18 months to month 90, then +3 a month), took 16 human worlds and kept 95k FP banked and 250k FP of fleets; its stance was never CONSOLIDATE. The fog-off twin pd3a grew bigger (205 hives at month 103) with the same strike rate per hive, so the fog is not the cause; the fogged swarm only stops garrisoning ahead of attacks it has not seen. -> `threat-fog.md` 4, `war-council-runs.md` 1
- **Why is the swarm 5x smaller with the council off (pd7a, 2026-10-02)?** With `warCouncil` off the attack planner sails about 2 sieges a month from month 39 (202 by month 97, 43 landed, 27 hives killed), which keeps `ThreatStance` in CONSOLIDATE (63 of 119 months) and so stops founding: 30 hives at month 97 against 155-201 in the council runs (1-2 hives killed, 15-27 sieges). The council replaces the planner by design (`ThreatAttackPlanner.active` false) and only HAMMER plays land, one major play per faction at a time (`ThreatPlays.major`), none under Hold. Supplies are not the cause. Open for the user. -> `war-council-runs.md` 4
- **Why won't the swarm strike a world it has never seen?** Under the swarm's fog a strike candidate needs a `ThreatSwarmIntel` place (`IncursionManager.strikeSeen`); `targetDefence` is the place's `defenceFP`, `Float.MAX_VALUE` without one. Places come from eyes, Bastion radar, Scouting Swarms (and re-scouts of stale systems), the once-per-save seeding of old saves' charted systems, or, with scouting off, `seedUnscouted`. -> `threat-fog.md` section 4
- **What does a sighted human base tell the swarm?** Its own stock only: `stagedFP` (`ThreatPosture.stagedBy`, no donors), `reachLY` (`IncursionManager.seenSiegeBaseReachLY`), guards and defence, dated and aged by trust. Remote donor depots were leaking into those figures until the review fix; whether the base counts as staging at all still uses the donor-pooled `siegeBasesFor` (an open yes/no leak). -> `threat-fog.md` section 4
- **Why did the swarm stop claiming systems for a year (pd2a months 37-49)?** First the PRESS stance halved `claimCap` under 6-7 held claims, then fuel: the 3.7-5k a far founding needs went on sector reinforcements (22-27 swarms a month) while blockades cut the fuel made. Re-scouts were suspected (478 Scouting Swarms with the fog on, 17 off) and since 2026-10-02 wait above the dearest pending founding (`ThreatSwarmScouts.claimReserve`), but pd5a stalled 11 months anyway: a generic fuel crunch (pd3a, fog off, hit it too). -> `threat-fog.md` 4
- **Why are bombers of opportunity driven off over worlds reported unguarded?** Until the swarm's fog (2026-10-01, `threat-fog.md`) the Threat saw every human fleet from the day it sailed, so it moved 1.4-2.8k FP of Defense Swarms over the world before the squadron arrived (h53c, every BOMBERS play). Now it sees a force only through its eyes, radar or scouts.
- **Why does Starve do no damage (h53d, 2026-10-01)?** Outmatched factions now draw it, but its saturation siege, sized from the means share (6.7k, 2.4k, 1.7k FP), meets 1.6-2.4x its strength on arrival: the Threat sees it sail and its posture counts it as an attack (Hybrasil wanted 16k, held 19k). The abstract break-off weighs every Threat fleet in the system, so it turns home whole ("Siege Called Off", ordnance unburned) or is caught by a passing strike (Persean, 11% back). No orbit is ever held and every Nexus keeps its garrison, so `bombable` never lets a squadron go, and each campaign ends "ran its course" with 0 Nexus-down days: a failure that learning then holds against Starve. Both h53d hammers failed the same way: Hegemony#11 sent 4.7k FP against 320 FP reported and met 5.4k.
- **What happens when a council's play cannot start on its focus?** `plan` clears the focus, so the next day draws another cluster, and logs once per strategy and play type ("Council f: no TYPE on X can start").
- **Where are the council's knobs?** `threatinc_council*` in settings.json; personalities are `threatinc_councilPersonalities`, keyed by faction id, with `default` as the fallback. Luna exposes `warCouncil`, `councilReviewDays`, `councilTemperature`, `councilLearning`, `councilHammerShare` (Escort Ceiling), `councilEscortMargin`, `councilInvadeDays` and `councilSquadronFP`.

## What NPCs know (intel)

- **Is there fog of war or radar on Threat strength?** Yes since 2026-10-01 (`intelFogOfWar`): `ThreatIntel` keeps each mobilised faction's and the player's report of each found system - swarm FP per hive world within 1,500 su, the day and how seen (eyes, radar, scout) - and every human-side reader asks `ThreatIntel.report` / `worldFP` / `trust`, never the garrisons. `ThreatIntel.see` is the one remote read; on-site judgement (contest, break-off, leash) stays live. Off, readers get the live picture. -> `attack-planner.md` sections 1 and 11
- **Which human-side code reads the swarm, and where are the choke points?** Since 2026-10-01 they all read the observer's report (`ThreatIntel.report` / `worldFP` / `worldFleets` / `trust`), and `ThreatIntel.see` is the one remote read. The `ownedFleetFP` leak in `garrisonNowFP` is gone. Still live: the board's Reach "grounded" (hive supply state), its Activity column (raiders, seeding waves) and the convoy rows' "HUNTED" (`attack-planner.md` section 11, "Still read live"). Before the fog, the choke points were `IncursionManager.siegeOrbitFP` (carrying the siege chain, Support, the stance target and hunts), `ThreatSoftening.garrisonNowFP`, the `countLiveGarrison` head-counts (cooldown, difficulty, convoy door, board, contracts, `InfestedSystemIntel`, `ThreatFrontlines.strikeOf`) and `ThreatFactionStance.evaluate`'s "theirs". Never swap `countLiveGarrison` or `garrisonsFor`: the Threat uses them. -> `attack-planner.md` "8a. Every human-side read of the swarm"
- **What does `sectorKnows` gate?** Whether a hive system is found (`ThreatScouts.sectorKnows` over `ThreatIncData.discoveredSystems()`, knob `hiveFogOfWar` on). Gates sieges, the response task force and its retarget, staging, stand-down, contracts, link founding, `hasSiegeableHive`, `siegeBasesFor`, the stance's `weakestTarget`. Does NOT gate `ThreatSoftening` (send, divert), `ThreatCoalition`, `ThreatAid`, or `ThreatPosture`'s attacks, hostiles and losses (its staged term goes through `ThreatConvoys.stagingHive` and its forward term through `ThreatFrontlines.hiveNear`, both gated). The swarm's `ThreatStance.weakTargets` is gated by the swarm's own charts (`ThreatSwarmScouts.swarmKnows`), not this; `weakestTarget` is the human `ThreatFactionStance`'s. The human stance's "theirs" is gated since the planner build (`ThreatIntel.systemFP` over found systems). "Found" is sector-wide, not per faction, and `getAllLiveColonyMarkets` / `totalColonySize` / `countColonies` count unfound hives too: never read them as "known". -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Finding the hive"
- **How do I list the hive systems and worlds a faction knows?** No API: filter `ThreatAttackPlanner.knownSystems()` (found, sector-wide) by `ThreatIntel.known(observer, id)`; the hive worlds seen are the keys of the report's `worlds`. A report holds swarm FP and age only, so sizes, industries and tiers (`SwarmBastion.tier`) are read live from those markets (planet facts stay live by design). Only the player and mobilised factions (`ThreatIntel.observers()`) hold reports. -> `war-council.md` section 10
- **How is a hive found?** The player or a scout entering its system, or any non-Threat colony in the same system (`revealNeighbours`); the find is shared by all factions. A strike gives the struck faction a lead and sorties sweep nearest-first (`ThreatScoutRoute`). No lead since 2026-10-05 (`strikeLeads` false, Decisions). `ThreatScouts.reveal` / `leads` -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Finding the hive"
- **DECISION 2026-10-24 (the user, "Leave it"): the swarm's supplies famine is not re-prioritised.** In the user's game the offensive refused every prong "out on the supplies away" (fund 37k FP, 14 targets within 29 ly; 37 hives ate 64k supplies a month at half share against 56k made, founding 10k and building 8k on top, stock 0.1 months) while still founding. Strikes-before-foundings was offered and declined: it is building 8,250 a month of new makers and recovers on its own. No swarm priority change for this.
- **Why did a faction hold for years with a found hive in reach (the Hegemony, 2026-10-24: "they dont seem to be doing anything")?** Its council's picture (`ThreatWarCouncil.assess`) holds only systems the faction has a report of (`ThreatIntel.known`: its own or a coalition partner's); the Hegemony had no partner and no report ("picture 0 hives in 0 systems", HOLD 690 d), and could get none: `planRoute` skipped every found system (`discoveredSystems`) and a CONSOLIDATE faction's patrols kept within `scoutRangeLY` 10 while the nearest hive, Pelephanar, lay 29 ly out - a loop (no picture -> Hold -> CONSOLIDATE -> 10 ly). Fixed 2026-10-24 (the user: "scouts should have as much range as they have fuel"): patrols have no radius for any stance, and a found hive system the faction has no report of stays on routes and is not skipped as a known stop (`ThreatScouts.unreported`). A scout that finds a hive already turns home at once with the find (`ThreatScouts.onEnter`, `carriedIntel`). -> `information-by-ship.md` 3
- **Do scouts refresh what is known?** Yes since 2026-10-01: a scout entering a system writes a SCOUT report (`ThreatScouts.onEnter` -> `ThreatIntel.see`), and each faction's planner sends a recon party (`ThreatScouts.recon`, one stop from its nearest base) to a found system whose report is older than `intelHalfLifeDays`, or missing. Before it, no: 20-FP `PATROL_SMALL` fleets sail on leads (a strike's origin, 6 ly) and sweeps every 60 days within `scoutRangeLY` 10; `planRoute` skips discovered systems, and `reveal` appends an id to a bare list - no picture, figure or age is stored. `ThreatScouts.launch`
- **Do forward bases give radar?** Yes since 2026-10-01: forward bases, military worlds and the player's outposts see every found Threat system within `radarRangeLY` (10) once a day, to two significant figures (`ThreatIntel.radarSites`, `advanceDay`). Before it, the only detection was strike warning (`ThreatFrontlines.detectedAt`), which sees strikes in flight, not garrisons. Vanilla sensor arrays add +700 (+400 makeshift) sensor range in-system but reveal no intel; in-system reveal is possible via `setExtendedDetectedAtRange`, cross-system is an engine limit, and a 4 ly picket does not fit 10-36 ly hive geometry. -> `frontlines.md` "3. What is built"
- **How much does a faction trust a report, and who shares it?** Trust = 0.5^((age + travel days) / `intelHalfLifeDays` 30); a plan's chance is 1 - the product of (1 - trust) over its unresolved siege prongs (`ThreatIntel.trust`, `ThreatAttackPlanner.chance`). Coalition partners share reports as they are made, and the player gets those of factions at Cooperative (`ThreatIntel.sharersOf`); `report` returns the newest. -> `attack-planner.md` sections 1 and 11
- **How is a Threat strike detected?** A live fleet visible to the player, in a system with a non-Threat market, or in hyperspace within `strikeDetectLY` 4 of a military world or link (routes at their interpolated position). Detection mobilises every faction with a world in the sweep and gives each one's scouts a lead. `IncursionManager.detectStrikes` -> `frontlines.md` "3. What is built"
- **Does either side recall a force when home is attacked?** The humans never do: relief and guards are new fleets built from depot stock (`ThreatFleetOrders.planRelief`, `ThreatFrontlines.callGuard`), relief owed holds only new sieges and plays not yet under way (`reliefOwed`, `ThreatPlays.pausable`), and a siege turns back only when outweighed, broken or out of targets. The swarm does since 2026-10-04, because its strike IS its garrison (`launchStrike` musters the swarms on station; `prepDays` 7-14 at the colony before it sails): `ThreatPosture.recallStrikes`, the Decisions entry "Strikes come home when convenient". hw7: relief sailed 9 / 5 / 4 times (19.1k / 9.1k / 8.9k FP) for 9 / 5 / 6 Threat landings. -> `swarm-defence.md` "Strikes come home"
- **How long does a Defense Swarm take to reach a sibling world, against how long a siege lasts (hw9 logs)?** Within a system a median 2-4 days from the order to its arrival (p75 5-7, p90 8-10; clean single-fleet samples, n 8-45 a run); from a neighbouring system 1.7-5 ly off, 11-13 days. A siege spends a median 5 days over a world, 3 of them fighting (`ThreatPurgeFGI.dailyDay`), and off-screen it is a route that appears at its target world: nothing meets it at a jump point, and the fight weighs only the fleets within `ORBIT_HOLD_RANGE` 1500 of that world (`ThreatGroundFronts.hostileFleetsNear`), seated in its garrison or not. So a hop begun when the siege comes down arrives as the fight is decided.
- **Why did the deliberate massing (hw9, `postureMass` on) lose 3 of 3 where hw8's side door lost 1?** Three faults. The need it masses to is the system's whole pressure (every force seen bound for the system in 10 days plus 30 days of losses) laid on the worlds a force is over (`ThreatPosture.overWorlds`): a median 2.4-4.3 times the siege that came down, so calls never close and every hive system within `postureRecallLY` is stripped to its reserve count. A stripped colony is below its want (`regrowing`) and musters no wave or strike: CONSOLIDATE 47-68 months, 8-27 seeding swarms since contact (hw8a 190), 0.6-1.7M fuel unspent. And before a siege comes down the need is split by the size tables, so the median massed toward a system in the 10 days before a landing is 0 FP: the fleets arrive after it, one at a time; sieges called off 13-16% (hw8a 30%). My mend (a sighting carries the target world) was rejected; the user chose the system defending as one (`systemDefence`, hw10). -> `game-runs.md` 7, `tools/test-harness/digest/mass.pl`
- **Did the system defending as one (hw10, `systemDefence` on) change the war?** It works and the outcome is hw8's: one runaway (175 hives at m123), two exterminated (m93, m74). Every siege was seen coming, 164-2,902 fleets rallied a run, sieges called off 20-56% (hw8 10-26%), trade 1.25-1.35 Threat FP a human FP (1.34-1.45). But a siege that meets the massed defence turns home the same day with 87-98% of its FP (`ThreatPurgeFGI.callOff`: outweighed, it pays nothing; `BattleRules.callsOff`), where one that fights and is beaten leaves 72-74% behind, so the rally turns kills into call-offs: the sieges lost 25-34% of the FP they brought down against 32-53% in hw8. The swarm only ever fights outweighed. Proposed, the user's call: a called-off siege pays a day of the exchange as it turns away (trade in the siege fights 1.22-1.33 -> 0.90-1.15). -> `game-runs.md` 8, `tools/test-harness/digest/calloff.pl`
- **Is bombarding a hive priced by its size (2026-10-05)?** No. The 10^(size/2) curve is a human colony's razing bar; a hive has none (`ThreatRazing.razes`), so saturation over it burns `satFuelPerFPDay` 2.86 x FP a day and tactical `bombardFuelPerFPDay` 0.04 x FP, both adding `hiveSiegeSuppressDaysPerDay` 30 x fleet / (fleet + defence) x condition days a day to every structure. Size enters through defence only (medians 3.6k / 4.8k / 6k / 10.8k at size 3-6): a 1,400 FP siege needs about 38 / 47 / 55 / 92 days of bombing for 210 days down, 2-5k fuel today. The user's direction the same day: tactical takes the size factor at a fifth of saturation's price; the exact curve is being settled. The humans hold 1.5-1.9M fuel at contact and bank 125-140k a month (hw11a dumps). -> `ThreatGroundFronts.bombardStructures`, `suppressionRate`
- **How close do patrols come to each other (hw22-hw26, 2026-10-06)?** `ThreatSwarmPatrols.logApproaches` ("Patrols passed"): over 15 games a Patrol Swarm and a human party shared a system 2,343 times (closest approach a mean 6,325 units, least 14) and passed within 2 ly in hyperspace 19,800 times. After an in-system pass the human party was gone 268 times and the Patrol Swarm 30 - gone means despawned, home or dead, so this is an upper bound on kills. Scouts caught by a strike in flight (`meetAbstract` DESTROYED): 0-3 a game.
- **What paces the swarm's strikes (2026-10-07)?** THE OFFENSIVE (`ThreatOffensive.pass`, from `IncursionManager.tryStrikes` monthly): one prong a known target system, priced by `stagedPlan`; the campaign is the most valuable set the strike fund plus `strikeFundPerMonth` x the months to its deadline (`offensiveHorizonMonths` 12 from the last launch, toward 3 by the losing pressure) pays; nothing sails until the fund holds the whole bill, then every prong in one pass; at the deadline the fund pays what it can. Since hw48 a prong takes the hive's spare Defense Swarms first, up to its need, and the fund builds the rest (`strikes-staged.md` 3). Losing by degree (`ThreatStance.losingPressure` p, `strikes-staged.md` 4): the reach shrinks from all known toward `offensiveNearLY` 10 of a staging hive, forward bases and bases staging against a hive x(1+9p). Relief strikes at once, the opening floor per strike as before. Before it (`swarmOffensive` off): one strike a pass at the closest payable target - hw41a 122 strikes, 62 under 1,000 FP, the fund never above 7k, 268 waits.. Six batches on the second sector (hw42-47, `game-runs-2.md` 42): the mechanism works - saving, multi-prong, arriving together, sized for the expected answer - and there it is supplies-sized (the hives bank 0 supplies: growth, structures and the navy charge spend it all; a 36k FP campaign at 40 ly needs ~160k away, the spare flow keeps 8-15k FP away), hw47 humans 3-0 by eradication. hw48-50 (`game-runs-2.md` 43): the prongs take the spare garrison, and a Fable review (2026-10-07) found the deadline had never fired (a -1 start day), no strike had ever reported what it met (a -1 sample day) and the spare never carried the navy charge; with those fixed hw50 the swarm survives in all three games (17 / 31 / 31 hives, hw49 23 / 0 / 12) and the answer ratio learns (1.5-2.0x). Held prongs were refused on their day through hw51: sends and the upkeep recycle took their earmarked fleets (held back since hw51), then the sends and the first prong drew the stock their campaign was priced on (a flow hold protected nothing); since hw52 a held prong's trip supplies leave the stock at launch and return on its day, as its fuel does, and since hw54 it is not gated on supplies again and its plan is capped at the size priced (hw52: gated whole, each prong was charged the campaign's negative flow alone; hw53: gated on the burn beyond the prepaid trip, plans grown on the day's bigger first fleets were refused); since hw55 the pricing keeps a prong burning no new supplies whatever the flow (hw54b: 36k FP and 7.6k spare swarms idle 1,850 days, "nothing the fund pays", the spare negative - the swarm's supplies stock falls to ~0 once it founds 17-24 hives, sustenance + navy charge over the output; a balance question for the user); and a held prong's own earmarked fleets are offered to it on its day whatever its systems' reserves read (hw54c: the systems that gave the earlier prongs their swarms read "regrowing" and the third prong found none), and since hw57 that earmark's muster takes live fleets from every colony of the system whatever its own availability reads (`IncursionManager.launchingEarmarkCovers` in `musterPool`; hw56: the count was forced but the walk still gated, 29 of 42 held prongs refused with "0 FP of spare swarms"; the "Earmark at X" log line shows what each system found); since hw58 the prepaid mark, not an amount above 0, skips `canSustain` for every prong of a campaign, held or sailing now (hw57a: 13 fleets found, the first refused as a "new burn" of a few FP, "0 FP of spare swarms"). -> `strikes-staged.md` 2 **Defence first (user, 2026-10-10, C1):** while any hive is short of what today's pressure needs held (`ThreatPosture.defenceShortFP`), `ThreatOffensive.payDefence` pays it from the strike fund daily and no prong is priced or sails. -> `strikes-staged.md` 7
- **Where does a staged strike take its swarms from (2026-10-07)?** Every colony of the hive, weighed (`IncursionManager.stagedSpares`): fleets above a colony's posture want before any below it - below it only at a REAR colony (`ThreatPosture.frontline` false: no exposure to a known human world, no hostile seen lately); the front keeps its want, and within that the safest colonies (farthest from a known human world, `threatinc_spareGatherDistanceShare` 0.55 of the score) and the biggest surplus first; a prong's staging is still the hive system nearest its target (`ThreatOffensive.nearest`). No hard never-take: a world can be stripped, last. Until hw58 the systems nearest the staging gave first, each to its reserve (Decisions). -> `strikes-staged.md` 2
- **What does a quiet hive system want (2026-10-07)?** Its base x its exposure (`ThreatPosture.exposure`, `postureExposure`), at least one swarm a colony: 1 while hostiles were seen in it within `postureExposureSeenDays` (180) or a human world the swarm has seen lies within `postureExposureNearLY` (10), falling to 0 at `postureExposureFarLY` (30) and beyond, and 0 with no human world known - so before the first scout report every hive holds one swarm a colony and banks the rest. Need (a pressed system) is untouched. -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "Exposure". Since S1 (2026-10-10) an exposed colony's need is at least the line (`postureLine`, "The line" there).
- **Which faction does the offensive campaign against (2026-10-07)?** One at a time (`ThreatOffensive.chooseFocus`, `offensiveFocus`): every prong at the focus faction, kept while its worlds answer the strikes with at least `offensiveBrokenAnswer` (0.5) of what they were sized for (`ThreatSwarmIntel.answerShare`), something known of it is left and the means - fund, fuel AND the supplies away, as the campaign loop gates them - still pay a prong there (hw61a: on fund and fuel alone hegemony was kept nine months of "nothing the fund pays" with 114-118k FP banked); else the faction the means cover most completely x value. Spoiling blows while losing stay, whoever's; relief and retaliation are outside the campaign. Logs `Offensive: focus on`. -> `strikes-staged.md` 5.
- **When is the swarm losing (2026-10-07)?** By degree, `ThreatStance.losingPressure` 0-1, read each posture pass: the larger of hives fallen below the 365-day peak (one fallen = 0, full at `losingHiveShare` 0.5 of the peak) and Threat FP lost beyond FP sunk over 365 days against the forges' fabrication over it (full at `losingExchangeShare` 1.0); 0 with no faction at war. Logged `Stance: losing pressure a -> b (...)` on a 0.1 change. Before hw48 a flag (consolidating, the 60-day exchange, hives down in 90 days) that one lost opening flipped. -> `strikes-staged.md` 4
- **What makes the swarm take human worlds (hw20-hw27, 2026-10-06)?** Strike size and the war's opening, together. Staged strikes paid from the banks or the spare garrison stay 650-1,000 FP and open the war at day 915 (extinct 5 of 6). A strike fund (`strikeFundShare` 0.3) of at least 3,000 FP: hw19's range, 1 world lost; 5,000: two games won, one extinct, 2-13 lost; 8,000: six of six won, 64-185 hives, 10-23 lost. The 8,000 fund fills at day 1401-1493, so the hive is found at m49-52 against m41-43; hw27 opened 3,000 FP strikes at the 8,000 fund's time: the hive survives (15 / 82 / 40 hives) and takes 1-2 worlds - the opening decides survival, the strike size decides conquest. Left on: fund 0.3, 8,000 FP, by the swarm's own score (`data-drives-balance`); the user's call whether it should win this often. -> `game-runs-2.md` 17-24. **Corrected 2026-10-06 (the user): the 8,000 floor was only ever meant for the war's OPENING strike** - the hive grows strong before the first attack wakes the humans - and every later strike is sized to its target; the hw22-hw28 trials had applied it to every strike (sl2 spent 8,169 FP on each 120-defence forward base, one strike every four months at 60 bases). `strikeStagedMinFP` now binds only while no faction is mobilised (`IncursionManager.stagedPlan`); hw27 (3,000 later strikes) is the nearest trial of the intended rule, untested with no later floor.
- **Does the swarm kill forward bases (hw19-hw20, 2026-10-05)?** Barely with garrison strikes: 1, 1 and 24 of 52, 55 and 116 founded were destroyed by a strike in hw19 (the 24 in the runaway game); the strike gate passed over a base 485-2,042 times a game (defence 3,065 against a strike of 633). "No garrison to hold it" closures (22 / 14 / 49) are not the swarm's doing: `ThreatFrontlines` logged "the garrison of .. is gone" 0 times and "recalled (unpaid: ..supplies)" 20-23 times - a front base unguarded `frontlineAbandonDays` after its guard went home unpaid. Staged strikes (hw20a) destroyed 17 of 47. -> `game-runs-2.md` 16, 17
- **Is the hive's fabrication bank a store a strike can be paid from (hw20, 2026-10-05)?** No: the pooled banks of the whole hive (`ThreatColonyManager.hivePoolableFP`) read 22-1,400 FP after most launches; production is spent on garrison swarms as it is banked. The hive's mass is its garrisons (12-48k FP of fleets). -> `game-runs-2.md` 17
- **Why does a surviving swarm take no human worlds (hw15, 2026-10-05)?** Its strikes never arrive, and those that do are too small. 68 of 76, 117 of 124 and 203 of 215 strikes were recalled a median 2 days after launch (`ThreatPosture.recallStrikes`): with 4-5 sieges a month some system within 10 ly always reads short, and the whole strike goes home for any shortfall (3,353 FP recalled for 218 FP short of 7,044). A strike is one system's spare swarms (324-3,800 FP of a 114-177k FP swarm), so the strike gate passes over nearly every world (strike 400-1,300 against defence 450-1,900), and the 4-5 landings a run (633-1,400 troops) are overrun, 13 of 14. -> `game-runs-2.md` 13 With the recall off (hw16) every strike arrives, 9-17 landings a run take 1-2 worlds and 32 of 40 are overrun by relief at 2 to 1 over the guards; the swarm, its defenders gone, is dead or at 2 hives by m120. -> `game-runs-2.md` 14
- **What stops the swarm's FP income once it is found (hw11a, hw12a, hw12c; 2026-10-05)?** The sieges' tactical bombardment. A hive's Swarm Nexus reads disrupted in the first monthly dump after its first bombardment day and stays down until the hive is gone: a median 210-240 days, up to 780-1,470, disruption topped up to the 360-day cap; the Core follows at the landing. Hives able to earn (Nexus and Core up): hw11a 16 of 16 at m48, 6 of 16 at m52, 2 of 10 at m60, so income 2,800 -> 100 FP a month with 11 hives standing. The ordnance logged for it is 0.6-4.6k fuel a world (`siegeOrdnance`, 0.04 x FP x days). The swarm banks under a month of FP income and holds 150-300k fuel it never spends. Digest: `tools/test-harness/digest/organs.pl <tag>` from `tools/warsim/validation`. -> `game-runs-2.md` 9-11
- **Does turning the strike recall off help the swarm (hw13, 2026-10-05)?** No: `postureRecallLY` 0 in three runs, extinct m74, m63 and 1 hive from m84 (hw12a with the recall m95). The strikes sail (4 / 4 / 7 since found, no more than with the recall: the strike gate passes over most targets), take 1-2 worlds, 21 of 25 landings are overrun or destroyed, and the 7-9k FP is away when the sieges come. -> `game-runs-2.md` 11
- **Do more forges or 10 troops a broken-up FP save the swarm (hw12, 2026-10-05)?** No. `fabFPPerShipUnit` 200 gave four times the income and 70k FP of fleets; the humans sent 553k FP of sieges, 135 of 254 called off with 97% of their FP, the trade stayed 1.29 Threat FP a human FP and 48 of 54 strikes were recalled. `fabricateTroopsPerFP` 10 has 5-7 Threat landings a run to act on. `systemDefenceOnlyIfEnough` declines 398-1,245 rallies a run, outcome unchanged. `sbs.ps1 -Knobs` did not work as committed (`$knobs` was the `-Knobs` parameter itself); a knob at an old default is migrated back by a store below `MIGRATION_VERSION`. -> `game-runs-2.md` 10
- **How long does a hive world take to fall (hw8, hw10: 134 hives)?** Months, not days. The "median 5 days over a world" is the siege fleet's stay, because most sieges are called off on day 1 or beaten in 5-21 days; the orbit is what is decided in days (an outweighed garrison loses up to 75% a day). From the landing to the last stratum, medians by size 1-7: 10 / 42 / 62 / 83 / 87 / 121 / 139 days (ranges 10-25, 22-54, 31-340, 52-184, 75-119, 92-476, 113-309). From the first siege fight over a world to its fall a median 165-545 days a run. Fastest: two size-1 hives in 10 days; nothing larger in under 22. All that time an unspawned landing's flotilla holds the orbit as `GroundFront.coverFP` (median 0.9-1.5k FP); the swarm outweighed it in 7 of 16 / 3 of 31 / 0 of 21 landings (hw10) and 18 of 30 / 8 of 43 / 2 of 17 (hw8), and the hive overran 2 of 16 / 1 of 31 / 5 of 21 armies. Since 2026-10-05 the system defence rallies against the cover too (`ThreatGroundFronts.coverOver`, `ThreatPosture.poll`); hw11: it reached 3 of 19 / 11 of 45 / 0 of 21 covered landings, covers lost 1 / 8 / 3, and 9 of those 12 hives fell anyway. -> `game-runs.md` 8, `game-runs-2.md` 9, `tools/test-harness/digest/fall.pl`, `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "The swarm holds the orbit"
- **What decides whether the swarm lives in a player-less run (hw8, hw10)?** How much of it the humans have found, which sets how many sieges come. Found 41-64% of the hives at m48: alive at m123 (hw10a, hw8a, hw8b); 77-88%: extinct by m74-m93 (hw10b, hw10c, hw8c). hw10a and hw10b both hold 25 hives at m60; with 9 found 1.7 sieges a month (2.3k FP) come against an income of 4-7k FP a month, with 21 found 5.2 a month (7.1k FP) against 5-6k. Human FP sent against swarm FP built since found: 0.85-1.0 runs away, 2.2-5.9 dies. Pressed, the swarm neither seeds nor strikes (hw10b: 26 seedings, 20 strikes, fuel 26k -> 418k over m54-m70) - its fleets are in the defence, not a rule. -> `game-runs.md` 8, `tools/test-harness/digest/months.pl`
- **Why is the swarm no longer exterminated in hw8 (picket + recall), where hw7 lost 3 of 3?** The recall masses its defence by a side door. A strike musters every spare swarm of its system (`ThreatColonyManager.launchPool`; `ThreatPosture.launchSpareFP` reads each colony's own need, 0 with no force over it), the next pass reads the SYSTEM short (`poll`, `Call`) and sends the whole strike to its shortest worlds (`recallStrikes`), a median 3-4 days after launch. Sieges called off in orbit: 107 / 19 / 8 (hw7 0-6); the trade is unchanged (1.34-1.45 Threat FP a human FP). hw8a ran away (154 hives, 245k FP at m122), hw8b held to m84 then fell to 3 hives once it stopped launching strikes, hw8c launched one strike after contact and died at m89. The price: the strike's fuel there and back (hw8a spent all it made, 194 launches held for fuel after m96) and the offence (one human world lost in three runs). Stopping the launch alone (a hold) would end the massing: the deliberate version - the pass sends the spare swarms itself, and the hold with it - was built the same night on the user's word (Decisions, "Mass the defence"). The transfer pass could not do it: it reserves in fleet points (`thinnableFP` over `minimumFP`) and bars a colony or fleet moved in 30 days, where a launch reserves by fleet count (hw8a: Alpha Laphirial VI, a Bastion of light fleets, gave nothing by transfer and 5 of its 8 fleets to a strike). -> `game-runs.md` 6, `swarm-defence.md`
- **Does either side see an attack coming (hw7a-hw7c, 2026-10-04)?** In those runs the humans did, the swarm mostly did not; since then the hives keep a picket (built the same evening, untested: a human attack force in hyperspace within `swarmPicketLY` 4 of a live hive is seen, about 5 days before it enters the system at a route's 0.75 ly a day - `ThreatSwarmIntel.sees(where, hyper)`). As run: the swarm sees a siege only where it has eyes that day - a hive, a front or any Threat fleet, a Scouting Swarm included, in the same system - and nothing in hyperspace (`ThreatSwarmIntel.sees`, `eyesIn`): 55-64% of sieges were first seen the day they entered the hive system (47 of 74, 30 of 55, 23 of 40), 32-40% at dispatch from a base in a system it had eyes in (a forward base inside a hive system, a world its strike sat over), and a contact stops counting `swarmContactDays` (10) after it was last seen while a siege flies a median 30 days, so one seen sailing has faded before it arrives. A Scouting Swarm records the worlds of a system it enters; it catches a siege only by being there that day (the log says "eyes" either way). The humans see a strike in any system holding a colony or outpost and in hyperspace within `strikeDetectLY` 4 of a military world or forward base (`IncursionManager.detectStrikes`, `ThreatFrontlines.detectedAt`): all 26 strikes were detected by a world or base, 10-17 days after launch once forward bases stood by the hives; their scouting parties find hives and refresh reports, they do not spot strikes. Forces in flight never fight each other: far from the player they are routes, near it they are blinkered (`FLEET_IGNORES_OTHER_FLEETS`, `ThreatFleetOrders.leash`, `ThreatColonyManager.sendReinforcement`). -> `threat-fog.md` 2, `game-runs.md` 5

## Reach, convoys, hunting, links

- **What is a base's reach?** No radius: reach is the bill. An NPC base reaches as far as its reserve plus pooled `siegeDonors` pay the smallest flotilla (2 fleets of difficulty 5, 250 FP) in passage fuel (100 per ly) and supplies; an empty depot reaches nothing. Player bases use `ThreatColonyManager.fuelRangeLY`. `ThreatReach.baseRangeLY` via `IncursionManager.expeditionRangeLY` -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "The factions' reach and stance"
- **How far does one market's stock reach another?** `ThreatConvoys.stockReachLY` = donor fuel above its floor / `haulFuel(1)` (12 fuel per ly). `convoyRangeLY` and `logisticsRangeLY` are gone; player markets reach any range. `IncursionManager.marketsReaching` -> `strategy-convoys.md` "Convoys"
- **What gates a convoy?** Only voyage fuel (`ThreatConvoys.paysVoyage`: `haulFuel(ly)` from the donor's stock above its floor or the fuel it ships). Supplies size the escort and never gate it; no supplies means unescorted. A refusal logs `Convoy held:`. -> `strategy-convoys.md` "Convoys"
- **What does a pool pay without a convoy?** `haulPerUnit`: 0.006 fuel per marine per ly, 0.002 per other unit; fuel pays its own passage. None within a system or from player markets. Used by siege and hunt donors, link upkeep, founding, ordnance. `ThreatConvoys.haulPerUnit` / `netOfHaul` -> `strategy-convoys.md` "Convoys"
- **How is a staging base's stock target set?** `siegeWants` x `stagingTargetMult` 1.5 while `siegeFundable` across the `stockNetwork`, else the smallest hunt's wants; hysteresis 0.8 / 1.0; sieges funded cheapest first. `ThreatConvoys.siegeStock` / `stagingTargets` -> `strategy-convoys.md` "Convoys"
- **What triggers a hunting force?** Each slow tick, per running `ThreatSwarmBountyIntel`, each mobilised NPC tries its bases best-paid first (`huntBases`). Not if it already hunts there, the base sent within `softenIntervalDays` 30, the base has a siegeable hive (siege first), or a hostile faction works the system. Since 2026-10-01, not if the faction has no report of the system, which also stages nothing for it (`ThreatIntel`). `ThreatSoftening.tick` -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Hunting forces"
- **How is a hunt sized and run?** Defense Swarm FP over the siege's worlds x 1.5, at least the garrison now x 1.5, both from the faction's report at face value since 2026-10-01, with no ceiling; fleets of `softenFleetFP` 1500. Musters 3000 units off the anchor, goes in when all arrive or after 15 days, merges to at most 90 ships, retreats below 0.4 strength or after 60 days. `ThreatSoftening.send` / `advanceForce` -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Hunting forces"
- **What pays for a hunt?** Fuel FP/25 x ly x `expeditionFuelPerPointLY`; supplies FP/25 x 30. From stock above the floor and the staging bank, plus donors net of haul; if the swarm blocks the base's own siege, its staging bank too. `ThreatSoftening.donorSpendable` / `huntSpendable` -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Hunting forces"
- **Why does a siege sail right after a hunt?** A hunt that clears a world, or any battle sinking Threat ships in a bountied system, calls `IncursionManager.huntThinned`. Since 2026-10-01 that is news for every faction's planner, so each plans on the next poll instead of waiting out its week; before, it brought the monthly siege pass forward. -> `strategy-hunting-reach.md` "Hunting forces"
- **Why do gathering hunts draw the Threat in?** Posture pressure counts NPC staged stock and sortie capacity, so the Threat reinforces a system while the hunt is still mustering (Epsilon Shero I 621 -> 10.9k FP). Emergent, not a bug. `ThreatPosture.siegeCapacityFP` -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "Posture"
- **Does an NPC faction have money?** No. It banks a share of commodity flow each month and pays nothing for it; credit figures in siege logs only weigh razing against invading. `ThreatReserves.accrualPer30` -> `economy-coherence.md` "3. The coherence rules"
- **What caps a faction's fuel banking?** max(what it produces, the sector's best exporter x `reserveBankImportsMult`), so Sindria (~16-18k a month) caps every faction; a forward base adds only with real production. No sector pool exists: cutting Sindria does not starve anyone yet. `ThreatReserves.productionShare`
- **Why does relief stop late in the war (hw4p, hw4q, hw4r)?** The human factions run out of supplies, not fuel: war-reserve supplies fall from 20-320k at month 60 to near nothing by months 120-132, while fuel sits at 50k-1M. Fuel banks the producers' whole output (`ThreatReserves.wartimeFuel`); supplies bank only above peacetime demand (`surplusUnits`) and pay every fleet's monthly upkeep (`ThreatUpkeep.charge`, 1.9-2.3M by month 126). A 300 FP relief needs 180 supplies at its base (`canProvisionRelief`). Since 2026-10-04 supplies bank like fuel (`ThreatReserves.wartimeSupplies`): at any share from 0.1, 1-5 worlds were lost in 8 game runs (`war-sim-real-runs.md` 22). -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 21
- **What does the wartime supplies share do (game runs hw4s-hw4z, 2026-10-04)?** The war is two-state on supplies income. About 44k a month (share 0: the small factions bank only their surplus) starves the humans by months 108-126 and 15-37 worlds fall (hw4r, hw4u). About 60k or more (share 0.25 lifts the small factions to the import cap, 0.5 to it, 1 lets producers pass it, 74-99k) loses 1-5 worlds to months 128-144, with 12-78 Threat landings by month 120 against 81-123 on the old rule. 0.1 holds to month 126, then the famine returns (5 lost at month 132). On the default stance the swarm then stalls at any share (next line): 5 of 8 supplied runs spent 36-72 of their first 120 months in CONSOLIDATE, hw5c at 0.25 among them. `ThreatReserves.wartimeShare` -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 22
- **Why does the swarm consolidate for years once the humans are supplied?** It reads each staging base's stock as the siege it could pay (`ThreatPosture.siegeCapacityFP`: min of fuel and supplies over their price a point, x 25 FP). Under the surplus rule supplies bound it; wartime supplies fill the depots, the staged FP balloons, systems turn THREATENED and `ThreatStance` holds CONSOLIDATE (no founding): 39-72 months to month 120 at share 1, 7-53 at 0.1-0.5 (hw5c, 0.25: 53 months, 48 hives), 14-16 at 0. Not a misread: sieges have no fleet ceiling, so the stock does pay that siege; the humans just rarely send it. A consolidating swarm stays at 8-10 systems, where 4 pressed keep it at the 0.5 threshold. `stanceConsolidateShare` 0.75 shortens it at share 1 but does not end it (hw5a, hw5b, hw5d: 2, 0 and 31 months, 116, 167 and 75 hives at month 120, 3, 6 and 1 worlds lost; hw5d still had 6 of 7 systems pressed). The root is the staged read. CORRECTED 2026-10-04: what held the long stalls was one wounded system of two reading as half the hive (the next-but-one line, "Why did supplied hw4s stall"); `stanceConsolidateMinPressed` 2 ended them in the game (`game-runs-humans.md` 1). -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 22
- **What do NPC fleets pay while out?** Vanilla maintenance per day out (`ThreatFrontlines.maintenancePerMonth`), every 5 days, home base first then `payFromOthers`; a month owed sends the fleet home (`ThreatUpkeep.starve`). Exempt: player, Threat, link garrisons. Repairs and CR are never charged (repairs once billed garrisons 12,901 supplies a month). Vanilla itself charges AI fleets nothing; patrols pay through market demand. `ThreatUpkeep.poll`
- **Why did links once stall toward far hives?** `ThreatFrontlines.planFor` stopped at the first site it could not garrison; it now skips an `unguardable` site and goes down the list (3 -> 24 links per faction). -> `frontlines.md` "3. What is built"
- **What do links build?** From size 3 a Patrol HQ (Military Base at 4) and a battlestation (fortress at 4); in a free slot the producer of what the faction is shortest of - hulls (Heavy Industry), fuel, supplies - and with no slot free a producer of a stock in surplus is torn down for it, or the Patrol HQ with hulls to spare, one a faction a month over the vanilla build time (`ThreatFactionStock`, the hive's rules per faction; user 2026-10-06). They grow by `ThreatFrontlines.feedSize` on the size-upkeep curve; sizes 1-2 are free, so a link never dies of upkeep. -> `frontlines.md`
- **How are NPC Support/Defend sorties sized?** `ThreatFleetOrders.dispatchOrbit` builds `buildSortie` to `siegeOrbitNeeded`, paid in full or stays home (`sortie_unpaid` / `sortie_short`); contested fronts get one from `ThreatConvoys.supportFor`. NPC navies are autonomous; only the player issues board orders. -> `strategy-orbit-outposts.md` "Support, and the refuse rule"
- **How is relief sized?** Threat points over the world x 1.5, built at the nearest base whose depot can provision whole fleets of `reliefFleetFP` 300 at any distance (no radius since 2026-10-03), no fleet cap, holding until the army is gone (`threatinc_reliefStays`); marine convoys carry what beats the army by `siegeBeachheadMargin`. A faction owing relief starts no new siege or planner raid (`reliefOwed` gates only new work: plan, `sailPending`, `cheapestFirst`; nothing already sailing, no hunt or coalition answer, no direct launch). `ThreatFleetOrders.planRelief`, `ThreatGroundFronts.reliefNeed` -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Relief"
