- **How are forward bases (links) founded?** `ThreatFrontlines.plan` every 15 days per mobilised NPC: nearest found hive with no base or link within `frontlineReachLY` 10, anchored on the nearest connected colony or link, on an uncolonised planet within `frontlineLinkLY` 12. Must pay `ThreatOutposts.linkCost` and spare a garrison; CONSOLIDATE founds none. -> `frontlines.md` "3. What is built"
- **What does a forward base cost?** `ThreatOutposts.linkCost` = `npcCost` (1,500 supplies + 800 fuel) + `ThreatBuildCost.linkKit` (Population, Spaceport, Waystation, station at vanilla supplies, ~4,000), paid pooled (`ThreatFrontlines.canFund`) and, since 2026-10-11 (`foundingConvoy`), carried to the site by a real convoy that founds the base when it lands with the whole kit (`ThreatConvoys.dispatchFounding` / `pollFounding`, `ThreatFrontlines.foundLanded`; off: `drawFounding`, stood up on the spot). Later structures at vanilla supplies and build time: Patrol HQ 3,000/60 d, Military Base 4,500/120, Battlestation 5,000/120, Heavy Industry 5,000/120, Fuel Production 4,500/120.
- **What is still free in the human war economy?** Reserves are uncapped with no decay; mobilisation seeds `reserveInitialMonths` 6 free plus a free Waystation; militia marines trickle free (`reserveBaselinePerSize` 5); structures cost nothing to keep ("not now"); stand-down never happens by default (`warModeStandDownDays` 0). `ThreatReserves.accrualPer30`
- **Is a link a base, and what guards it?** Yes (`IncursionManager.isBase` takes any link with a Waystation; `ThreatFrontlines.isOutpost` means a link). A front link keeps the strongest strike in reach x 1.25, at least 200 FP; a rear link only gets the call when a strike is detected. -> `frontlines.md` "3. What is built"

## Ground fronts and landings

- **How does the player put marines on their own (or an ally's) colony under a Threat army (2026-10-17)?** The fronts table's **Reinforce** button on the army's row (`ThreatWarBoard.playerCanReinforce`): a convoy of marines and heavy armaments into the colony's stockpile - own colony `ThreatConvoys.reinforceTo`, ally `ThreatAid.dispatchReinforce` - sized by the Supply run ladder (`reinforceWants`: Min = `reliefNeed` + `defenderArmsNeed` net of inbound, never under one reference load (`convoyMarineCapacity` marines and their `frontResupplyDays` of arms - the player decides what is enough), Med x`convoyExtraLoadFactor`, Max = all the donor spares). The marines arm over `marineArmingDays`; the convoy waits off a blockade (`holdsOff`). The automatic relief (`planRelief`) still sails for every mobilised faction, the player's included. -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "Orders from the board"
- **Do a colony's defenders burn heavy armaments (2026-10-17)?** Yes: every armed marine burns the front rate (the whole garrison is in the field; engaged only caps the bleed), x `frontPushUpkeepMult` while the marines push as a landing pays (the user, Sindria 2026-10-24: "their arms consumption is the same as when not pushing"), from the colony's stockpile each tick (`ThreatGroundFronts.burnDefenderArms`, `defenderDailyUpkeep`), and with none in stock fight at `frontDryEffectivenessMult` (`defendersDry`, applied in `colonyMarineDefense`, so holding and counter-attacks both). Knob `defenderArmsEnabled`. A hive's troops never do. The front tooltip has the line. -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "Defenders burn armaments"
- **What does a convoy do at a blockaded colony (2026-10-17)?** Holds off at the system's jump-point (`ThreatFleetOrders.interceptPoint`, ORBIT_PASSIVE) while `ThreatBlockade.penalty` > 0, runs in when it lifts, gives up after `convoyBlockadeWaitDays` (30) and sails home with its cargo (`ThreatConvoys.holdsOff`, logs `Convoy holding off` / `runs in` / `gave up`). Plain convoys only - front runs have their own door (`pollFrontRun`). -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "Convoys wait off a blockade"

- **When does a besieger land?** `readyToLand`: orbit done (`orbitSpent`) or troops x `frontLandingMult` 0.75 >= 0.17 x defence. An NPC first landing also needs `beachheadSurvives` (troops x 0.75 x 2^(1/e) >= counter-attack x 1.25). Since 2026-10-04 that holds even once orbit is done (`siegeNoDoomedLanding`: short, the marines sail home), and sieges carry x `siegeMarineHeadroom` 2 for the marines lost with hulls. VERIFIED in the game 2026-10-04 (hw5e-hw5g): 4 of 35 human landings overrun against 19 of 41, 27 doomed landings refused, 6-14 hives eradicated against 5-8 (`game-runs-humans.md` 1). The player's landing is their call. `ThreatGroundFronts.readyToLand` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Sieges from orbit"
- **What are the front states?** Since 2026-10-23 derived from the line's rate (`lineSuppressionState`: HOLDING while the landing gains past `frontGrindBand`, GRINDING inside it, FOOTHOLD while it loses); the board shows the six line words instead (`lineState`) -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "The line". Before: HOLDING >= `frontHoldFraction` 0.17 x current defence, GRINDING >= 0.10 (hysteresis 0.1). Effective = troops x entrenchment: 0.75 on landing -> 1.0 over 30 days holding, frozen while pushing, halved on a layer taken or a counter-attack. `ThreatGroundFronts.effectiveStrength` / `entrenchMult` -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "The front"
- **How do fronts wear defences?** HOLDING wears Core/Nexus/port/defence structures and the shield; GRINDING only defences; FOOTHOLD nothing. Rate `frontWearRate` 12 x E/(E+D) days a day, not cut by condition. `ThreatGroundFronts.tickFront` -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "The front"
- **How is a colony's defence figure built?** Garrison (vanilla base x stability x districts held) + armed stockpile marines (`ThreatReserves.armedMarines`, ramp over 20 days, Storage never counts; the board calls them deployed, the rest reserves or staging for X) x fortification multipliers scaled by condition. `ThreatGroundFronts.colonyGarrison` -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "Sieges against human factions"
- **Why does a hive's Ground Defenses line vanish from the vanilla Defenses tooltip (2026-10-12)?** Not the proportional rule, which still runs (`disruptedDefenseResilience`: 1 - days / `defenseWearDays` 300, so 15 days on the clock = 95%): the structure's multiplier is 1 + bonus x input-deficit x condition, and vanilla's `getDeficitMult` is (demand - deficit) / demand, 0 when no heavy machinery or metals reach the hive (a full human blockade, `ThreatBlockade.hiveCut` at 1.25x the swarm's orbit points, sets shipping to 0 units in `applyPortDisruption`, and `getMaxDeficit` takes the worst input, so any hive without its own refinery loses its guns the day a siege outweighs the orbit - before a shot; `batteryShare` then reads 0, so the return fire and vanilla's bombard fuel bill collapse with it, never the 300-day wear). A x1 multiplier is hidden by vanilla. The figure left is the size anchor alone (`hiveDefensePerSize` 400 x size). `ThreatGroundDefenses.apply` -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "Fortification is hardware" **Fixed 2026-10-12:** the deficit is weighed by `defenseDeficitWeight` (0.5, both theatres, `ThreatSiegeMalus.weighDeficit`): a full deficit halves the bonus instead of removing it.
- **How often does a colony counter-attack?** It no longer does (2026-10-23): its marines push the line continuously with `defenderPressure` -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "The line". Before: `frontCounterAttackDays` 40, /1.5 with a military command, / force-ratio tempo (clamped 3x), and no more than `frontOverrunMusterDays` 10 when it would beat the army past 2:1 odds (`counterAttackOverwhelms`); never while its defenders are dug in (`defendersHoldFire`). Stability does not enter since 2026-10-17 (user). Marines count at `marineCounterAttackMult` 1.0; engaged defenders lose 20% per 30 days plus 15% per counter-attack. `ThreatGroundFronts.counterAttackTempo` / `spendDefendingMarines` -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "Marines defend, and they die", "The defenders choose too"
- **Can a defending colony choose how to fight (2026-10-17)?** Yes, like an attacker: **Push** moves the line with the defenders' pressure (since 2026-10-23; before, counter-attacks on the cadence); **Dig in** holds fire and earns `frontEntrenchDefenseBonus` 1.5 on the defence figure over `frontEntrenchDays` 30, lost (`defenderEntrenchKeptFraction` 0) when the army takes a district. The player's own colonies take the order from the fronts table; NPC colonies push when the counter-attack would win and dig in when it would bounce; hives always counter-attack. `GroundFront.defence`, `ThreatGroundFronts.tickDefenderStance` / `defenderCoverMult` -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "The defenders choose too"
- **How often does a hive counter-attack?** It no longer does (2026-10-23): it pushes the line with `defenderStrength x hiveCounterAttackPace` continuously -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "The line". Before: 40 / `hiveCounterAttackPace` (fed share / break-even x strata not held / size) / tempo; a hive uses its whole body. -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "Marines defend, and they die"
- **How big is a Threat landing?** SHIPS ARE THE SOLDIERS (2026-10-25): every troop is a hull broken up at `fabricateTroopsPerFP` (5) - `ThreatStrikeFGI.embark`, flagship and last hull spared. A first landing is max(`strikeFrontMinTroops` 300, `overrunTroops` - the landing whose line overruns the world, 2026-10-25), a reinforcement is `fabricateNeed`; what can still land is `landable` (fragments aboard + hulls); under 50 the landing is called off. The free pool `strikeTroopsPerPoint` is 0 (was 20). Passes per world = fleets + 2. `IncursionManager.expeditionPasses` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Sizing the landing"
- **Do Threat fronts use armaments?** No (`needsArms` false unless `threatFrontNeedsArms`). A pushing Threat front paid 2x casualties (`threatPushLossMult`) until THE ATTRITION RULE (2026-10-24: attacking costs the cover instead); reinforcement is the next expedition, weighted 4x toward a losing front. -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "The swarm does not fight on armaments"
- **Who holds an orbit?** Orbits are per planet: armed non-trader hostiles within 1500 su hold it while their FP >= 0.5 x the besieger's warships there; garrisons are leashed within 700 su. A held orbit blocks landings and every tactical and saturation slice; abstract sieges skip the check (autoresolve weighs every Threat fleet in the system). `orbitContested` (head-count) is only for convoy refusal. `ThreatGroundFronts.orbitHeld` / `orbitContestedFor` -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "NPC fronts"
- **Can marines raid fortifications?** No; only a landed front reaches the guns. On a hive only the Fabrication Core is raidable (`ThreatFortificationRaids.OrganRaid`). -> `suppression-balance.md` "2. No raids on military structures"
- **What happens when the Threat takes a colony?** `convertConquered`: vanilla teardown, then a size-2 hive paid by the nearest live colony's bank; the landing fleet digs in as its garrison. A Threat front kills a colony only by taking its last district. -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "When the colony falls"

## Bombardment, saturation, off-screen fights

- **Who pays in an off-screen fight?** Both. The attacker loses min(0.75, 0.5 x defence/strength) per world; every hostile fleet vanilla counted loses min(0.75, 0.5 x attacker/defence) of its FP. Knob `abstractDefendersFight`. `ThreatAbstractBattle.fought` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Off-screen fights cost both sides"
- **How many ways into an off-screen `autoresolve`?** Four: `resolveOnArrival`, `notifySegmentFinished` at the payload's end, vanilla `FGRaidAction.directFleets` once the player is out of spawn range, and vanilla's own damage loop. Guard each against double charging. -> `ground-war-code-paths.md` 1
- **Does the abstract battery toll reach route damage?** No: it lives in `abstractLeft` and the cargo scaling; the spawn count, `refundOnReturn`, `settleLedger`, upkeep and the next world's start read route damage only. -> `ground-war-code-paths.md` 4
- **How often does a watched siege slice?** Per fleet about every 3.3-4.5 d (vanilla `checkRaid`: tracker, 0.5 d hold, 3 d `RECENTLY_PERFORMED_RAID`); `siegeSliceDays` integrates the gap, clamped to [0.5, 10] d, first slice 3. `breaksOff` and the leash run every frame. -> `ground-war-code-paths.md` 3
- **Can the orbit contest weigh an abstract besieger?** Not as is: `orbitHeld` reads the friendly side from real fleets only, so an abstract flotilla counts 0 and any defender holds. Supply its abstract FP. -> `ground-war-code-paths.md` 7
- **How fast does orbit wear fortifications?** Per day per world: rate x F/(F+D) x condition; rate 12 colony, 30 hive. Condition 1->0 over 180 days (colony) / `defenseWearDays` 300 (hive), no floor. `ThreatGroundFronts.siegeSlice` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Sieges from orbit"
- **When does a commander stop bombarding?** `orbitSpent`: a day gains less than the 1 d/day repair, takes under `bombardFPWorth` 30 defence per FP lost (`hiveBombardFPWorth` 10 on a hive, 2026-10-13), or would drop the fleet under 0.33. Worth is waived while NPC troops aboard cannot hold. The swarm values a hull at 44 (`hullWorth`). -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Bombardment v2"
- **What do the guns cost bombarders?** Per day 0.0008 x the defence Ground Defenses/Heavy Batteries add (0.0024 on a hive since 2026-10-13, `hiveBombardReturnFirePerGunDefence`), smallest ship first, flagship spared; one faction's fleets over one world answer once (`orbitPoints`). Fuel 0.04/FP/day tactical. `ThreatGroundFronts.returnFirePerDay` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Bombardment v2"
- **Does saturation raze a hive?** Never. Over a hive it is per-day wear (30 x F/(F+D) x condition x shield), unrest 10 x (1 - condition) as tactical (2026-10-13; was a flat 10), growth paused, 2.86 fuel/FP/day. A hive dies only to a ground victory (`ThreatColonyManager.eradicate`). `ThreatRazing.saturationSlice` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Saturation presses a hive"
- **How is razing a human colony priced?** Fuel per level at size s = 10,000 x 10^(s/2) / 144.8 (sizes 1-8: 218 / 909 / 3,093 / 10,000 / 31,841 / 100,910 / 319,320 / 1,010,000), poured at 2.86 x FP a day, cut by shield throughput; a level paid is a size off, story-critical stops at 3.
- **Does the swarm raze human colonies?** Not by default (`strikeSaturationEnabled` false): same engine and 0.0008 gun constant, but it lands a real ground war, pays bombardment fuel from the hive stock, and has only the launch gate at ratio 1.0 (no break-off on arrival, no 1.5x margin). `ThreatStrikeFGI.saturationPass` `ThreatRazing.levelFuel` -> `suppression-balance.md` "4. Saturation bombardment"
- **Bombing squadron vs flotilla?** The flotilla holds orbit; a squadron (least that reaches the stop, stepped x1.5) bombs, chosen by cheapest fuel + trip supplies per day down. `ThreatGroundFronts.squadronPlan` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Saturation presses a hive"
- **When does an NPC raze instead of land?** A colony where razing fuel costs less than the landing's marines and arms at base prices; a hive only where its landing is beyond the marines held and it still produces, never with a front on it. `IncursionManager.razeWorlds`, `npcRazeEnabled` -> `ground-war-sieges.md` "Saturation presses a hive"
- **Why did supplied hw4s stall (2026-10-04)?** Not because sieges cannot sail: hammers struck 18 of 23; the 432 postponements are STARVE saturations, short of the whole raze set's fuel (600k-950k at the home system) and of supplies for ~110 days a world. What held CONSOLIDATE from month 66 is a wound: STARVE saturations kept Epsilon's Ports and Nexuses disrupted for 65 months (`ThreatPosture.read` wounds on `anyOrganDisrupted`), a hive has no razing bar, the invasions after were overrun, and with 2 systems 1 wounded is the 0.5 `stanceConsolidateShare`. Staged stock drove the earlier THREATENED turns (80%). -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 1
- **What stops one pressed system of two holding CONSOLIDATE (2026-10-04)?** `threatinc_stanceConsolidateMinPressed` (default 2): the share counts only with at least that many systems pressed, or all of them (`StanceRules.pressedEnough`, called by `ThreatStance.evaluate` and the simulator). VERIFIED in the game 2026-10-04 (hw5e, hw5f, hw5g): 11, 0 and 8 months in CONSOLIDATE to month 120 against 73, 57 and 40 before, 96-154 hives, out of every CONSOLIDATE within 1-6 months. The war is hotter for it: 60-99 Threat landings and 3-8 worlds lost by month 120 against 12-29 and 1-2. -> `game-runs-humans.md` 1
- **When does Defend vs Support bombard?** Support whenever the orbit is clear; Defend only while its own front cannot hold. Only the Threat fabricates troops from hulls (`defendFabricates`; off with `fabricateDefendEnabled`, which keeps the beachhead's break-up). A navy's Defend goes home when the front is gone, or it is worn below 0.33 and outweighed (`navyHoldsOver`). -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Fabricating troops from the fleet"
- **Who pays ordnance?** The fleet's own fuel, then its supply line's spendable reserve (`payOrdnance`); with none it holds orbit idle. The swarm pays from the hive's fuel stock. -> `suppression-balance.md` "Built 2026-09-28"
- **How does the planetary shield work?** Absorb = 0.75 x shield condition; all other disruption x (1 - absorb); the shield wears from orbit and fronts. `ThreatShield.throughput` -> `ground-war-defenders.md` "The planetary shield"
- **DECISION 2026-10-13 (user, after the return-fire tables in `suppression-balance.md`): hive guns fire at 0.0024, three times a colony's 0.0008, and a hive commander's worth is 10 (`hiveBombardReturnFirePerGunDefence`, `hiveBombardFPWorth`, `ThreatGroundFronts.returnFireConstant` / `bombardWorth`).** Why: "a size 8 hive over a 30 day siege with heavy batteries should annihilate multiple 1000fp of fleets"; the stop-aware tables showed 0.008 shuts every NPC commander with marines out of bombarding a fortified hive (6 defence per FP at 10,000 FP against the 30 demanded) and kills a 1,000 FP player fleet in 17 days, so the user took the recommendation: x3, with worth 10 so the same commanders bombard a size-8 hive as before (6,000 FP and up). A 1,000 FP fleet lingering 30 days loses 510, 3,000 loses 359, nothing dies. Colonies unchanged. A pre-0.8 NPC balance change on hives, knowingly: the batch sweep verifies it (`handover-2026-10-13-testing.md`).
- **Why can I not bombard a hive my navy is already bombarding (2026-10-13 evening, Delta Mengryla I)?** Two Defense Swarm scraps of 7 and 12 FP, kept 1,200-3,000 su off by six navy fleets, counted as inbound and locked the menu; the siege itself bombarded because it uses the orbit strength contest. Now `ThreatincMarketCMD.gateOnDefenders` asks the navy's own gate, `ThreatGroundFronts.orbitContestedFor(player)` (armed swarm fleets within `ORBIT_HOLD_RANGE` at `orbitContestFraction` of the player faction's warships there): held closes raid and bombardment, not held leaves vanilla's verdict, and the text says "Your side holds the orbit". User's rule: if the AI navy can bombard, the player can -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "Military options"
- **Why can I not bombard a hive whose swarms are across the system (2026-10-13, Delta Mengryla I)?** Three reinforcements that fought on arrival (`engageOnArrival`, aggressive, blinders off) were dragged 13-15,000 su off by vanilla's pursuit AI, and `ThreatincMarketCMD.gateOnDefenders` counted any fleet bound for the world as inbound at any distance. Now an engaged reinforcement is leashed back like a garrison (`ThreatColonyManager.enforceReinforcementLeash`, beyond `ORBIT_HOLD_RANGE`) and the menu counts a fleet inbound only within `defendInboundRange` (3,000 su; 0 = any) -> `ground-war-fronts.md` "Military options", `swarm-defence.md` "Fight on arrival"
- **Player bombardment limits?** One a day (`bombardCooldownDays`, shared tactical/saturation); unrest raised to 10 x (1 - fortification condition) by either kind (saturation was a flat 10 until 2026-10-13), never stacked. `ThreatincMarketCMD.bombardTactical` -> `suppression-balance.md` "1. Tactical bombardment"

## Hive economy

- **How is a Threat fleet paid for?** From a per-colony FP bank filled by forge SHIPS output x `fabFPPerShipUnit` (100 FP per unit per 30 d), split across nexuses by hulls drawn. No bank, no build. `ThreatColonyManager.fabricationRatePerDay` / `forgeOutput` / `nexusDraw` -> `hive-economy.md` "Fabrication bank"
- **Why is a hive earning 0 FP?** Its Fabrication Core or Swarm Nexus is down or disrupted, no forge reaches it, or the forge is disrupted. More nexuses split the same hulls; only forge output raises the total. `ThreatColonyManager.nexusDraw` -> `hive-economy.md` "Fabrication bank"
- **What stops a rich hive hoarding fleets?** Garrisons cost 0.04 of their FP per 30 d (`garrisonUpkeepPerMonth`), so they settle at income / rate; with the bank negative, `recycleForUpkeep` scraps the weakest for 0.8 of their FP. `ThreatColonyManager.upkeepPerDay` -> `hive-economy.md` "Upkeep"
- **Is there a cap on force size?** No. Bounds are banks, stocks and yards plus engine limits (`maxShipsInAIFleet`, fleet difficulty 0-10, hive `colonyMaxSize` 8). `SIEGE_FLEETS_SANITY` is a loop guard only. -> `design-theory.md` "2a"
- **What do hives stockpile?** Two hive-wide stocks, fuel and supplies, gaining every plant's and forge's whole output x econ unit (fuel 1,500, supplies 750) x `hiveSurplusMult` (1.0; `reserveSurplusMult` until 2026-10-02) a month; no Spaceport demand deducted, nothing imported. `ThreatFuel.perMonth` / `accrue` -> `hive-stocks-and-upkeep.md` "Structures cost supplies"
- **What draws on the hive stocks?** All through `ThreatFuel.pay`: passage, founding cargo, structures, away-fleet supplies, size upkeep, bombardment fuel. Unpaid demand is booked (`heldBuild`, `unmet`) and drives the planner. -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **Where does surplus go?** `convertSurplus` turns every surplus fuel plant the production left can spare (as many a turn as the surplus spares, since hw60) into a forge, then a missing chain link, then a Swarm Bastion; idle FP buys a Bastion then Command (`militaryIdle`); `retireMilitary` swaps back to production. Surplus: P >= D and S >= D x T. `ThreatFuel.surplus` -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **What binds the swarm's war economy, and what is reserved (hw60, 2026-10-07)?** Supplies: hw60c made 197k a month and spent 207k (colony sustenance 87k, navy upkeep 8k, the rest fleets away, builds and foundings) with the stock at 0-7k, 2.5M fuel (70-130 months) and 294k FP idle; 176 seedings held on supplies, 20 of 29 campaign saving lines "not yet kept". Since hw61 a forge the planner waits to build or convert to has its price reserved and sustenance, growth, foundings and new trips draw on `ThreatFuel.free` (the humans' yards-first rule for the hive); the standing navy's upkeep and the fleets away pay from the whole stock (hw61b lost 30 swarms to unpaid upkeep when they drew on the free stock) -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "The shortage's answer is paid first"
- **Why did the strike fund hoard with the supplies spare at zero, and what spends it (hw62, 2026-10-08)?** The fund banked 38k -> 141k FP in every hw62 game and never fielded a prong of its own: the colonies' spare for new trips (`ThreatColonyUpkeep.feed`'s "spare") kept sustenance at its share of the production - an expansion tithe of 1/0.7 x sustenance, ~13k a month in hw62a - and seedings took 5k each (110-173 a game), so every month's supplies went to spread while the fund sat and hw62a's hives fell 76 -> 47 to Tri-Tachyon's fronts. Since hw63 (THE CHEST - my shape; hw63 confirms it: campaigns 48 / 22 / 17 against hw62's 15 / 11 / 16, eradicated hives 0 / 4 / 8 against 75 / 35 / 32, the fund cycling between the horizon and half of it; the costs are foundings held while full and small swarms lost to upkeep with the stock at zero): a fund holding a horizon's saving (`strikeFundPerMonth` x `offensiveHorizonMonths`) with a known target is a full chest (`ThreatStance.chestFull`, left below half of it); then the spare is the production less what the colonies actually took, nothing is founded the supplies surplus does not pay (`ThreatFuel.canFound`), and a full chest is a reason to PRESS (not losing). Log: `Stance: the chest is full/spent - N of M FP a horizon` -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "Stance" **Since hw70 (2026-10-08): full also needs the fund to cover the cheapest priced prong (`ThreatOffensive.cheapestProngCost`) - see "Why did the ck3 swarm open the war at 13 hives".**
- **Why did no hull convoy sail in hw60-hw62 (2026-10-08)?** Two gates, no log: `hullsSurplus` asked the donor for no unrebuilt losses (fixed hw62), then `pickAllyDonor` read the minimum load off the cargo hold - 30 units (3,000 FP) against Tri-Tachyon's 25 spare in hw62b. Since hw63 hulls sail by the unit (a ship is a load), and `aidHulls` logs `Ally aid: no hulls for F (N units short at port) - helper gate, ...` monthly while nobody sends. -> `player-aid.md` "Aid X with N ship hulls"
- **How big is a prong (hw61)?** `ThreatOffensive.price`: the larger of the defence seen on the day and (defence + the faction's navy seen elsewhere / prongs at it) x `ThreatSwarmIntel.responseRatio`, which now learns both ways (hw60 met 0.41-0.61 of sized; floored at 1 every prong was double) -> `strikes-staged.md` 3
- **Why did no hull convoy sail in hw60 (2026-10-07)?** `ThreatFactionStock.hullsSurplus` asked the donor for no unrebuilt losses at all, and a faction at war always has some (Tri-Tachyon: 3,400 free, 640 lost; the Independents at 0 free, 3,564 to rebuild). Since hw62 a donor needs free hulls only (`ThreatHulls.freeFP` is already net of its debt); the Patrol HQ retirement in `ThreatFrontlines` keeps the strict test -> `player-aid.md` hull aid
- **Why was a colony with reinforcements inbound stripped to zero (hw60b Chlorr)?** `stagedSpares` tier 1 measured the room above want with `ownedFleetFP`, which counts reinforcements in flight and raiders out; since hw61 the room is what stands on station -> `strikes-staged.md` 3
- **What do Swarm Bastion and Swarm Command give?** The hive's Military Base and High Command (two rows on `SwarmBastion`; needs a Swarm Nexus). `garrisonReserve` = base x (1 + tier): x2 Bastion, x3 Command, staying home and paying upkeep; ground defence x1.2 / x1.3, worn by disruption. `SwarmBastion.tier` -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **What do they cost and how long?** Vanilla supply cost at 1.2 supplies per FP: Bastion 3,750 FP, Command 1,250 FP; vanilla's 120-day build. Command is an in-place `startUpgrading`, no slot. `ThreatBuildCost.fleetPoints` / `buildDays` -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **When is one built?** `maintainMilitaryTier` / `militaryIdle`: garrison at its posture want, income carries the extra upkeep, pooled banks (`poolableFP`) hold price + `militaryExtraFP` + upkeep over the build, and a free or conversion-freed slot - not while supplies or fuel are wanted and the world lacks that producer. -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **When is one torn down?** `retireMilitary`: only if the hive needs a producer (a missing chain link, or a stock `mayAnswer` says runs dry) and no world has a free slot. Bastion before Command, bigger world first; the producer then takes its build time. Skips worlds under a front, saturation or building. No refund. -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **How does the planner add or retire plants and forges?** `runsDry`: S < (D - P - C) x T, T = 4 months; one answer per stock per 30 d (`mayAnswer`); spares only if `wantsSpare`. Plants retire only if `surplusProducer` and never the last, biggest, or one with a relic or AI core. Forges never retire, so while fuel is tight (`wantsSpare`) idle supplies build a fuel plant before a forge, one a month, once the hive has two plants (`investFuelWhenTight`, `investFuelMinPlants`; 2026-10-03, hw4n's stall, hw4o's lost forge). -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **Why does a second equal-size producer add nothing?** Availability is a broadcast: each market uses its best single source. Deficits come only from size mismatch or starved inputs. `ThreatColonyManager.planHiveEconomy` -> `hive-economy.md` "Availability is a broadcast"
- **Why is hull supply always short?** Nexus demands ships at size, a forge supplies at size-2; the gap scales the garrison by vanilla's ship-deficit multiplier. Home relics close it. `SwarmNexus.apply`, `maintainHomeRelics` -> `hive-economy.md` "Vanilla industry numbers"
- **How do hives grow and shrink?** Size 3+ costs `sizeUpkeepAt3` 100 x 2.5^(size-3) supplies a month; 1-2 free. Paid in full grows at 60 d x size; below the 0.5 break-even a size is lost per 90 d. Sustenance capped at the largest stance share (`sustainShare`, 0.7); growth gets the stance share (expand 0.5 / press 0.7 / consolidate 0.5; consolidate was 0.9 until round 20). Never desized by razing. `ThreatColonyUpkeep.perMonth` / `growthRate` -> `hive-stocks-and-upkeep.md` "Size upkeep"
- **What does a hive structure cost?** Vanilla credits / 100 x `structureSuppliesMult` in supplies (`buyStructure`); unaffordable builds wait (`buildWaiting`). `foundingFPPerStructure` applies only with `structuresCostSupplies` off. `ThreatBuildCost.supplies` -> `hive-stocks-and-upkeep.md` "Structures cost supplies"
- **What does a strike, raid or scout cost?** No radius (`billedReach`). Passage FP/25 x ly x 10 fuel (0.4 per FP per ly round trip) plus about 0.78 supplies per FP a month away; `canSustain` must hold, so cutting supplies stops launches. `ThreatFuel.passage`, `ThreatReach.canSustain` -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Reach is the bill"
- **What does a Seeding Swarm carry?** 1,500 supplies + 800 fuel (`ThreatOutposts.npcCost`) plus `foundingKit` (about 3,500 supplies) and passage; the new hive starts with an empty bank. Waits while `canFound` fails. -> `hive-stocks-and-upkeep.md` "Size upkeep"
- **When does the swarm open a war with a faction?** Before phase 3 only one already at war, with a grudge (`ThreatAlarm.grudge`), or that never mobilises. Phase 3 needs a size-6+ forge world and a core world the stock can fuel a swarm to. `IncursionManager.warOpen` -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Reach is the bill"
- **How big a garrison does posture want?** `wantFP` = max(base, share of need); need = pressure / 1.5 x `postureMargin` 1.25; base = `garrisonReserve` swarms at the cheapest rows (`minimumFP`) + `LAUNCH_STOCK` 2 rows at a forge colony of size >= min(`spreadMinSize`, `strikeMinSize`); PRESS adds `ThreatStance.extraWantFP`. Since 2026-10-04 the pressure leaves out staged depot capacity, no system is written off, and once a force is over a world the need stands at that world (`overWorlds`), not split by the tables. Surplus goes to pressure transfers, then waves/strikes, then recycling after 150 d. `ThreatPosture.wantFP`, `redistributeByPressure` -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "Posture"
- **Does the swarm take a feint?** Narrowly. An attack counts at its target from dispatch; within one posture pass (5-5.6 days) the target's want rises to about 0.83x its pressure, and the deficit moves one whole fleet per donor, same system first, then the nearest. So a feint pulls only if it outweighs what the system holds (by about 1.2x) or sinks swarms. Donors refill from their banks in 1-5 days; a colony that received rests 30 days before it can donate. Staging against a system raises its pressure before anything sails. No reply swarms: the only retaliation is a strike after a hive is eradicated (`IncursionManager.retaliate`). -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "How fast it answers", `war-council.md` section 9
- **What can a raid wear down, and what needs saturation?** A raid (`dispatchRaid`, `siegeSlice`) wears the war-strata only: defence structures, the Swarm Nexus and the Bastion or Command (`Theatre.fortifications`). With the Nexus down a colony cannot rebuild garrisons. Forges, ports and fuel plants fall only to saturation (`saturationSlice`), which runs inside a siege expedition (`razePass`, the squadron) or the player's Bombard. -> `war-council.md` section 10
- **What is the Threat's stance?** One sector-wide PRESS / EXPAND / CONSOLIDATE per posture pass; sets `claimCap` via `expansionShare` (1 / 0.5 / `stanceConsolidateSpreadShare`, 1 since 2026-10-04, was 0), PRESS staging want, growth share; dwells 30 d. `ThreatStance.stance` -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "Stance"
- **What does disrupting or blockading a hive do?** A disrupted port caps shipping at 3 units and halves imports; a blockade cuts imports half or all (`ThreatBlockade.hiveCut`); a raided forge stops stock and growth. `applyPortDisruption`, `ThreatPurgeFGI.raidValue` -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Levers, verified"
- **What locked hw137c's swarm on fuel (2026-10-10)?** One world's port. A fuel plant demands volatiles at its size and loses output by the deficit (size-2 minus it); off a deposit it imports from the single best in-faction source (the broadcast), and on OceanPena that is Unhcegila (home, size 7, volatiles 8) in all three games - every other source is 3-6. In hw137c the humans held Unhcegila from war day 3,049 (163 daily-siege slices, 9 port cuts from d3,164, blockaded 13-59% of days d3,060-3,800 and all of it from d3,960; a: 51 slices from d3,694, b: 5): its exports fell to 3 units ("In-faction shortage (max in-faction production source has 3)" at every importer in the d3,566 save), every plant off a deposit made 1 fuel instead of 4-5, and the 17 plants the planner built from d3,127 (`comingPerMonth` counts size-2, not the input) added ~1 each - output 20 units from 4 plants before, 18-24 from 8-15 after (a: 53 from 11, Unhcegila open). Stock 311k (d2,205) -> 0 (d3,309), every strike, raid, patrol and seeding then held on fuel, hives 35 -> 2. Not the claims loop the handover guessed: c's claims and volatiles matched a's to d2,756 (36 / 35 claims); claims stopped at d2,794 with 231k fuel because the cap was full (16-23 claims pending against 11-22 free forges). Reads: `tools/test-harness/reads/fuelseries.pl`, `savecom.pl` (a save's `available` breakdown per hive), `blockfrac.pl`. -> handover 37
- **Why didn't hw137c's swarm settle another volatiles world (2026-10-10)?** It could see no shortage to chase. `pickSpreadTarget` and `pickExpansionPlanet` weighted deposits by today's shortage (`groupMineableDeficits`, replaced 2026-10-10 by `mineableNeeds`, a balance across inputs at full growth, blind to blockades) - each colony's demand minus what it can get NOW - and Unhcegila's 8 units (size 7, diffuse, +3 from its Plasma Dynamo home relic) reached every plant, so volatiles read 0 and the held systems filled by `depositScore`, where an ore + rare-ore world beats a lone volatiles deposit (Alpha Pantheon, claimed d2,035: Alpha Pantheon II settled, its two abundant volatiles worlds never). The d3,566 save mines ore 98 units, rare ore 39, organics 14, volatiles 17. Free volatiles worlds stood in held systems (Gad plentiful, Loutron abundant, Sherden) and empty systems 2-4.5 ly out (Kumari Kandam, Isirah, Zeta Ang, Zeta Xanthe Che - plentiful). Once the siege made the deficit real the pull worked, too late and too small: Gad founded d3,416, Zeta Ang IV-A d4,080. Volatiles mining is size + richness - 2 (Michon abundant size 6 -> 5, Corwin trace size 6 -> 3), so a plentiful world must reach size 7 to feed one size-7 plant alone. Read: `tools/test-harness/reads/volsys.pl` (save + sim map). -> handover 37
- **Why do importers read an old figure after a build?** Vanilla recomputes supply monthly; structural changes call `markEconomyDirty` and `flushEconomy` runs `tripleStep` once a tick. -> `hive-economy.md` "The monthly lag"
- **How do NPC reserves fill?** Per 30 d: `getSizeMult(surplus over peacetime demand)` x econ unit x `reserveSurplusMult`; troops at 0.5. Mobilised, the reserve covers a colony's peacetime deficit first, up to 0.5 per issue. `ThreatReserves.accrualPer30` / `coverShortage` -> `economy-coherence.md` "3. The coherence rules" Since the wartime shares (`reserveWartimeFuel`, `reserveWartimeSuppliesShare` 1.0) a producer's WHOLE output banks, held to the faction's own making or the sector's best exporter (`shareFigures`): hw31a Hegemony "made 16, banked 108 gross, share 15%" = 16 units x 750 x mult; the hive's rule (`ThreatFuel.perMonth`) is the same whole output x `hiveSurplusMult`. Parity (user, 2026-10-06): `reserveSurplusMult` 1.5 -> 1.0, equal to `hiveSurplusMult`; the one asymmetry left is imports (`reserveBankImportsMult` 1.0 - the hive imports nothing).

## Player aid, Nexerelin, omens

- **What can the player do to help a faction?** Mobilised, faction at war with the Threat, standing >= -0.5: Defend, Aid and Hunt from the player's own bases, sized by fleet-size x `aidBaseFP` 100, no credits. Rep = value / 10,000, cap +10. Plus missions, in-person hand-over and swarm bounties. `ThreatAid.canAid` / `onDelivered` -> `player-aid.md` "2. Player aid"
- **Nexerelin interop?** Reflection behind `isModEnabled("nexerelin")`. Our menu overrides yield on non-hive markets (`ThreatincNexCMD`), so Nexerelin owns those and we own hives. A besieged colony gets `$nex_npc_no_invade` and invasions are cancelled. `ThreatNexCompat.poll` -> `nexerelin.md` "2. Besieged colonies are not invaded"
- **When do omens play?** Only until any hive is discovered (`KEY_DONE` latches); clocks 20 / 25 days, range 6 ly; off with `omensEnabled` or `hiveFogOfWar` false. `ThreatOmens.active` -> `omens.md` "2. When it stops"

## Threat fleets
- **Where does the swarm's supplies income go, and why does it collapse rather than settle (hw68a peak, 2026-10-08)?** 43 hives, 28 forges, 72k made a month (forge output size-2 units x 750, +1 corrupted, +3 pristine - reconciles to the census); sustenance bill 45k of which 29k is three size-8 worlds, paid at break-even 22.5k; home navy above the patrol table 18.5-20k (27-29k FP at 0.70 a FP); fleets away 18-37k. Every forge world of size 3-7 nets positive (a plain size-5: +2,250 made, -312 sustenance, -650 garrison charge = +1,290, keeps ~1,850 FP away for good); the drag is the 17 hives of 43 WITHOUT a forge (~420/mo garrison carry each + a 5k kit, ~1.5 years before a unit). The spare was +3-6k/mo at war 3062-3185 - balanced at 43 worlds - until a campaign launch (fleets away 18k -> 34k, prepaid from the 23k stock) ran the stock to 0; then the payment order (`maintainGarrisons`: `paySupplies` fleets away -> `payNavySupplies` -> `ThreatColonyUpkeep.feed` sustenance last) starved the colonies first, sizes 179 -> 93, income 72k -> 25k in eight months, and the navy shrank (96k -> 45k) only after the forges were gone. Humans cannot spiral: vanilla feeds their colonies. -> `game-runs-2.md` 43
- **What answers a human landing on a hive?** Nothing until it falls: `IncursionManager.retaliate` strikes the winner after the fall; the system rallies only what can win (`ThreatPosture.rally`). The counter-stroke (a strike at the landing faction at once) was built and measured in hw68 - a trade, not a relief - and deleted 2026-10-08; `strikes-staged.md` 6 keeps the record. The hives' real defence was solvency: sustenance first (hw69).

- **How does the swarm decide to strike?** Once a month (`IncursionManager.tick` -> `tryStrikes`, phase >= 2), each hive system in shuffled order gets one pass: `pickStrikeStaging` (biggest colony, size >= 4, ships, >= 2 spare swarms, fuel, a working Nexus) -> `pickStrikeTarget` (weight size squared x grudge / strikeDays x stance; a dry Threat front wins outright) -> `launchStrike`. No coordination, no cap, no plan object. -> `swarm-strategy.md` section 1
- **How big is a swarm strike?** Everything above the reserve (`peekMuster`), not sized on the target; trimmed by fuel (`ThreatFuel.canPay`), supplies (`ThreatReach.canSustain`) and the bank. h53d: 23 strikes of 297-6,694 FP (mean ~2.2k), 13 of them within 4 ly. -> `swarm-strategy.md` section 1
- **Why does the swarm never take a human world (h53d)?** It scatters: each hive system spends fuel on its own monthly strike, the hive's fuel stock sits at 3-3,000 against 50-77k a month spent (up to 31 sends held a month), so no front gets follow-up waves. 11 landings, 5 beachheads overrun, 7 strata, 0 worlds in 14 months. The offence-led fix is the Hive Mind design. -> `swarm-strategy.md` sections 2-3
- **Does CONSOLIDATE stop the swarm spreading?** Not since 2026-10-04 (`stanceConsolidateSpreadShare` 1, `stanceConsolidateStrikes`); before, only new outward claims. `tryExpandInSystem` waves, matured claims (`advanceStages`) and conversions go on; the real cap is one `pickSpreadTarget` claim per tick plus `seedToColonyDays` 120 (h53d: 5 waves launched under CONSOLIDATE). -> `swarm-strategy.md` section 1
- **Are the swarm's harassment and saturation passes live?** No: both need `strikeSaturationEnabled` (false), so the frontier harassment strike is dormant. A short landing share breaks hulls into troops (`beachheadLanding`, `fabricateTroopsPerFP`: 151 FP became 1,510 troops at Salamanca). -> `swarm-strategy.md` section 1
- **How are Threat fleets composed?** `ThreatFleetComposer` re-spends vanilla's escort FP per archetype (host, vanguard, battery, tide, hunter, scout) for jobs strike, garrison, seeding, scout. `ThreatFleetComposer.pickArchetype` -> `fleet-archetypes.md` "How a fleet is composed"

## Tooling and testing

- **Can balance be tested without the game?** Yes, since 2026-10-02 (both sides built and joined; the joined war is still being fitted to pd9a): an offline simulator in `tools/warsim/` sharing pure rules with the mod (`src/threatinc/rules/`), calibrated from the run extracts and validated in three gates (swarm alone to month 36, pd7a, pd4a-pd8a). The logs cannot be replayed (runs part on any change) and lack game dates and the star map: the mod needs a `Clock:` line and a state dump first. -> `war-sim.md`
- **How many forward bases do the factions hold?** None: in pd4a-pd8a (2026-10-02) they founded 9-50 a run and lost every one, about two thirds to Threat strikes on the station and a third given up for want of a garrison; pd8a ended holding 3. The census's `bases` counts every colony with a depot, its `links` the forward bases. -> `war-sim.md` 7
- **Does the simulator's swarm match a real run?** To month 36, mostly (2026-10-02, against pd9a over 50 seeds): hives 16 real against 17 [14-19], total size 65 against 66 [57-72], first hive war day 138 against 138, first claim 368 against 369, first Seeding Swarm 489 against 490, first strike 641 against 642. Out: the Bastion comes about 180 days early, garrisons at month 36 run about 1500 FP low, and strikes run half again as often. The swarm's formulas the simulator shares are `threatinc.rules.HiveRules` / `PostureRules` / `StanceRules` / `SpreadRules` / `StrikeRules`; the planner, feed, posture reading, fog and strikes are re-implemented, so a change to those in the mod must be made in `tools/warsim` too. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 2-6
- **What does a Seeding Swarm cost the hive?** 5000 supplies (`outpostSupplies` 1500 + the spaceport and nexus kit), `outpostFuel` 800 plus about 120 fuel a light year, one spare swarm from the source system's pool, and the bank the wave's 600 FP beyond that swarm ("600 FP of hulls, 0 FP of structures": the structures are priced in supplies, so `foundingFP` bills nothing). `ThreatColonyManager.launchColonizationWave` -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 3
- **Is the simulator's human side built?** Yes, 2026-10-02 (`warsim.HumanSide`, planner only; a council run counts `councilNotModelled`). Against pd9a from war month 36 with a test-double swarm: sieges 220 of 201, kills 44 of 23, landings 109 of 41, bases founded 37 of 15, none held. Misses and their causes -> `war-sim-humans.md` 5
- **Does the joined simulator match pd9a?** Mostly (2026-10-02 round 4, 30 seeds x 114 months, 168 of 218 figures inside p10-p90). In at month 115: sieges sailed and landed, fronts overrun, strikes 189 against 163, Threat landings, beachheads overrun, hives killed, hives 41 [20-80] against 56, hunts 244 against 183, mean hive size at 7 of 10 months. Out: garrison FP from month 84 (36.6k against 65.7k), swarm supplies (94k against 16k), worlds lost 13 against 7 (needs the strike gate's defence in the dump), months in CONSOLIDATE from month 84 (36 against 53). A colony at war never falls in the simulator. `warsim check -dumps <dir> -seeds 30 -months 114` -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 9-10, `war-sim-humans.md` 6
- **How much do two real runs of one recipe differ?** A lot after mobilisation (2026-10-02, pd9a against pd10a, same settings, planner): equal to month 36 (16 hives each), then at month 96 hives 32 against 90, garrison 54k against 79k, sieges 201 by month 115 against 276 by month 96, the swarm in CONSOLIDATE 53 months against 10. Not a regression: every rule moved into `threatinc.rules` between the two was reviewed hunk by hunk as equivalent. pd10a ended (war day 3374, about month 112) at 176 hives, 45 eradicated, 19 human worlds gone, 58 forward bases founded and none held, 0 exceptions. One run is one branch; fit the simulator to the envelope of both (simulator against pd10a: 109 of 193 inside). Dumps `simdump-pd10a`, extract `ti-pd10a.txt`. -> `war-sim.md` 11
- **Why do pd9a and pd10a part (32 against 90 hives at month 96)?** The war is bistable on the swarm's stance. Both entered CONSOLIDATE at war day 1253-1254 with 5-6 of 10 systems pressed. In pd9a the Persean League mobilised first and sailed 13 sieges (4 landings) over days 1260-1440, so the stance held to day 1510 and nothing was founded; in pd10a the Hegemony mobilised first and sailed 8 (1 landing), the pressed share fell to 3 of 9 at day 1348 and the swarm sat in EXPAND for 356 days (17 to 31 hives, 10 to 16 systems). After that 5-7 pressed systems are short of half of 15-17, so twice pd9a's sieges cannot bring CONSOLIDATE back. EXPAND feeds at 0.5 and the spare supplies pay Seeding Swarms; CONSOLIDATE feeds at 0.9. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 11
- **How fast does the swarm fill a held system?** A wave per unclaimed resource planet each tick, bounded by swarms and supplies (pd10a: up to 7 seedings a tick); 0.88 of spare planets taken in systems held since month 60. `ThreatColonyManager.tryExpandInSystem` -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 11
- **Does the simulator cover both real runs?** Since round 5 (2026-10-02): hives at month 96 are 70 [30-120] against 32 and 90; inside p10-p90 pd9a 155 of 218, pd10a 130 of 193; both covered in 93 of 168 cells. Biased high on both: strikes launched, worlds lost, months in PRESS, hunts. Never covers both: swarm supplies, months in EXPAND. `warsim check -dumps A -dumps2 B -log2 ..` (`Main.both`) -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 11
- **Does `check` start the simulator in the real run's home?** Since round 21 yes, for a run dumped before the landing: `Main.check` reads the home system and the landed chain from the first dump with hives (`Start.ogSystem`, `threatinc_homeWorlds` set to what landed) and prints them; `-set warsim_checkOwnHome=true` is the old way (each seed picks a home). Inside p10-p90, own home -> real home: tr1a 50 -> 59 of 93, pd9a 57 -> 40 of 90, pd10a 69 -> 63 of 90 - the old bands were wide with the homes the seeds drew. From Gamma Vucub-Came the simulator's swarm outgrows pd9a from month 60 (59 hives against 30 at month 84) and covers pd10a. Compare a `check` figure only with one taken the same way. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 17
- **Where are the real runs' dumps?** `tools/warsim/validation/`: machine-local since 2026-10-11 (gitignored and purged from history, 348 MB of 400; the user: logs stay local, never commit them); pd9a (117 months), pd10a (115), tr1a (116: run tr1, a three-planet home, council on, to month 113), hw4a (117: run hw4, the four-world default, council on, to month 115), hw3a (121: three worlds on hw4's sector, to month 120), hw4b (114: hw4 again on the same save, the first with `gate` in the dumps). A `check` of the last four needs `-set threatinc_warCouncil=true` and, for the event rows, `-log <ti-<run>.txt>` (machine-local, the docked laptop's `%TEMP%\threatinc-tests`). What each real run showed: `war-sim-real-runs.md`.
- **Do the game's humans do what the simulator's do?** Within reach since round 24 (2026-10-02). The first reading of tr1 - "a fifth as effective, one siege in seven lands" - was the instrument: `check` counted the STARVE plays' saturation expeditions as sieges and left the HAMMER plays' forces out of the hunts (tr1 by month 108: 8 sieges, 4 landed, 36 hunts), the simulator's saturation cost a tenth of the game's, and its swarm had three faults. Now, real | simulator at month 108: hives killed hw4 1 | 4 [2-10], tr1 3 | 9.5 [3-20], hw3 2 | 7 [1-22]; sieges landed 5 | 5, 4 | 15, 4 | 12.5; figures inside p10-p90 of 330: 223 / 247 / 216. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 1-3
- **Does a four-world home play harder than a three-world one in the game?** Not that four real runs can show. Hives at month 108: tr1 (three worlds) 131, hw3 (three, on hw4's sector) 113, hw4 (four) 152, hw4b (four, hw4's own save again) 209 - the two four-world runs differ by more than either does from hw3; the fourth world shows for two years (4 / 8 / 17 hives at months 12 / 24 / 36 against 3 / 3 / 14) and is gone by the third. The simulator's 3 -> 4 gap stays unconfirmed. -> `war-sim-real-runs-oct2.md` 3-5
- **What did the first run on the four-world default show (hw4, 115 months, council on, `strikeMinSize` 4)?** The Threat ahead, the humans standing: 171 hives, 55 of 59 human worlds left, 1 hive killed, 5 of 12 sieges landed (29 saturation expeditions besides), 43 of 57 forward bases destroyed by month 108. Its twin hw4b ended at 253 hives, 10 worlds lost and no hive killed. No mod exception in either. -> `war-sim-real-runs-oct2.md` 3, 5
- **What stops the human factions attacking more (hw5e-hw5g, 2026-10-04)?** Rules, not means: they earn 6.4-7.5M supplies and 12-13M fuel in the war and hold 12-17k marines each, and the landing sieges draw 0.2-0.4% of the supplies and under 1% of the fuel (12-22 sail in ten years, 8-14 land). The chain: the band weighs colony sizes against hive sizes, never fleets or stock, so 57-84% of faction-months read outmatched and draw Hold (attacks nothing, 40-45%) or Starve (42-56%); Starve may not invade until every Nexus is down; its bombing expedition waits on the whole system's bombing fuel (312-416k) and, sailing alone, is called off 29-37 times; a hammer pays a hunting force 17-30x the swarm reported before its siege, which then fails all-or-nothing on supplies (3-10 a run); a bombing expedition books the whole system (7-12 hammers end on it); one siege makes one landing. Supplies are the one shortage and the hunts cause it (upkeep 37-54% of income, 301-416 hunt orders stood down unpaid). `ThreatWarCouncil.assess`, `CouncilRules.scores`, `ThreatPlays.plan` / `toMuster` / `strike` / `saturate` -> `game-runs-humans.md` 2
- **Why does the council hold the humans back - was a tactical benefit assumed (2026-10-04)?** The layer was built to replace sieges sized to reported FP with committed plays (user, 2026-10-01), not to make the humans cautious. The restraint is in its tables, written on assumptions no run tested: "outmatched does not invade" (Hold; the band weighs colony sizes against hive sizes because fleet FP was ruled out as a measure), "bomb first, the landing is cheaper" (Starve), "one play at a time" (`majorLimit`, a simulator result). Only the massed escort has a measured benefit (landing sieges called off 0-1 times a run against 29-37 for the bombing expeditions that sail alone), and it is 17-30x the reported swarm. The one run with the council off (pd7a) killed 27 hives against 1-2; "every strategy sieges" was proposed then (`war-council-runs.md` 4, option A) and dropped on the simulator's round 13 alone, never run in the game. -> `game-runs-humans.md` 2, "Why each link is there"
- **What do the real council's sieges wait for?** Fuel for a saturation: 577 of tr1's 587 postponements are the STARVE play's saturation expedition (`ThreatPlays.saturate`), which razes every hive of the play in the system and has each one's whole saturation fuel set aside (`IncursionManager.expeditionFuel` sums `razingFuel` over the raze set): a median 240k fuel, ten times the passage. 24 sailed all the same, 123 times "no saturation expedition the pools pay"; the other 10 postponements were short of supplies. The simulator prices it the same since round 23 (`warsim_saturationFuelSize2` 3000 x size squared a world, `HumanCouncil.saturate`; 0 is the old one-world stay). -> `war-sim-real-runs-oct2.md` 1
- **How often does the game call a guard against a seen strike?** tr1, months 50-113: 73 calls to a front link, 19 to a rear link, 6 guards sent behind the front, 11 turned back on the way home; 21 links faced a strike with no guard called ("cannot pay the voyage"). About 1.4 calls a war-month against 2.5 under the simulator's `warsim_reliefToBesiegers`; 35 of 50 links fell regardless. `tools/test-harness/fastforward/guard-digest.ps1` -> `war-sim-real-runs-oct2.md` 1
- **What do `check`'s event rows count?** The same thing on both sides since round 23 (`Main.EVENTS`): `siegesSailed` the expedition draws with `razing 0)`, `saturationsSailed` the STARVE expeditions, `huntsSailed` bounty hunts plus play forces, `plays.<TYPE>`, `playsEnded.<outcome>`, `siege.<end>`, `squadronsArrived`, `reinforcementsSent` (the posture's transfers). A row read before that is not comparable. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 1
- **Does the simulator's strike gate read the orbit?** Since 2026-10-02 when the dump has it: the monthly dump carries `gate` (`IncursionManager.liveTargetDefence`) for every human world (`ThreatSimDump.worlds`, runs from hw4b on), `Start` reads it into `World.gate`, `SwarmOps.defenceOf` prefers it; `warsim_orbitGate=false` reads the ground figure. New-game start with the figures: `tools/warsim/start/pd9a-newgame-gate`. By itself it moves nothing (worlds lost 13 -> 13). -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 2
- **What did round 24 fix in the simulator's swarm?** Three faults, each behind a switch (`SwarmKnobs`): a landing's fate was decided before the struck faction mobilised, so each faction's first invaded world always fell (`warsim_landingRace`; worlds lost 16 -> 7); the Path was struck as a faction that never mobilises (`warsim_pathNeverMobilises`; the game excludes pirates alone); a strike sailed with whatever part of the muster the fuel paid (`warsim_strikeWholeMuster=false`; the game strikes with the whole muster or not at all - strikes broken off 38 -> 6, bases destroyed 22 -> 49). `run -home <system id>` starts one seed in a real run's home. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 3
- **Where does a new session start, and how far is the simulator to be trusted?** `handover-2026-10-02-night.md`: its section 2 lists `check`'s rows by fit over hw4a, hw4b, hw4c (about 72% of figures inside p10-p90, where a perfect model would score about 80%) - in: the swarm's economy, the humans' sieges, hunts and plays; out: strike and landing volume (early by a few months, too many late), forward bases held after month 60, months in a stance. `fitrows.pl` remakes the table.
- **What is still out between simulator and game after round 26?** Late-war strikes and landings (inside to month 60; after month 84 strikes about 1.6 times and landings 2.5 times the game's: hw4 month 108 138 | 218, 52 | 136), a supplies stock that piles up late (hw4 12k | 703k), worlds lost on hw4 (2 | 10), months in CONSOLIDATE (game 0-4, simulator 9-27). `check` has 1,082 of 1,472 figures of the four runs inside p10-p90. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 8
- **Did the sweep fix hold in the game (run hw4c, 109 months)?** Yes: five factions mobilised within 60 days of the first sweeping strike (day 1,320-1,380), and no Threat landing came down on a world whose owner was not at war. It does not save the world the mobilising strike lands on: the Diktat, first struck on day 3,000, mobilised that day and lost Sindria (size 7) to 1,419 troops landed within the month. Month 108: 179 hives, 8 worlds lost, 0 hives killed - between hw4 and hw4b, inside their noise. -> `war-sim-real-runs-oct2.md` 6
- **Would mobilising every faction when the swarm reaches phase 3 help?** Simulator trial `warsim_mobiliseAtPhase=3` (60 seeds, month 104): human worlds lost 9 -> 5 (clear), swarm landings 155 -> 74 (clear), sieges sailed 9 -> 35.5 (clear), hives 139 -> 103, two-sided outcomes 28% -> 83%, humans' score 25 -> 35 and its p10 15 -> 25; neither side's score clear of the noise. At phase 2 the swarm is beaten in one seed in eight and the dead years double. BUILT 2026-10-02 (the user's yes), knob `threatinc_mobiliseAtPhase` 3. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 9, 11
- **Does a strike's fleet stay over its landing?** Yes, spawned or not, since 2026-10-02 late: a spawned strike's fleets go on `ThreatSwarmDefend`; an unspawned one builds its first fleet over the landing (`ThreatStrikeFGI.guardUnspawned`, its share of the ledger weighted by `estimateFP` since 2026-10-03) and re-banks the rest. Run hw4d: 196 guards, about 0.2 of each strike's FP. The guard bombards, then breaks hulls into troops while its front cannot hold (`fabricateTroops`, 10 troops a point then; 5 since 2026-10-03): 41,718 troops from 4,153 FP in hw4d against 7,296 in hw4c. Simulator: `warsim_strikeDefends` true, `warsim_guardSwarmsPerFleet` 1.42, `warsim_guardFeedsFront` true (`SwarmOps.feed`). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md`
- **Why did the humans collapse in hw4d (2026-10-03)?** The guards. Fronts the guards feed outlast the defenders' marine reserves (Persean 19k to 3k), then nothing overruns them: 38 colonies fell after day 3,000, guarded landings went from 16 of 64 taken to 30 of 41. The break-up rate is not the lever (simulator: 5 or 2.5 troops a point both lose 39 worlds); guards that do not feed their fronts lose 10. Waits on the user. -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 7, `war-sim-calibration-r29.md`
- **Did the fuel fix stop the collapse (hw4e, 2026-10-03)?** It delayed it a year: 3 worlds lost at month 108 (hw4d 25), 37 at 126 (51 at 125), the same guards' troops outlasting the marines (103k to 14k). The delay was the swarm's stance: short of its garrison want it held CONSOLIDATE 13 months by month 72 (simulator 4.6), striking only forward bases (42 of 72 strikes in months 60-84). -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 8
- **What does hull break-up do to the NPC war (game runs, 2026-10-03)?** Worlds lost by month 125-134 on the hw4 sector: as built 51 (hw4d), 37 (hw4e), 37 (hw4j, only 10 by month 120) and 43 by month 132 (hw4l, 21 at month 120); `threatinc_fabricateEnabled` off 26 (hw4f) and 42 (hw4i); `threatinc_fabricateDefendEnabled` off, beachheads still break up, 22 with 36 left (hw4g) 33 (hw4h, which held to 7 lost at month 120 and then lost 26) and 26 by month 127 (hw4k, 11 at month 120). The Defend fleets' break-up (4,996 FP into 50,089 troops in hw4e) is what lets fronts outlast the marines. Counted from the logs, by month 126: as built 52, 39, 21, 40 (median 39.5); Defend off 21, 20, 26; none 21, 42 - a lean toward Defend off, not proof. -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 9-15
- **What does 1 troop a broken-up FP do (`threatinc_fabricateTroopsPerFP` 1, guards and beachheads)?** Simulator (2026-10-03 morning, 30 seeds on the hw4 sector, `chk-hw4*-tp1`): worlds lost 19 / 35 / 43 / 48 at months 108 / 120 / 126 / 132 against 32.5 / 46 / 50 / 53 at 10; the defenders keep 57k marines at month 120 against 12k; fronts are overrun 106 times by month 120 against 62, so the swarm lands 159 times against 121. The simulator over-kills unfed fronts (it put Defend off at about 40 where the game lost 22 and 33). Game run hw4m (user asked 2026-10-03): 12 lost by month 120, 26 by 126, 33 at 132 - in the Defend-off band (21, 20, 26 at month 126) against as built's 52, 39, 21, 40; the swarm broke up 28,260 FP of hulls for about half the troops and 72 strikes held back. -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 16
- **And 5 troops a broken-up FP?** Simulator (2026-10-03, `chk-hw4*-tp5`): nearly as built - worlds lost 30-31 / 44-45 / 52.5 at months 108 / 120 / 132 against 32.5-33 / 45.5-46 / 53 at 10, marines 16.6k at month 120 against 12k. A guard tops its front up to the hold line x `fabricateHoldMargin` whatever the ratio, so a lower ratio mostly costs it more hulls (hw4m at 1: 15,835 FP for 15,835 troops; hw4l at 10: 2,867 FP for 28,670); the front goes short only when the guard runs dry, which at 5 is rarer. Game run hw4n (5 lost by month 126) does not test it: its swarm stalled on fuel at month 60-72 (21 hives against 32-39) and made no landing for two years. Nor does hw4o (5 and the fuel-tight plant): the humans won the early war, eradicating seven hives by month 85 (Alpha Laphirial VI among them), and lost 1 world in 140 months. hw4p does (defaults of 3adaa16): 35 worlds lost by month 126, inside the as-built range (52, 39, 21, 40), not the Defend-off band (21, 20, 26). Its guards broke up 8,959 FP into 44,795 troops (at 10: 2,867-4,754 FP into 28,670-47,540). -> `war-sim-real-runs.md` 17-19
- **What does the simulator's base run since 2026-10-03?** 5 troops a broken-up FP and the fuel-tight plant (`investFuelWhenTight`, from two plants: `investFuelMinPlants` 2), both on by default. A check against the as-built runs (hw4d-k, 10 a point, no fuel-tight step) needs `threatinc_fabricateTroopsPerFP=10;threatinc_investFuelWhenTight=false` in `-Extra`; that reproduces the 2,493 inside. The one-plant version cut forges at month 36 (median 4.5 to 4) and lifted p10 hives at month 120 by 20-30; the two-plant rule is as without the step (2,477 inside at 5 against 2,471), the simulator having no hw4n stall to cure. -> `hive-reach-and-stock.md` "Idle stock"
- **Can the simulator judge the break-up lever?** No (2026-10-03): it lands about twice the game's beachheads and overruns three times as many (its colonies counter-attack on no stability and neither side gains veterancy, `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Fronts, the gate and the runaway swarm"), and on the same setting the game itself lost 22 (hw4g) and 33 (hw4h); for hw4g's settings it loses 50.5 worlds against the game's 22 and lands 246 times against 109. Its strikes muster 1.5-2x the game's swarms late in the war (same muster rules; the state differs, not traced) and it lands on 84% of its colony strikes against the game's about half. Since r42 (brace, pace) as built and no break-up sit on the game; Defend off is still out because its fronts die young: with no guard feeding them the simulator's counter-attack ratio drifts (p90 3.65 against the game's 2.02 on every setting), which three missing game rules fix on seed 3 (`warsim_wearClock`, `warsim_reinforceDilutes`, veterancy; worlds lost 53 -> 23 against 19). On the seven-run check they bring Defend off to the game per landing but make the as-built fronts too safe (overruns 23.5 against 46), costing 99 cells inside, so they stay off. Use the game runs. -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Why the Defend-off fronts died young", `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Beachheads and landing troops"
- **Does the swarm's guard stay until its front ends?** Mostly not (2026-10-03, `guardend.pl` / `standdown.pl` on hw4d-k): 108-157 guards a run (of 204-222) stand down with their front still standing - relief fleets and patrols wear them in vanilla battles, on screen or off, and `ThreatSwarmDefend.tick` sends a guard home below `defendMinStrength` 0.33 of its arrival FP unless it has started breaking hulls (`defendCommitted`). Of the fronts later overrun, 38 of 57 (hw4j) and 44 of 65 (hw4k) had lost their guard over a month before. The simulator's guards stood to the end: `warsim_reliefFights` + `warsim_guardStandDown` (`SwarmOps.standsDown`) build it, off: overruns come nearer the game's but the seven-run check keeps 2,379-2,452 inside against base's 2,493 (`warsim_reliefTakesGuard`, one battle takes an uncommitted guard: 2,390). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "The guards go home"
- **What troop count does a swarm beachhead need, and when is it sized?** `ThreatGroundFronts.beachheadTroops` against the world's counter-attack after bombardment (`readyToLand` waits for `orbitSpent`): big garrisons are down to 0.2-0.4 of pre-war when the strike lands. A strike lands max(world share, need) out of what is aboard (`availableLanding`); in practice the first world takes the whole pool (median 0 left aboard). Simulator: `warsim_landingSuppress` 0.65, `warsim_strikeSweep` (off). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md`
- **What makes the game's strike gate grow over a war?** Human guards in the system, nothing else (2026-10-03, hw4d/f/g dumps): a world with no GUARD/HUNT/MUSTER fleet arrived in its system kept its day-5 gate to day 3,600 (median ratio 0.9-1.1); one with a guard read 1.1-1.5 units per guard FP above it, every world of the system alike. Simulator: `warsim_gateSystemGuards` / `warsim_guardUnitsPerFP` built, off (moves landings under 3%). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Fronts, the gate and the runaway swarm"
- **How often does a colony counter-attack a Threat front?** (Superseded 2026-10-23 by the line, `ground-war-fronts.md` "The line"; the cadence below is history.) Every `frontCounterAttackDays` 40 / `colonyCounterAttackMilitaryMult` 1.5 with a military command / tempo (ca over the front's strength, clamped 1/3-3), floored at `frontOverrunMusterDays` when it would overrun, and never while dug in: `Theatre.COLONY.counterAttackInterval` (the stability divisor was removed 2026-10-17; the figures below are from before that). In the hw4 runs the first came 54-60 days after the landing, later ones every 25-28; a battering costs the front 2-4% of its troops (`frontCounterAttackLossFraction` 0.10 x `ThreatMarineXP.frontLossMult`), and about one counter-attack in ten overruns. The simulator had no stability and no veterancy: its first came at 23 days, its fronts died twice as fast. `warsim_counterAttackPace` 0.68 / `warsim_firstCounterAttackPace` 0.55 are on since 2026-10-03 (check 1,718 -> 1,735 inside over hw4d-h); `warsim_veterancy` is built, off: it brings the simulator's front lives to the game's (164 days against 168) but removes its slow-swarm seeds. With the brace fix (`warsim_braceAfterAttrition`) in, it costs 108 cells inside (1,794 -> 1,686) for distances a little better. A game front's level climbs about 0.12 a counter-attack, so its batterings shrink (Salamanca, hw4g: 100 marines, then 83, 69 ... 40). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Fronts, the gate and the runaway swarm"
- **Does the game relieve invaded colonies?** Yes, every NPC faction at war (`ThreatFleetOrders.planRelief`): a task force sized to the swarm's guard there x `reliefOrbitMargin` (none with no swarm over the world, 2026-10-24), from the nearest base that can provision it - 293-566 fleets a run, 126k-210k FP on the hw4 sector. Simulator: `warsim_reliefToInvaded`, off (neutral alone). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Relief to invaded colonies"
- **Does a human fleet over its own invaded world hurt the Threat army?** Yes, since 2026-10-03 (the user asked why breaking up hulls was no trade-off). Before, nothing did: `ThreatGroundFronts.tickSwarmBombard` ran only for a non-Threat front on a hive, and relief went home after `guardDays` 90. Now relief stays until the army is gone (`threatinc_reliefStays`; `reliefDone`: front gone, or the fleet worn below `defendMinStrength` and outweighed by `siegeBreakOffRatio`). While the defenders are losing and no Threat fleet is over the world, it bombards the army on the swarm's formula (`tickReliefBombard`, `threatinc_reliefBombardPer30Days` 0.60), paying fuel only above its passage home (`reliefFuelHome`). Holding the orbit already blocked landings, guard bombardment and break-up (`orbitContestedFor`). In game (hw4q, against hw4p): relief stayed a median 134 days (p90 227), bombarded on 2,522 relief-days for about 12,900 troops and 73,500 fuel, and bombarded out 2 landings; the guards broke up half as much (19k troops against 38k); worlds lost by month 126 34 (35), inside the runs' spread. Relief never came to 11 of the 30 worlds taken (14 of 32 in hw4p): `no base in reach can provision it`, a siege's range, removed for relief the same day (`pickReliefBase`). `guardwin.pl` -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Relief"
- **Does relief that holds and bombards help the humans (simulator, 2026-10-03)?** A little. Against relief that goes home after 90 days, it takes 0-4 off worlds lost at months 120-132 (hw4p 47.5 -> 44 at month 132), with hives 3-8% fewer, on all three starts but inside the seeds' spread. The bombardment kills 0.7-2k troops in a war where the guards break up 29-40k, and holds fire once the defenders are winning, so raising `reliefBombardPer30Days` to 2 or 6 changes nothing (it stays 0.60). Relief at all does more (worlds lost 43 -> 32 at month 120), because the swarm's guard cannot break up hulls while relief contests the orbit, a game rule the simulator lacked until now (`warsim_feedNeedsOrbit`). The relief rules stay off by default. The game bombards far more (hw4q: 12,900 troops) because the simulator's relief fights the guard only on arrival. `SwarmOps.reliefBombard`, `warsim_reliefFightDays` -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Relief that holds and bombards"
- **Does more supplies income help the humans (simulator, 2026-10-04)?** Strongly. On hw4r, doubling it (`warsim_suppliesAccrualMult` 2) keeps 27.5 worlds at month 120 against 15.5, and the check fits the game better (375 of 538 against 334). x4 adds little more, because the supplies pile up unspent. It cannot choose `reserveWartimeSuppliesShare`: it banks by `HumanFit` (fitted to the surplus rule) and its swarm spends 4-5 months in CONSOLIDATE where the supplied game runs spent 24-72, so it grows twice the hives. -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Supplies income", `war-sim-real-runs.md` 22
- **What becomes of a game strike on a colony?** hw4g's 224: a new front 36%, an existing front reinforced 33%, fought and no landing 8%, no off-screen fight logged 17%, outweighed 5% (`life2.pl` in `%TEMP%\threatinc-tests`). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Fronts, the gate and the runaway swarm"
- **Why does one game swarm stall and another run away on the same setting?** The invest rule: a size-3+ world builds a forge only while the supply stock pays a forge and a founding kit besides. hw4g's stock never got there (3-15k from month 48), so no invests after month 84, forges stuck at 21-24 and size-4 hives in 10-11 systems; hw4h's did (88 invests in months 84-120, forges 42 -> 185, 43 staging systems). Strikes stage only from a size-4 hive, so this sets the strike count. -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "Why hw4g's swarm stalled"
- **Why does a fresh game beachhead survive its first counter-attack?** It is sized to (`beachheadTroops`: the counter-attack x `siegeBeachheadMargin` 1.25 / (0.75 x 2)), and a front that would be overrun if caught pushing braces, asked after the day's attrition (`shouldBrace`). hw4g's first counter-attacks overran 1 of 85, hw4h's 1 of 74. The simulator asked before the attrition and lost 24 of 138; `warsim_braceAfterAttrition` (on since 2026-10-03 05:00) fixes the order: check 1,735 -> 1,794 inside. -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md` "The brace came a day late" **2026-10-18: the line is REPEL** - `beachheadRepels` draws the beachhead at 1:1 x the margin (`BattleRules.beachheadOdds`), ~2x the marines; and `frontBraceWhenBeaten` braces any front the blow would beat, ground held or not.
- **How strong is a colony on the ground?** Vanilla's ground defence (`colonyGarrison`, `getDefenderStr(market, true)`) plus armed reserve marines x `reserveDefenseMult`; the dumps' `defence` is that sum (Ancyra 305, Sindria 3,120). The simulator used 15 a size until 2026-10-03 (`World.garrison`). -> `war-sim-calibration-r29.md`
- **How many fuel plants does the swarm keep?** 12-15% of its hives, nearly all on size 5 and up (month 108: hw4 18 of 152, hw4b 23 of 209, hw3 14 of 113, tr1 20 of 131), against forges on 55-69%; hw4's planner built 26 in 115 months (1 first, 5 `fuel short`, 20 `spare`). `check` rows `fuelPlants`, `forges`, `swarmFuelPerMonth`; `hivemix.pl`. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 7
- **Does a strike that waits on fuel book fuel demand?** No. `IncursionManager.pickStrikeTarget` passes over a world the whole muster's passage is not paid for, so `launchStrike` sees only affordable targets and its `ThreatFuel.held("strike from ..")` is not reached (hw4: none in 115 months; 175 Seeding Swarm holds, 136 raider, 24 scout, 1 reinforcement). The planner (`ThreatFuel.runsDry`) therefore builds no fuel plant for waiting strikes. Booking it in the simulator (its old behaviour) doubles the plants and adds half again to the strikes without moving the swarm's own score - offered to the user, not applied. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 7
- **What did rounds 25-26 fix in the simulator's swarm?** Four things, each behind a switch (`SwarmKnobs`): the posture's transfers run on the game's half-day loop (`warsim_postureLoop`); a strike goes home after its landing pass (`warsim_strikeDefends` false: hives 86 -> 140, held of want 0.8 -> 1.0); a held send books its unpaid bill once a `SHORT_DAYS` a source and a waiting muster books nothing (`warsim_holdsBookMonthly`: fuel plants 44 -> 19, fuel spent 5.7M -> 3.5M, strikes 385 -> 255). -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 6, 7
- **What does a Threat landing come to?** Over tr1, hw4, hw3 and hw4b: 17 of 18 on pirate worlds took the world; on a faction's or the Path's world 8 of 199 did where the owner was at war (the garrison overruns the beachhead: 173 overrun; five of the eight were hw4b's last year, a 250-hive swarm) and 5 of 5 where it was not - the swept-world bug, fixed 2026-10-02 (`IncursionManager.onStrikeDetected`). Landings are beachhead-sized (300 troops the commonest), 21-38 reinforcing passes a run against 65-85 landings. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 4
- **Where does the swarm's fuel go?** About half to its own garrison transfers (`Posture: X sent N FP to Y`): 1,100-1,900 sends a year by years 8-9, 200-320k FP shipped against a 100-240k garrison. Relay, not ping-pong: 11-14% returns to its sender, 62-69% passes through hives that both receive and send - the hives a human force is staged against gather a garrison and disperse it when the want falls (`ThreatPosture.poll`). So the first strike waits for fuel until days 1,140-1,410 though phase 2 comes on day 630-750 and phase 3 on day 1,050-1,110. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 4
- **Simulator trials of 2026-10-02 night that were not built?** `warsim_saturateAffordable` (raze the worlds of a play the pools pay, not all or none): noise on the humans' score. `warsim_coloniesFall=true` with the landing day fixed: worlds lost 7 -> 29.5, the game's garrisons overrun beachheads instead. Partial strike musters: worse fit on all three runs. -> `war-sim-calibration.md` 1, 3
- **Is the council in the simulator?** Yes, since 2026-10-02: `warsim.HumanCouncil` mirrors `ThreatWarCouncil` / `ThreatPlays`, switched by `threatinc_warCouncil`; `check` forces the planner (pd9a and pd10a are planner runs); counters `council.months.*`, `plays.<TYPE>.<outcome>`. After round 7 (coalition pairs as the real pictures name them, per-observer intel as `ThreatIntel.advanceDay`, the relief-owed gate, Threat fronts read from `Swarm.landings`) it matches the real council runs (pd4a, 5a, 6a, 8a; pd7a was a planner run): hammers 6 against 7-16, starves 12.5 against 13-25, sieges plus saturations 17.5 against 15-29, hive kills 2 against 0-2, outmatched months 96% against 82-90%, Hold/Starve 51/46% against 52-59/36-46%. Off: hives 135 against 152-236, forward bases founded 70 and held 11.5 against 9-50 and 0-3; relief owed is overstated (the simulator never sends relief). -> `war-sim-humans.md` 7
- **How lively is the war by the user's measures?** Not (simulator, 30 seeds, 2026-10-02): the swarm gains ground every year under planner and council alike (`swings` 0), `reversals` on enemy ground only about 5 a run, `contested` systems 15-18, `deadYears` 0.6 from mobilisation; no seed decided, none a stalemate. Measures: `Main.measures`, `Sim.hands` (`turnover`, `swings`, `reversals`, `contested`, `deadYears`, `decided`); classes `Main.outcome` still key on `swings`. -> `war-sim.md` 7
- **What did the first simulator experiments show (2026-10-02)?** Council baseline from a new game, 104 months: hives 135, kills 2, bases held 11.5, scores 875 / 11.5. No lever moved `reversals`, `contested` or `deadYears` clear of the seed noise. The swarm's economy levers only shrink it and turnover falls with it: Seeding Swarm price x2 (`warsim_seedPriceMult`) hives 59 and threatScore 492, the strongest brake; CONSOLIDATE at 0.25 pressed hives 69; EXPAND feed 0.7 hives 71. Two major plays at once (`warsim_councilMajorPlays`) doubled hammers (6 to 16) with no extra kill: the council's bottleneck is what a siege achieves, not how many it starts. The planner alone moves kills (2 to 23) and costs every forward base. Mid-war (month 114 start, 48 months) the council killed 0 and sieged 0.5 times; only the seed price was clear there. -> `war-sim-humans.md` 7
- **Does ground that can change hands twice make the war back-and-forth?** No (simulator round 8, 2026-10-02, 30 seeds; switches `warsim_basesHold` and `warsim_coloniesFall`, off by default, no mod change). Bases that fall only to a 30-day station siege (`SwarmOps.stationSiegeDay`) move nothing: the relief `HumanBases.garrison` sends is sized to the wanted guard, never to the besiegers, so it is sunk. Colonies at war that fight the front engine (`SwarmOps.threatFrontDay`, `colonyDefence` 15 a size plus marines - a guess) hand the swarm 23-30 colonies a run and nobody takes them back; mid-war `reversals` fall to 0. What is missing is an answer in the same place: relief sized to the enemy at the base, and a counter-siege of a freshly conquered world - a reactive, place-bound response neither the planner nor the council has. Reach and tempo are not the blocker. Back-and-forth class also from `reversals` >= 1 a year (`Main.BACK_AND_FORTH_REVERSALS_PER_YEAR`); the baseline mid-war council reaches it in 27% of seeds. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 12
- **How much do the sides destroy, per year since first mobilisation?** Real planner runs: pd9a swarm 1.9 (0.5 worlds, 1.4 bases) against humans 3.6 hives, pd10a 9.4 (2.4 / 7.0) against 7.3. Simulator planner 4.2 (1.7 / 2.5) against 3.5 [2.5-4.4] - between the two real runs on every rate. Simulator council 8-10 against 0-0.3: one-sided in 47% of seeds from a new game and 93% mid-war, matching the real council runs' 0-2 hives killed a run. Measures `threatKills` (`.worlds`, `.bases`), `humanKills`, `mutual` = the smaller; classes both sides (mutual >= 1 a year), one-sided, quiet. `Main.measures`, `Main.outcome`, `Main.check` -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 14
- **Which lever raises mutual destruction?** None on the swarm's side (simulator round 11, 2026-10-02, 34 cells x 30 seeds): `mutual` is bounded by `humanKills` in every cell, so the class is set by how many hives the humans kill - council 0-0.3 a year, planner 1.8-3.9. CONSOLIDATE at 0.25 clearly lowers the planner's kills (3.9 to 2.0); colonies that fall (B) only raise the swarm's rate. The lever is the council's siege rate and what its sieges achieve. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 14
- **What did removing radar do (simulator round 12, 2026-10-02)?** The swarm destroys a third fewer forward bases (7.3 to 5.1 a year from a new game, 8 to 4.9 mid-war), the humans scout three times as much (561 to 1,611 sailed) and kill fewer hives (planner 3.3 to 3.0 new game, 1.8 to 1.0 mid-war), so more hives stand (planner 90 to 129).
