Fidelity drift against the radar-era runs: pd9a 130 to 115 of 218 inside, pd10a 130 to 143. `HumanIntel.sweep`, `SwarmOps.radar`; `warsim_radarLY` / `warsim_swarmRadarLY` restore the old behaviour for comparison. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 15
- **Did planner sizing for council plays change anything (round 12)?** Not measurably: the council sails only 3-4 play sieges a run (relief owed pauses its plays about 70% of faction-days - overstated in the simulator per round 7 - and recon plays outnumber hammers 30:1), so sizing cannot matter until it sails more. `warsim_councilPlannerSizing` -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 15
- **Which of the council doc's ideas work (round 13, 2026-10-02)?** Only two raise the humans' hive kills from 0: option B, major plays scaling with siege capacity (`warsim_councilMajorPerFP` 3000: 0.9 a year, sieges sailed 2.5 to 21.5, bases intact) and lifting the relief pause (1.0 a year, an upper bound). Option A, every strategy sieges, burns the paying bases (13 to 3.5) for 0.4; A+B+C reach mutual 1.9 at the cost of the humans' score (bases 2). Option C, hammer shares, the muster floor, learning, temperature and personalities move nothing clear of noise. Feints drew the swarm about 20% of the time and the strike's landing did not depend on it. Hive Mind decision 5 as worded (CONSOLIDATE on losses) gives more CONSOLIDATE, not less. Recommended for the mod: B, and not pausing a play whose forces are already mustered. -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 8
- **How many major plays may a faction run?** No limit since 2026-10-04 (the user's "no artificial constraints"): `majorLimit`, `siegeCapacityFP` and `councilMajorPlayFP` are removed; one strike a system (`ThreatPlays.strikingAt`), which sails a siege a world (`besiege`). -> `war-council-invasions.md` 1. Before: since 2026-10-02 `ThreatPlays.majorLimit`: max(1, siege capacity / `councilMajorPlayFP` 3000), capacity = `ThreatPlays.siegeCapacityFP(faction, picture)` (each base of the picture's clusters against its nearest, via `ThreatPosture.siegeCapacityFP`); a partner joins a joint play while under its own limit. Replaces the one-major-play rule (`war-council-runs.md` 5 option B, chosen in the simulator). -> `war-council.md` 16
- **Does relief owed pause a play under way?** No, since 2026-10-02: `ThreatPlays.pausable` holds only PREPARE, a MUSTER below `councilMusterFloor` of its planned FP, and a BOMB/WATCH with no squadron out and no siege sailed; STRIKE, EXPLOIT and WITHDRAW run on. -> `war-council.md` 16
- **Why did the council's hammers send no siege in the 2026-10-02 check?** Both reached STRIKE with report-sized needs (1,400 and 740 FP over the strongest world) and logged "no siege paid ... the hunts go alone": the pools could not fund a report-sized siege; every STARVE found no saturation expedition the pools pay. The plays reach sizing; paying is the brake. Also: short checks need Fleets Ignore You ON for the run (a Threat fleet caught the player's fleet and opened the encounter dialog) and OFF after. -> `war-council.md` 16
- **Why do council hammers send no siege (simulator round 14, 2026-10-02)?** Supplies, not FP or fuel: an unpaid report-sized siege is short about 3,300 supplies against about 2,300 callable at the base (10.5 of 14.5 unpaid short of supplies, 2.5 fuel, 1 marines; fuel callable 24,500). Where a faction's supplies go in a year (new game, income 183k): forward-base links 60k, guard upkeep 46k, fleet upkeep 44k, colony size upkeep 27k, guard voyages 8.6k, saturation 8.3k, hunts 13k, squadrons 5.9k, scouts 3.5k, play sieges 2.5k - 1-2%. The forward-base line spends first and takes about two thirds; the pools are not short, the allocation is. `HumanPools.pay(.., what)` ledger -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 9
- **Which levers free supplies for sieges (round 14)?** Letting the coalition's bases pay a siege raised paid hammers 27 to 36.5 a run (not clear of noise at 30 seeds); accrual x1.5 and half guard upkeep buy forward bases (held 12 to 17.5), not sieges; the orbit margin at 1.0 pays 32.5 but kills no more; holding hunts for the siege helps nothing (hunts are 4% of supplies). With the mod's two new rules mirrored (`HumanCouncil.pausable`, `siegeCapacity`) the council kills 1.0 hives a year from a new game (0 before), 0 mid-war; seeds are bimodal (hives 56-180). -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 9
- **Does reserving supplies for the siege pay more council sieges (round 15, 2026-10-02)?** No. Ranked by humanScore (bases held + hives killed, kill weight 0/1/2 all agree): first call for the staging siege, 25% and 50% standing reserves, a reserve by strategy, link caps of 2 and 4, and links waiting on an unpaid siege - none beats the base (21); reserves cut paid hammers 27 to 16-20 because forward bases are the hammers' staging bases (`HumanCouncil.startHammer` / `baseOf`); link caps lose bases for nothing (cap 2: 28 founded / 6 held, clear). The unpaid hammers are not short because links spent first: with provisions reserved the staging siege was affordable 80% of staging days; the shortfall arises at `strike`. -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 10
- **What is an unpaid council hammer short of (round 16, 2026-10-02)?** Supplies for the trip, by 7x not a margin: a report-sized siege needs ~1,790 FP and the pools provision 256 (14%); the want is ~77 supplies a point (`ReachRules.siegeSuppliesPerPoint`: 30 hull deposit + 25 FP x 0.94/month x `siegeTripDays`/30, the trip two thirds of it) against ~2,300 callable. The mod's gate (`IncursionManager.siegeCanPay`) prices ~117 a point (`siegeStayDays` on the slowest world + razing, full orbit, fuel 4x) and in pd10a postponed 12,606 times against 423 draws. Partial sailing never fires, billing the trip out moves nothing, every depot is already called on. -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 11
- **Does relief sized to the besiegers help the humans (round 16)?** It halves forward bases destroyed (22.5 to 12.5 new game, 15.5 to 7.5 mid-war, clear; station sieges lifted 1.5 to 5.5) but humanScore is flat: its voyages and garrisons spend the links' and hammers' supplies (paid hammers 27 to 16.5) and the war slows. `HumanBases.besiegers`, `warsim_reliefToBesiegers` -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 11
- **Why can't council hammers pay, and what fixed it (round 17, 2026-10-02)?** The bill is real maintenance - `ThreatReach.suppliesPerFP(faction)` measures vanilla upkeep per FP from the fleets out, 0.94 is only the default - for `siegeTripDays`, and the pools' income is 1.5-2x short of it. Cutting what the gate asks (deposit 30 to 15/10, trip halved, margin 1.25/1.0, full orbit off) sails sieges that starve en route: unpaid hammers rise, the Threat's score climbs. Halving the burn would be a 2x discount on real costs (inadmissible). Winner by the objective: `threatinc_reserveSurplusMult` 1.0 -> 1.5 (built the same day, LunaLib migration 11): at 60 seeds humanScore mid-war 32.5 against 12 (97% of seeds; bases held 32 against 11.5), new game 27.5 against 18 (80%; bases 17 against 10.5, kills 8 against 5); the Threat's score rises too (more to destroy), both-sides share up, no quiet seed. Caveats: the simulator scales all four commodities (the mod's troop share is `reserveTroopSurplusMult`), colony accrual is a dump fit (`HumanFit.ACCRUAL_BY_SIZE`). Game check the same day (ng9, 5 min at 64x): migration 11 fired (store 1 -> 1.5, marker 10 -> 11), 0 exceptions, the ledger accrues 1.5x fuel and supplies only (Salamanca supplies 562 -> 843 per 30 days; marines and armaments unchanged - the troop share is `reserveTroopSurplusMult`), one council siege sailed (Tri-Tachyon, 950 FP against Tia). -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 12
- **Is reserveSurplusMult shared?** No, since 2026-10-02: `threatinc_reserveSurplusMult` (1.5) is the humans' pools alone (`ThreatReserves.surplusMult`), the hives' banking reads `threatinc_hiveSurplusMult` (1.0; `ThreatFuel.perMonth`, `ThreatColonyUpkeep.localSupplies`; simulator `SwarmKnobs.surplusMult`). Until then one knob fed both, and 1.5 for both handed the swarm the larger gain (new game threatScore 4,109 against 886, humanScore 17.5 against 28.5). The split's defaults reproduce round 17's humans-only cell on both starts within seed noise (30 / 28.5 new, 32.5 / 33.5 mid; the one difference is marines, which `warsim_accrualMult` had scaled too). -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 13 Game check of the split (ng9, 3 min at 64x, jar 13:26): clean load, LunaLib registers the key and shows the row, the hive plan reads "made 78000/mo" against the 13:00 jar's 117000 (1.5x), so the hive is back at 1.0.
- **Is the CONSOLIDATE share at 0.75 a winner for the swarm (round 19, 2026-10-02)?** Not clear at 120 seeds from a new game (`threatinc_stanceConsolidateShare` 0.5 vs 0.75, -killWeight 1): threatScore 832 [552-2,321] vs 1,432 [596-2,537], the Threat ahead in 68% of seeds (the rule wants 75% and medians outside each other's bands); humanScore 27 vs 30, hives killed 10 vs 7.5, both-sides 65% -> 55%, quiet 0%. The seed spread (the game's own bistability on the stance) is wider than the gain; it stays at 0.5. Command line in `handover-2026-10-02.md` 3. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 15
- **Does the council's muster floor judge anything (round 18, 2026-10-02)?** No: `councilMusterFloor` x `plannedFP` compares the muster against the FP `force` sent, not the report's need, so it fails only a muster that lost ships en route (0 a run in the simulator; 0.25 and 0.75 reproduce 0.5 to the digit). The hunts then go alone when the siege is unpaid: 9 hammers a run (new game) take 0 worlds and lose ~1,100 FP of hunts. Judging the floor against the report-sized want fails the plays whose siege would have been paid (26 a run); standing the hunts down saves the FP and moves no score; holding them at the base to re-try the siege (`warsim_hammerWaitsForSiege`) buys 5 sieges a run and loses more in muster upkeep (bases held 17 -> 14.5). -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 14, 15
- **Which swarm lever moves the Threat's own score (rounds 18-19)?** Only `threatinc_stanceConsolidateShare`, peaking at 0.75 (0.5 -> 844, 0.65 -> 1,066, 0.75 -> 1,520, 0.85 -> 1,176 at 60 seeds new game; 0.75 in 76% of seeds, humans above their p10, no quieter war) but never clear of seed noise (threatScore's p10-p90 is three times the gain); inert mid-war. Seed price, EXPAND feed share and `strikeReinforceWeight` (the relief weight never decides a pick) are inert or mixed. Round 20 found a second, the consolidate feed share (below), and with it at 0.5 the CONSOLIDATE share is noise (0.35 / 0.65 / 0.75 ahead in 47% / 39% / 42% of seeds). -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 13, 15, 16e
- **Why is `threatinc_feedShareConsolidate` 0.5 (round 20, 2026-10-02)?** It was 0.9, and the largest stance share is also the share sustenance may take in any stance and the price of `spareSupplies` (`ThreatColonyUpkeep.sustainShare`): at 0.5 the cap is the press share, 0.7. 120 seeds, old against built: new game threatScore 889 -> 1,636 (76% of seeds; hive levels gained 233 -> 411), mid-war 326 -> 464 in every seed, clear (hives founded 93.5 -> 140, strikes 245 -> 188, the swarm ends with 195 hives against 148); humanScore 29 -> 29 and 37 -> 31, above the floor; the opening to month 48 unchanged. LunaLib marker 12. A cap of 0.65 scores 558 mid-war, 0.6 puts the humans under their floor (18 against 19.9) - a separate cap knob was not built. Not yet checked in the game (no test save on the machine that built it; `handover-2026-10-02.md` 3). -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16c-16d
- **Has the built consolidate share been checked in the game?** Yes (run tr1, 2026-10-02, new game, council on, 113 months): the migration line logged, marker 12, all 370 live knobs at CSV defaults, 0 mod exceptions; `Colony upkeep: ... share` reads 0.5 in EXPAND (75 months), 0.7 in PRESS (35) and 0.5 in CONSOLIDATE (2). -> `war-sim-real-runs-oct2.md` 1
- **What does a three-planet home do to the swarm?** `IncursionManager.pickOGSystem` takes any outer-half system `ThreatColonyManager.canSupportFullChain` passes, and that asks three colonisable planets. On three, `launchOGChain` lands Mining, Heavy Industry (`SEED_FORGE_KEY`), Mining - no refinery, no fuel plant until a world's second slot at size 4. Real run `tr1a` (Alpha Garbhodaka): fuel income +0 in months 5-20, first spread month 27, no strike by month 36 - the war a year later than pd9a's five-world home. Simulator, 60 seeds (`warsim_ogChain=3`): threatScore 1,569 -> 691, hives 131 -> 62.5 (clear), hives killed 7.5 -> 13, both-sides wars 55% -> 83%; a four-world home equals five. The late fuel plant is not the cost (chains of three and four both seed on day 760). Answered and built the same evening: next line. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 17
- **How many worlds does the swarm start on?** `threatinc_homeWorlds`, 4 (user's decision 2026-10-02; LunaLib 3-8, new wars only): `IncursionManager.pickOGSystem` asks that many colonisable planets (`ThreatColonyManager.canSupportChain`; steps down to three in a sector with none), `pickChainPlanets` lands on exactly that many, the home's other planets go to `tryExpandInSystem`, paid. Simulator, 60 seeds: 3 is the easier war (threatScore 1,701 -> 878, hives 124 -> 77, both-sides wars 57% -> 85%); 5, 6 and 8 are noise against 4 - the spare planets are taken within two years either way. Three is weaker because the fourth world's second slot is the second forge (day 504 against 778) and claims go per free forge - not for want of a refinery (`warsim_homeMinesOnly`, noise). Checked in the game (run hw4): an 8-planet home, four landings on days 133-144 (two mines, the seed forge, a refinery), the other four planets taken on days 671-710. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 18
- **Is a later first strike good for the swarm (`strikeMinSize` 5 / 6)?** By its score yes - 6 is clear from a new game (889 -> 2,514, 85% of seeds) - and it is not built: the gain is the war starting a year later (strikes by month 36: 9 / 3 / 0 at 4 / 5 / 6), nobody mobilised to attack it (0 months in CONSOLIDATE against 11, hives killed 6 -> 3). The stalemate clock starts at the first mobilisation and cannot see a longer quiet opening; when the war starts is the user's call. The knob is also the Phase 2 gate (`IncursionManager.getPhase`), the scouts' gate and the frontline guard's reading of a strike-capable hive. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16d
- **Do the swarm's scouts need a strike-sized hive?** Yes: `ThreatSwarmScouts.pickStaging` asks `strikeMinSize` and an operational nexus. The simulator did not until round 20 (`SwarmOps.scouts`; `warsim_scoutsAnySize` restores it): scouts sent 239 -> 139 a run, the base inside seed noise. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16d
- **Do sized strikes or expansion while consolidating help the swarm (round 20)?** No: `warsim_strikeSizedMargin` 1.5-3 destroys more bases (24 -> 29-36) for the same threatScore, `warsim_consolidateExpansionShare` 0.25-1.0 is noise. Also noise or worse at 30-60 seeds: `frontlineStrikeWeight` 1 / 6, `stanceSecondaryShare` 1.0, `stanceDwellDays` 90, `stanceWeakOdds` 0.25 / 0.75, `feedSharePress` 0.5; `stancePressRatio` 1.0 / 2.5 is inert (identical runs). -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16b
- **Does relief that goes home help the humans (round 20)?** Sending relief home is what turns the saved bases into bases held (a relief that stays is a garrison past the upkeep budget: bases founded 65 -> 56.5), but the mod already does most of it: `ThreatFrontlines.callGuard` calls a guard against a seen strike outside the budget and a rear link's goes home when no seen strike is bound (`garrison`). The new rule - the front's called guard goes home too (`warsim_reliefGoesHome`) - adds nothing from a new game and 4.5 bases mid-war (75% of seeds, inside the band): not built. The simulator's base models no `callGuard` and its relief switch overstates it (saves half the links; real council runs lost two thirds of theirs to strikes with `callGuard` in the build), so the switches stay off. `HumanBases.reliefHome`, `warsim_reliefRearGoesHome` -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16a
- **Is `strikeReinforceWeight` inert only in the simulator?** No, in the mod too while `strikeReliefFirst` is on (the default): `IncursionManager.pickStrikeTarget` returns `relief.pick()` whenever a front of the swarm's wants an expedition, and every relief candidate carries the same multiplier, so it cannot change a pick. It weighs only with relief-first off. Not a fidelity gap. `SwarmOps.strikeFrom` mirrors it.
- **Does the siege gate price more supplies than a siege eats?** Yes, 1.5x under the council and 2.1x under the planner (simulator, 30 seeds: trip priced 36.3k against 23.9k burned over 20 sieges; 699k against 336k over 304) - a siege is home, or smaller, inside the horizon it was priced for. Halving the price did not raise the humans' score (round 17), so the margin is not the brake. Counters `siegeTrip.priced` / `.burned` / `.owed`. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16f
- **Do the simulator's armaments bank at the troop rate?** Yes since round 20 (`HumanPools.daily`, as `ThreatReserves.surplusMult`); they had banked 1.5x too fast. The base moved inside seed noise: armaments do not bind. -> `war-sim-rounds.md` 16
- **Does the simulator postpone sieges as the mod does?** In count yes (13,189 against pd10a's 12,606 postponements; 361 sailed against 423), in make no: the simulator's planner refuses mostly on fuel (farther options paying passage), pd10a on supplies with fuel 4x over. -> `war-sim-council-trials.md` 12
- **Why did a Hegemony hammer go "all in at 27 of 27 FP" against 1,326 FP reported (game check 2026-10-02)?** `Play.plannedFP` is the FP actually sent (`ThreatPlays.strike`'s hunt: `councilHammerShare` x `ThreatSoftening.playPayableFP`, a 25-FP absolute minimum), so `councilMusterFloor` x plannedFP only fails a play through losses en route and never judges the plan against the report's want; the simulator's `HumanCouncil` mirrors this exactly (why the floor moved nothing in round 13). The hunts then go in alone against the reported swarm. Rule variants (floor against the want, no hunts alone, a relative hunt threshold) are in the simulator's round 18. -> `war-council.md` 16
- **How fast is the simulator?** After rounds 9-10 (2026-10-02): a 104-month run about 1.7 s (was 10 s), a 30-seed batch about 13 s (was 69 s), a 30-seed compare about 19 s, tables byte-identical; seeds run in parallel (`Main.runs`). `HumanPlanner.size` is memoised on every input it reads (`State.sizeMemo`). Hot paths, fixes and what is left by symbol in `war-sim-swarm.md` 13. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 13
- **What does the simulator's swarm pressure read?** `ThreatPosture.read`'s five terms since round 4: contacts for `swarmContactDays`, staged capacity by trust, losses, hostiles, forward guards. `SwarmPosture.sight` / `stagingHive`. In the simulator `stanceDays.*` are the human factions' stances; the swarm's are `monthsExpand` / `monthsPress` / `monthsConsolidate`. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 10
- **Why does the simulator's swarm stay small-hived?** Before round 4 its stance left CONSOLIDATE within a month (pressure read 4-8k against 12-20k; fixed to month 72, the low seeds still leave near month 60); pd9a's `ThreatStance` held CONSOLIDATE 53 of 72 months in years 3-8, then founded 20 hives in the last 7 months. Mean hive size 3.2-3.9 against 5.4. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 9
- **Where do the swarm's supplies go (pd9a)?** Month 100: made 77-84k; sustenance 47k of a 93k bill, fleets away 20k (30-41k FP, mostly strikes holding Defend stations over their fronts, `ThreatSwarmDefend`), growth 0-6k. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 9
- **What does the strike gate read?** The system's vanilla fleet strength plus the station, one figure per system, not ground defence (Donn 1,447 against a ground defence of 55). `IncursionManager.strikeOutweighed` -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 9
- **What raises an NPC hunt?** Only a running `ThreatSwarmBountyIntel` on the system: posted at the provisions gate and when a siege calls off. `ThreatSoftening.tick`, sizing in `ThreatSoftening.send` -> `war-sim-humans.md` 6
- **Where is a siege sized?** `IncursionManager.siegeSizesFor` -> `siegeFleetGoal` -> `siegeOrbitNeeded` (= `siegeOrbitFaced` x `npcSiegeOrbitMargin`, from the faction's report), for the planner (`launchPlanned`) and the council's plays (`ThreatPlays.strike` / `saturate` / `stage`) alike since 2026-10-02. No trust term. Hunts, decoys and squadrons keep share-of-means sizes. -> `war-council.md` 16
- **How does a faction see a hive system?** Only through `ThreatIntel.eyesIn` - any of its fleets, colonies, ground fronts or unspawned routes there - since 2026-10-02 (no radar); with nothing there the last report stands and ages. The swarm: `ThreatSwarmIntel.sees(LocationAPI)` answers EYES for a system with a live hive, Threat front, strike route or any live Threat fleet, nothing in hyperspace. LunaLib migration 10 drops `threatinc_radarRangeLY` / `threatinc_swarmRadarRangeLY` (`LunaConfigBridge.drop`). -> `threat-fog.md`, `attack-planner.md`
- **How is an NPC siege's landing sized?** On the larger of the defence as it stands and the Nexus anchor (400 x strata left x 1.5 x tier), worn by the plan's share, so a hive already worn still draws a full landing. `IncursionManager.raidStrNeededAt`, `nexusAnchoredDefense` -> `war-sim-humans.md` 5
- **Why did pd9a's fronts on hives win 24 of 41?** Landings sized on the anchor push at E >= D; small ones dig in, brace, are battered at about 1.6:1 every 20 days and wait for a reinforcing siege; the flotilla's cover stops the swarm's bombardment (59 set, 9 lost). -> `war-sim-humans.md` 5
- **How does a Threat landing end (pd9a)?** 86 landings: 56 overrun by the garrison (median 136 days unreinforced, 405 reinforced), 7 colonies taken, all pirate or Path worlds with no reserve. Counter-attacks run 1.3-1.9:1 and overrun at 2:1. Troops are 0.8 per FP in orbit. -> `war-sim-swarm.md` 3-4
- **Where do the human rules shared with the simulator live?** `src/threatinc/rules/`: `BattleRules`, `ReachRules`, `ReserveRules`, `PlannerRules`, `CouncilRules` - pure, no game types; the mod classes call them. Sizing still mirrored, not shared: `IncursionManager.needAndWear`, `ThreatReach.tripSupplies`. -> `war-sim-humans.md` 1, 3
- **What eats the factions' supplies?** Fleet upkeep. pd9a Hegemony: a median 18 fleets out, 1,237 supplies per 5-day pass (~7,400 a month) on ~6,900 income, 85% of it paid; 252 of 945 hunt orders ended "out of supplies". Reserves have no cap. `ThreatUpkeep.poll` -> `war-sim-humans.md` 5
- **When did each faction act in pd9a?** War day mobilised / first base / first siege / first landing / first kill: persean 1126 / 1193 / 1186 / 1333 / 1455; hegemony 1183 / 1286 / 1244 / 1425 / 1604; tritachyon 2355 / 2430 / 2480 / 2717 / 2761; independent 2390 / 2461 / 2454 / 2865 / 3003. -> `war-sim-humans.md` 5
- **What brakes link founding in a run?** "cannot pay for a link - no base holds the stock" (260+ lines in pd9a), then the garrison gate (`ThreatFrontlines.garrisonBase`: upkeep within `upkeepBudget`, the voyage payable). pd9a founded 15 links in 114 months, 7 of them in the first year of the war. -> `war-sim-humans.md` 5
- **How long does an off-screen siege take?** pd9a's 150 daily sieges: landings came on day 1 at the median (p90 61-88 days), a beaten one lasted 19 days with 6 fight days and kept 0.30 of its FP; 53 were called off, most on arrival. `ThreatPurgeFGI.advanceDaily` -> `war-sim-humans.md` 5

- **What state must be cleared on game load?** Every static holding `Global.getSector()`: `ThreatReach.forget()` nulls them and zeroes `committed`; `ThreatIncModPlugin.onGameLoad` also clears `ThreatFrontlines.forgetCaches`, `ThreatReserves.forgetCaches`, `ThreatStance.forget`. Add any new static there. -> `engine-code-paths.md` 1
- **Does loading an old save behave like a new game?** No. A hive stock first seen is endowed `FAB_ENDOWMENT_DAYS` 180 of production (on lt: ~54k supplies + 160k fuel, spent in one pass on spare copies), and the save's territory and fleets are a head start. A save drifted by a broken rule is not a fair test of its fix. Judge balance on a new game, over several runs: six runs of one sector ranged from extinction to a 78-world snowball.
- **Will a 0.6 / 0.7 save load on the current build?** Statically yes (2026-10-06): a Starsector save dies on a mod class or field it holds that the jar no longer has (XStream); every `threatinc.` class in the latest Saturn save (`save_SaturnHadean_8807243588242812142`, mod 0.6.0, 2026-09-07) and StarLord save (`save_StarLord_1669224817518795825`, 0.7.0 dev build of 2026-09-30, already holding `ThreatFrontlines$Outpost`) still exists, and every field they store is declared on the class or a vanilla superclass (`dR`/`m`/`sB` are vanilla aliases). Check: `savefields.pl` over `campaign.xml` vs `javap -p` of the jar with the superclass walk. New persisted fields are primitives or lazily created maps, the strike fund absent reads 0, the Luna store migrates to version 15. Loaded in the game the same day (batch sat/sl, `sbs.ps1 -Bases`, Officer Extension enabled for it): both load in ~3 min and ran 100-120 days with no exception; the strike fund starts at 0 on an old save and reached 7,729 / 5,317 FP by day 120, so the first staged strike comes 4-6 months after the upgrade; hives already found stay found; patrols fly at once. Both saves also list Officer Extension, not enabled now; the real player campaigns on the machine are the Saturn (0.6.0) and StarLord (0.7.0 dev) ones - no save of the 0.7.0 release itself exists here.
- **Which save tests what?** `lt` is month 144 with old-rules hives (mean size 7.5, 36-41 hives, 80-90k FP); ng7a (the save `save_AresDaniels_5500560879992783035ng7`, so `-From ng7`; ng7a was its first run's tag) is a new game at month 38 on current rules (11 hives, 11.6k FP, no sieges yet). `save_PoseidonDeimos_8594169485512677365` is a new game generated 2026-10-01 by the swarm-fog build: pd2a (fog on) and pd3a (fog off) ran 15 chunks from it. Compare runs only at equal age and with the same factions mobilised. -> `testing-harness.md` "Fast-forward runs and a new game"
- **How do I add a knob?** In `settings.json` and `ThreatIncConfig`, plus a `LunaSettings.csv` row ONLY if a player would change it for a tangible effect (63 rows since 2026-10-27; a balance lever or an internal rule gets none - the user, that day). `ThreatIncConfig.luna(key)` asks LunaLib only for keys with a row: asking for one it lacks logs an ERROR per read (34k lines in pd2a, fixed 2026-10-01); `ThreatIncConfig.f()` throws on a missing key, so remove getter and keys in one change. Log verdicts with `logOnChange` (port disruption once spammed 106 lines, "Wreck or siege" ~1,000). A changed default never reaches an existing LunaLib store: add a `LunaConfigBridge.migrateStoredDefaults` block (`bump` throws on a Boolean). -> `engine-code-paths.md` 10
- **Where does a daily reader of the swarm go?** In the `IncursionManager` poll just before `ThreatFleetOrders.poll`, gated on a calendar day (`ThreatReach.today()`) or a persisted stamp - the manager is transient, so its fields and `tick()` re-run after every load. -> `engine-code-paths.md` 2
- **Does `garrisonsFor` mutate?** Yes, it puts an empty list when absent; a pure read is `garrisons().get(id)`. Threat FP over a hive is `ThreatGroundFronts.swarmOrbitStrength` (no flag filter; a fleet near two worlds counts twice when summed). -> `engine-code-paths.md` 3
- **Which routes are in a system?** `RouteManager.getRoutesInLocation`: one location per route from its current segment, mid-jump routes in hyperspace; strength in vanilla units (about 50 per size point), `extra.fp` usually null. So a siege's "(vanilla units)" fight line reads 2x its FP (25 per point); pd6a's 7,450 FP saturation fought as 30,507 because an expired route still in the system (its base there) was counted twice, fixed 2026-10-02 in `ThreatAbstractBattle.attackerStrength` (vanilla's own autoresolve still does it). -> `engine-code-paths.md` 8
- **Does a player outpost watch anything?** `ThreatOutposts` are market-less and outside the economy, so `ThreatFrontlines.watches`, `detectedAt` and `ThreatReserves.marketsOf` never see them; read `ThreatOutposts.outpostsOf`. -> `engine-code-paths.md` 6
- **How do I build?** `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File compile.ps1`, then check `jars/ThreatInc.jar`'s timestamp. In-process, javac's "unchecked" note stops the script before `jar` and the old jar stays. The game locks the jar: kill `java.exe` first. -> `CLAUDE.md` "Quick facts"
- **Where is the game log?** `starsector-core/starsector.log`, appended across launches (take the last match), rolls to `.log.1` at about 50 MB. Mod lines start `[ThreatInc]`; none at all means `threatinc_debugLogging` is off in the LunaLib store (`saves/common/LunaSettings/threatinc.json.data`, which shadows CSV defaults). -> `testing-harness.md` "Reading results"
- **How do I run a fast-forward test?** `tools/test-harness/fastforward/`: `clone.ps1 -Base save_X -From lt -To h40`, `launch.ps1`, `run.ps1 -Chunks 3 -Seconds 110 -SaveEvery 3 -Tag h40a`. Probe 1 chunk first. Never pass `-Unpause` to a running game. -> `testing-harness.md` "Fast-forward runs and a new game"
- **How do I read the results?** `extract.ps1 -Tag` writes the run's `[ThreatInc]` lines to `ti-<tag>.txt`; `census.ps1 -Tag -Every 4` tabulates the census; `reach-digest.ps1` digests reach. Output in `$env:THREATINC_TEST_OUT` (default `%TEMP%\threatinc-tests`). A chunk with 0 lines means a dialog stopped the clock. -> `tools/test-harness/fastforward/run.ps1` header
- **How do I run a new-game test?** `launch.ps1 -MenuOnly`, click through creation with `step.ps1`, F5, quit, `newgame-prep.ps1 -Base save_<Name>_<id> -To ngN`. Pristine save `save_AresDaniels_5500560879992783035`. -> `testing-harness.md` "Fast-forward runs and a new game"
- **Why does the test fleet get caught or the clock stall?** The Threat is hostile to everyone. `player-ignored.ps1 on` before, `off` after (the LunaLib store is global). -> `testing-harness.md` "Fast-forward runs and a new game"
- **Can game runs go side by side?** Yes since 2026-10-04: `tools/test-harness/fastforward/sbs.ps1 -Tags a,b,c -Days 3750`. Two games ran 210 and 212 days a minute each against 215 alone, so a batch takes one run's time. Each game has its own `saves\_sbs\<tag>\saves` and `logs` (the `-Dcom.fs.starfarer.settings.paths.*` parameters; the Starsector folder itself is not writable), so its own LunaLib store and dumps; the launcher's `continue` takes a path into that folder. A Shift posted to a window does nothing: LWJGL's `WindowsKeyboard.poll` lets go of Left Shift unless `GetAsyncKeyState` shows it physically down, system-wide - so the script holds Shift with `keybd_event` and posts the key-down to every window, and lets go while keyboard or mouse is in use. Untested: more than two games, late-war speed, the stall nudges. -> `testing-harness.md` "Side-by-side runs"
- **How fast does a long run go, and what bounds it (2026-10-04)?** About 100 in-game days a real minute over a 125-month run (38 minutes): 189 a minute in months 0-40, 94-120 in months 40-105, 47-55 after (run hw5e; normal speed is 6). `shiftspeed_mult` 48 is never reached: the campaign runs on one thread (1.4 of 20 cores used) and each day costs more as fleets multiply. Stack samples of the main thread (`jstack`, `%TEMP%\threatinc-tests\prof2.pl`): month 47, 2% in the mod; month 112, 21% of 156 samples in the mod, spread thin (`ThreatScouts.poll` 4.5%, mostly `ThreatConvoys.stagingHive` under `launchSorties`; garrison upkeep 4.5%; `IncursionManager.tick` 3%), vanilla fleet AI (`TacticalModule`) 20%, fleets 9%, locations 8%, markets 6%. So the mod's own code can buy at most a quarter; runs side by side are the real lever (blocker: Shift held in the foreground window; each game takes 2.8 GB). -> `testing-harness.md`
- **Can a run's `-Knobs` undo a LunaLib migration?** Yes: `run-go.ps1` puts each knob back to the value the store held before the run, so a migration that ran during that run is undone and never runs again (the marker has moved). Found 2026-10-04: the store held `threatinc_fabricateTroopsPerFP` 10 a day after migration 13 set 5; set to 5 by hand (`luna-set.ps1`). Before a run, diff the store against `LunaSettings.csv` and pass only knobs that differ from the shipped default.
- **How do I force a state the save lacks?** Clone the save and edit `campaign.xml` (XStream, one element per line); `clone.ps1` fixes `saveDirName` so the clone does not write back to the original. -> `testing-harness.md` "Forcing a game state"
- **How does vanilla compute a market's "available" units (decompiled 2026-10-26, `com.fs.starfarer.campaign.econ.reach.CommodityMarketData`)?** For each demanded or produced commodity: `available` = "Local production" (its `maxSupply`) + "Desired volume" (`maxDemand - maxSupply`, the imports wanted), less an "In-faction shortage" (the wanted imports over the faction's largest single source, `CommodityMarketDataAPI.getMaxExport(factionId)` - a maximum, never a sum) and "Insufficient accessibility" (over the same-faction shipping, `Misc.getShippingCapacity(market, true)` = (accessibility + 0.5) / 0.1 units); the global route is the same over `getMaxExportGlobal` and out-of-faction shipping, the better of the two taken. So it never reads above the world's current demand and is 0 for anything not demanded - what a bigger demand would see is `ThreatColonyManager.availableAt(market, commodity, demand)` (fix 6). -> `hive-economy.md` "Vanilla industry numbers"
- **How do I look at vanilla internals?** `javap` on `starsector-core/starfarer.api.jar`; API source in `starfarer.api.zip`; obfuscated UI in `starfarer_obf.jar`. No decompiler is recorded: ask before assuming one. -> `hive-economy.md` "Vanilla industry numbers"
- **Where do the game's swarm strikes go, and what stops them (round 32, 2026-10-04)?** Joined by target system in hw5b/hw4z/hw4p logs (`sfight2.pl`): bases 235/152/78 strikes, colonies 101/172/237. A colony strike lands or reinforces ~78% of the time; 15-19% arrive outweighed (`Off-screen fight` defence >= strength: vanilla skips the raid, nothing lands), bases 22-28%. The simulator broke off 1-8% (its arrival defence reads the start dump's gate) - still open. -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 7
- **Why did the simulated swarm strike colonies 2:1 where hw5b's struck bases 2:1?** Forward-base garrisons. The mod stands a garrison only where a hive would strike that faction first (`ThreatFrontlines.strikeAt`/`frontOf` under billed reach); the simulator garrisoned every base in reach, so its swarm saw 68% of bases guarded (median 1,607 defence units) against the game's dumps' 190-330 (`gate`, 30-43% at the bare 120 station). `warsim_frontFacedOnly` is on since round 32. -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 7
- **How do the game's Threat fronts end (hw4z to ~m126)?** 73 overrun (median 200 days after landing, not days), 10 ground down by relief, 5 colonies lost - all no-reserve worlds (Qaras, Garnir, Kanta's Den, Lost Astropolis, Laicaille) - and 20 still up, most at stratum 0 for 300+ days. Share 0 (hw4p) loses 40 by m126, reserve colonies included. `fronts.pl`. -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 7
- **What does a Threat fleet away burn?** 0.73 supplies a FP a month (Reach lines, hw4p/hw4z/hw5b, 0.71-0.74 a run); the simulator's `SwarmFit.SUPPLIES_PER_FP_AWAY` was 0.5 (old runs) until round 32. Even so the simulated swarm piles 180-545k supplies by m120 against the game's 6-170k. -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 7
- **Which NPC fleets pay fleet upkeep in the game?** Every live non-player fleet with a `ThreatReturns` home, garrisons excepted (they pay through their link) - relief, convoys and scouts included - at vanilla maintenance; unspawned sieges by `chargeAbstract`. hw4z: 3.34M supplies by m120, the simulator's orders-only upkeep 1.8M; game human supplies fall to 136k by m120, the simulator's stay ~900k. `ThreatUpkeep.charge` -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 7
- **Why did the simulated swarm sit in CONSOLIDATE from month 43 (round 33, 2026-10-04)?** An early-war chain. Its first strike sailed on day 729-1094 with 389-2,962 FP; the game's sail on day 976-1190 with 3,000-6,000 FP (hw4p/q/s/z, hw5b/d), because a strike waits until the fuel stock pays the whole muster's passage (`IncursionManager.pickStrikeTarget`) and the simulator's muster was lighter. So it took Qaras in Yma on day 1100 (game: 1567). A hive inside a human system is found at once, the councils left HOLD 100-180 days after their first council (game: about a year), and 15 hammers started in months 24-47 (game: 0). Each hammer's muster reads as staged pressure on its hive system (35k FP on Beta Laphirial), so 3 of 6 systems turned THREATENED. The swarm then consolidated over months 43-90 (game: 52-64), founded nothing and piled supplies (150k by month 72). -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 8
- **What does a swarm cost by its row?** Read from the dumps' `wantFP` (`ThreatPosture.rowsFP` of the learned `swarmCostEstimate`, every forge hive with no need, hw4p/s/z, hw5b): LOW 67, MEDIUM 143, HIGH 345, and a fabricator about 555 more (size 5's {1, MEDIUM} row 724, size 6's {1, HIGH} 876). The mod's fallback table (`HiveRules.swarmCostFallback`) says +40 a fabricator. The simulator built on that until round 33, so its size-5 hives wanted 800 against the game's 1,355 and held about half the game's garrison (910 against 2,020 at day 904). The "Garrison fleet fabricated" lines cannot give this: they log no spec, and the row a build fills (`fit`) is not the fleet count. `SwarmFit.builtFP`, `FABRICATOR_FP` (`warsim_fabricatorFP`) -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 8
- **How does the sector find a hive (and where was the simulator wrong)?** `ThreatScouts.launchSorties`: each strike leaves the struck faction a lead at its true origin. A party walks a route through the unknown, uninhabited planet systems within `scoutLeadRadiusLY` 6, nearest first from the faction's nearest base, staying `scoutStayDays` 2 at each stop, and ends at the first live hive. Parties keep launching until the lead's area is swept, and a faction with no lead sweeps 10 ly around each military world every 60 days. hw4z found its hives one system at a time over days 1283-1528. Until round 33 the simulator's scout flew straight to the origin and filed every hive within 6 ly at once (five systems on day 1199). The councils then hammered from month 37, and the hammer musters' staged pressure held the swarm in CONSOLIDATE for years. `warsim_scoutRoutes` (`HumanIntel.sorties`) -> `war-sim-calibration-r30.md` 8
- **Screenshots and clicks?** `tools/test-harness/ui.ps1` and `gameshot.ps1` in a fresh `powershell -NoProfile -File`; clicks fail silently unless the game is in front. Coordinates per resolution in `testing-harness.md` "Coordinates". Screenshots are read by a subagent, never the main thread.
- **Pause state in tests?** `pauseAfterMap` is true: the campaign is paused after leaving intel, one space unpauses. Check the campaign date, not the log. A dialog blocks F5; never kill the game on an unconfirmed save. -> `testing-harness.md`
- **Environment traps?** `python` is a Windows Store stub (use Perl, PowerShell or Edit). Line endings are mixed (74 LF, 8 CRLF on 2026-10-01): check with a raw Perl read, not Git Bash grep, which misreports carriage returns; `sed -i` strips them. `-Crop` does not parse through `powershell -File` on this decimal-comma locale; write save XML numbers with InvariantCulture (`600000,0` broke a save). PowerShell `-replace` is case-insensitive (corrupted `mS=` in a save: use `.Replace`), `Set-Content` adds a BOM, backticks in double-quoted strings break patch anchors (use Edit). `git commit -F -` from a here-string pipe fails: use a message file.
- **Does `git apply` keep a file's line endings?** No. With `core.autocrlf` true it writes every file it touches as CRLF, LF files included (2026-10-01: the off-screen siege patch turned 8 LF files CRLF). Note each file's endings first and put the LF ones back with a Perl script file (`s/\r\n$/\n/` per line; inline `perl -e` mangles the backslashes in Git Bash). A worktree agent's patch is cut against HEAD, without the main checkout's uncommitted work: `git apply --check` first, then compile.
- **What blocks the clock or F5 in a test?** Accident reports (out of supplies or credits), Hegemony inspections, Luddic Path and Church hails. Clone with 600k supplies and 60k fuel, transponder on, park at the system edge, and verify the quicksave timestamp.
- **Why did a long run lose its early months?** The log rolls at 50 MB; read from the last "Reading save data from", stitch `starsector.log.1`, `.2`, ... (`extract.ps1` does since 2026-10-02), or copy the log each chunk. Open it with FileShare.ReadWrite while the game runs.
- **A fresh machine?** git and the JDK may not be on PATH in an existing shell (rebuild PATH from Machine+User env); use the portable Temurin zip (winget hung on UAC); set a repo-local git identity; the first push needs the user's login. At 125% scaling harness clicks miss until `SetProcessDPIAware`.

## Traps that cost hours
- **Never `Global.getSector().getStarSystem(system.getId())` (2026-10-16, a player crash):** vanilla matches a system's optional unique id when one is set, else its base name, while `getId()` is always the lowercased base name - vanilla's own abyssal "Deep Space" systems (`AbyssalRogueStellarObjectEPEC`, `NamelessRock`) carry unique ids, so they come back null and all share `getId()` "deep space" (Scouting Swarm NPE in `ThreatScoutRoute.sendTo`). Look systems up with `ThreatScoutRoute.systemById` (scans on a miss), and keep abyssal systems off routes (`ThreatScouts.unreachable`).
- **Human colony counts before 2026-10-19 16:45 are 1-2 factions high.** `read-hw208.sh` summed the last EIGHT `Census: <faction> colonies` lines, but a census block has seven factions (fewer once a faction is gone), so one or two lines of the previous block were counted twice: hw219a 52 is 38, hw227 22 / 13 is 22 / 9, hw229 (partial) 41 / 37. Count the last block only: `tools/test-harness/reads/colonies.sh <ti-log>` (also prints bases and the per-faction split); read-hw208.sh fixed the same way.
- **Never `tail -f` `sbs-status.txt` during a batch (2026-10-08):** Windows locks it, every `Say` in `sbs.ps1` fails and the batch's status lines are lost (the games and dumps are unharmed; `sbs-go.done` marks the end). Poll with `tail -n` - `testing-harness.md`.
- **Why does `watch.sh` flag `hoard` and `nowar` in the first poll of a checkpoint batch (2026-10-09, hw121)?** The ck2 / ck5 checkpoints start at war day ~2,186 with no strike launched yet, so both fire on month 1. Launch the watcher with `hoard,nowar` in the ignore list, and only after `java.exe` is up (before it, the watcher reads "batch ended" and exits). Its `broke` signal fired on every game until 2026-10-09 (the sed lacked `-E`, so the awk compared words: hw121 read 51k supplies against +69k a month as broke) - fixed in `watch.sh` the same day: `tools/test-harness/reads/watch.sh hwNNN 70 "hoard,nowar,overdue,feed,churn,enough,muster,rowfor" ./check-<hypothesis>.sh` - the chain's older checks (`check-*.sh`) flag their own past hypotheses (a front braced 13+ times, a swarm kicked 3+ times) on every run; ignore all but the batch's own. With the parse fixed, `broke` (three censuses running under 5% of a month's supplies output) is a reading, not a fault: hw117a, the 151-hive game, spent 19 months so, hw119a 1, hw121 1-3 by m55 - ignore it too
- **How is a batch launched from a session (2026-10-10, hw138)?** As a background Bash task: `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools/test-harness/fastforward/sbs.ps1 -Tags hw138a,hw138b,hw138c -Base save_OceanPena_1024894203100914406 -Days 4700 > $LOCALAPPDATA/Temp/threatinc-tests/sbs-run-hw138.txt 2>&1`. `Start-Process powershell ... -WindowStyle Hidden` from the PowerShell tool died silently before the first status line (no process, no log) - never that. Then the watcher as its own background task once `java.exe` is up, with the batch's own check file (`check-balance.sh` for hw138: the `Mineable balance:` census line, flags `lopsided` and `onesource`). **The window check (2026-10-10 11:17):** `sbs.ps1` wants a `client 1600x900` window; that morning the driver listed no 1600x900 mode (hw138a's log: 1280x720, 1440x1080 ... 1920x1080; hw137a's: 1152x864, 1440x900, 1600x900 ...; a display reconfiguration at 10:50:46 in the System event log) and every game came up 1280x768 and was killed (`NO GAME WINDOW`). `-Resolution 1920x1080` (new; `run-settings.ps1` takes it too) runs the batch at a mode both lists hold, Continue at (1350,372) - the probe loaded and ran. When 1600x900 is back the default still works.

- **Why is 0 a bad "never" for a day?** The calendar's day numbers are negative: `ThreatPosture.today()` (`getElapsedDaysSince(1L)`) is about -642,000 in cycle 206, so a 0 or -1000 sentinel lies in the future. h50a's raids never sailed: `ThreatAttackPlanner.siegeArrival` returned 0 for "no siege", and every raid waited for day 0. Use `-Float.MAX_VALUE`. Or a flag: `ThreatStrikeFGI.metSampleDay` = -1 for "not sampled" made every strike since the answer ratio was built report "no read" until hw50 (the answer ratio never learned; `metRead` since), and `ThreatOffensive.startDay` = -1 for "no campaign" reset the start every pass, so the deadline launch never ran in hw42-49 (NaN since); `ThreatPosture`'s `S_SURPLUS_SINCE >= 0f` test means the stale-surplus recycle (`recycleSurplus` past `breakEvenDays`) has never fired - left as it is, the user's call (2026-10-07: it would recycle the spare the offensive now spends).
- **Assert from the code or the log, never memory.** "Links build no industry", "552k fuel at Chicomoztoc" (a 16-market pool), "vanilla NPCs pay nothing for supplies" and a guessed "circular hunt exclusion" were all wrong and cost the user's trust.
- **A shortage rule that answers any unmet demand overbuilds** (20 fuel plants in one tick, later 59 never retired). Pace answers, build retirement with them, and compare a stock against the other commodity, not against wants that stay "short".
- **One lever per run, and check the opposite resource**: links building fuel first fixed fuel and starved supplies.
- **A frozen number is a clock bug, not AI**: a pressure stuck at 6,442 was `getElapsedDaysSince(0L)`; a static 30-day timer never fired. Log raw numbers early.
- **Review before the long test**: after a big change a read-only review found statics surviving load, 100,000-fleet siege lists, quadratic re-sorts and a doubled refund.
- **Builder agents die on API errors** (ENOTFOUND, self-signed cert): resume with SendMessage. Give builders disjoint files, and give each task its own scratch subfolder (shared names like `ex.pl` got overwritten).
- **Pool membership reads `ThreatReserves.available` only.** Reading spendable, spare, `escortStock`, `stagingBank` or `expeditionRangeLY` inside logistic reach recurses without bound.
- **A new gate can starve a flow silently**: gating convoys on escort supplies cut them 82 -> 17. Diff a counts table against a baseline log, and make gate and reach read the same stock measure (mixing them took stand-downs 17 -> 47 in h45a).
- **Planner and executor share one pricing function**: the squadron verdict priced with `Float.MAX_VALUE` fuel, then gated all-or-nothing, hid smaller squadrons and left the runtime path untested.
- **Ask before skipping a balanced engine**: the one-shot 300-day "wreck" read to the user as a regression.
- **Do not assume a mechanic the user remembers exists**, and do not deny one either: check, then answer with numbers before building.
- **Grep the raw `starsector.log` for exceptions**: `extract.ps1` drops Exception/ERROR lines.
- **Subagent hand-backs are model output**, never the user's decisions.

## Intel UI

- **What can't the intel UI do?** No new intel tab, no reusing the Planets tab, no icons in stock table cells, no horizontal scroll. Stock columns stretch, so scale overlay offsets by drawn width / column sum. -> `intel-ui-platform.md` "Things you cannot do"
- **Fatal UI traps?** "May only anchor on siblings": anchor to the `beginTable2` panel and offset by row. Add floating buttons last and restore `setHeightSoFar`. Controls inside `createCustom` never report callbacks. `ThreatWarBoard.addFrontButtons` -> `intel-ui-platform.md` "Traps"
- **Why did E stack Intel screens?** (2026-10-24, built untested) An Intel screen hosted in a dialog (`showCore(INTEL)`, the old notice click) or reached by E inside the board's colony screen (a nested CARGO core) opens another Intel on E instead of closing. Notice clicks now open the campaign's own Intel (`ThreatNoticeClickDialog.OpenBoard`, `showCoreUITab`); E inside the colony screen closes it back to the board (`ThreatColonyScreenDialog.Guard`). Never host the Intel tab in a dialog.
- **Why did Min/Med/Max jump the board to the top?** `updateUIForItem` rebuilds the large description at scroll 0; ladders and orders now go through `ThreatIncursionIntel.rerenderInPlace`, which saves the scroller's Y offset and `createLargeDescription` restores it (2026-10-24, built untested). `saveSmallDescScrollState` covers only the small description.
- **What width does the board get?** 1195 px at 3440x1440, 997 at 1080p; folds narrow below `ThreatWarBoard.NARROW_WIDTH` 1050. -> `intel-ui-platform.md` "Geometry you actually get"
- **When does an expedition play vanilla's colony-crisis alarm?** Only when a target is the player's (user, 2026-10-24). Strikes and sieges keep `playerTargeted` true (vanilla's strength checks read it), so the sound is overridden instead. `ThreatStrikeFGI.getCommMessageSound`, `ThreatPurgeFGI.getCommMessageSound`
- **Why does a dialog menu ignore setEnabled/setTooltip?** `removeOption` rebuilds the panel and drops them on the other options: shape first, state last. -> `CLAUDE.md`, `ground-war-fronts.md` "removeOption drops the enabled state"
- **Where do order buttons route?** `ThreatFactionView.addOrderPrompt` / `executeOrder` for both views; tooltips via `disableWith`; tier selector `addTierSelector`. -> `war-board.md` "Layout, top to bottom"
- **Absolute day stamps?** `getElapsedDaysSince(0L)` is `Float.MAX_VALUE`; use `ThreatPosture.today()` (`since(1L)`). -> `README.md` "Gotchas"
- **Why did the hw29a swarm die on 1.7M fuel and 450k supplies (2026-10-06)?** Not the stance (CONSOLIDATE founds since 2026-10-04): FP. Swarms are bought with forge FP only, the posture read `want 23k, held 10k, surplus 0` for 4.5 years, so garrison replacement took every FP and seeding none; fuel and supplies buy no ship, more forges need more size-3+ hives. -> `game-runs-2.md` 29
- **How is a fleet paid for in hulls (every side, 2026-10-06)?** The hull pool (`ThreatHulls`): a market's standing hulls = vanilla patrol counts (`PATROL_NUM_*_MOD`) x 20 / 37.5 / 62.5 FP through `Misc.getAdjustedStrength` (quality, fleet size, officers); a faction's pool is the sum, every fleet out holds its FP against it (`ThreatAidCapacity` ledger, committed at `ThreatReturns.provision`), a loss is a debt rebuilt only by Heavy Industry / Orbital Works ships output x `fabFPPerShipUnit`. Caps `ThreatSoftening.payableFP`, the NPC siege's payable, sorties, link guards, the player's capacity; the hive's want is `ThreatPosture.minimumFP` = its standing hulls. -> `hull-pool.md`
- **What did the hull pool do over a full war (hw31, 2026-10-06)?** Humans fight to the last hull on 1-8k pools (yards 0.2-3.8k FP/mo) while hoarding 350k-1.1M fuel and 110-520k supplies - every postponement hull-bound, none for fuel or supplies; the swarm (50-65 forges, 21-26k FP/mo) is fuel-bound days 600-1000, supplies-bound 1000-1900, then hoards fuel 230-555k and converts plants back; forward bases still build fuel before Heavy Industry; hull-starved factions launch no scouts and stay blind. `game-runs-2.md` 31.
- **Did the stock-plan builder stop the self-dismantling (hw32, 2026-10-06)?** Where yards came: hw32a abandoned 33 of 96 bases (hw31a 64 of 79) with Hegemony yards 200 -> 2,900 FP/mo; hw32c level; hw32b worse with no Tri-Tachyon yard. Heavy Industry first everywhere (43 / 23 / 41), Military Bases 23 / 9 / 33, zero conversions (nothing surplus to tear down); pools still 0 free - production-bound. `game-runs-2.md` 32.
- **Which sector are the hw runs on?** Two since 2026-10-08: ck2 (`AphelionDysnomia`, hw60-hw69, opener at Kazeron) and ck3 (`AmaruDugas`, the hw4 sector, hw70, opener at Jangala; remade as ck5 on the chest fix for hw72+, opener at Chicomoztoc, 32 hives at the opening) - `testing-harness.md` "ck3". Before that one: every `sbs.ps1` batch (hw30-hw33, 2026-10-06) clones the same pristine hw4 new-game save, so the swarm's first colony is Alpha Laphirial VI and the opening strikes land on Yama / Culann / Eochu Bres / Cethlenn in every run - Tri-Tachyon is weak because it stands by the landing, the Luddic Church never mobilises. Three runs are three seeds of play on one map; a new sector needs `newgame-prep.ps1` (the user asked, 2026-10-06). A second sector, `save_AphelionDysnomia_4103534775338436064` (generated 2026-10-06 18:56), runs as hw34 (`sbs.ps1 -Base`).
- **Why does the swarm claim nothing before the war on a new seed (hw132/hw133, 2026-10-09)?** no forge can launch a wave before the war: `ownAvailableForLaunch` lets a colony send only the fleets it holds above `garrisonReserve`, a count of swarms from the size table (half `desiredGarrisonCount`, 1-6), and counts FLEETS (`countLiveGarrison`), while under posture the builder stops once held FP reaches the want (`wantsGrowth`) - one or two fleets (the founding's ~700 FP seed fleet, grown fleets embodying several swarms). hw133a on the eve of the opening strike: Unhcegila 5 fleets against a reserve of 6, holding 2,018 FP of a 1,678 want; every colony above its FP want and under its fleet reserve, from day ~640 to the strike (`Spread target: none - candidates 176, no forge 176`). The strike's war unlocks it (claims within days of it). ck5's pre-war spread (from day 1,553, on the 2026-10-08 13:15 build) had pressure transfers between its hives (14 reinforcements arrived before the first claim) that gave colonies extra fleets. hw134 (current build from day 0 on ck5's own sector): first claim day 1,856 / 1,614 against ck5's 1,553, 11 / 13 hives at day ~2,050 against ck5's 32 at the opening; ck5's pre-war spread was the pressure pass fabricating for "short" quiet colonies (13 fabrications; 2 since the fit floor, f65b0bd2), each landing as a fleet. Options A/B/C in `handover-2026-10-08-morning.md` 36, A recommended, the user's call. -> `hive-garrison-and-upkeep.md` "The fleet count locks".
- **Why did the ck3 swarm open the war at 13 hives (hw70) where ck2's opened at 37 (hw69)?** The chest rule (`ThreatStance.evaluate`, 54db5cdc 2026-10-08 01:28) ran in ck3's pre-war and not in ck2's (checkpointed 2026-10-07 22:13). With 4 hives the fund saves 150 FP a month, so a 12-month horizon is 1,800 FP and the chest read full on day 707 with nothing fieldable (the cheapest prong 8,244 FP); "the fleets are fed before expansion" grew the home fleets to 3x ck2's, their upkeep put the supplies in deficit, and `ThreatFuel.canFound` founds nothing without a supplies surplus while the chest is full - every Seeding Swarm held from day 1856 to 2129 with 188k-402k fuel and 144k-175k supplies in stock, the pending claims at the cap. Geography is not it: both hives sit 19-33 ly from the nearest faction world, and both sectors found nothing between day ~340 and ~1470 (fuel under 500 until the plants came). `game-runs-2.md` 43 hw70. Fixed 2026-10-08 (the user's pick, full = fieldable): `ThreatStance.evaluate` reads the chest full only if the fund also covers `ThreatOffensive.cheapestProngCost` (the cheapest prong the fuel in stock can sail); `ck3` remade as `ck5` (32 hives at the opening). hw72 (ck5) confirms: hives 32 -> 174 / 130, the humans down to 5 / 3 colonies; hw71a (ck2, same build) 39 -> 83, humans 10 - `game-runs-2.md` 43. The fund banks 300k+ FP again (the hw62 hoard shape at scale) - open.
- **What did the navy rule do (hw33, 2026-10-06)?** Decided the war instead of stalling it: hw33a the humans eradicate the swarm (0 hives by d3485, navies 78-117k FP free); hw33b the swarm takes Chicomoztoc at its third landing (d2481), Hegemony eradicates the hive and loses its only yards for good (0 FP/mo, forward-base Heavy Industry unpayable at 27k supplies), swarm 128 hives at the end. Hunting forces waiting on hulls 57-440 (hw32 1,155-2,121); supplies now bind the humans (15-55k a faction at the end where the war goes on). `game-runs-2.md` 33.
- **Why did no ally send the Hegemony a unit in hw33b (2026-10-06)?** `ThreatCoalition.allyAid` fired 0 times in all of hw31-hw33: its trigger `ThreatAidRequests.shortageStanding` reads vanilla's peacetime market deficit, never the war reserve or the stock plan, and a depot empty of war stock is no shortage to it. Since 2026-10-06 `aidStockPlans` sends a real convoy on the stock plan's word (`ThreatFactionStock.aidNeed`), any two war factions not hostile to each other, no standing or chance roll; and a base holds the shortage's producer's price above its floor (`hold`, `ThreatReserves.floor`). `player-aid.md` 5, `frontlines.md`.
- **Why did hw34b's swarm do nothing for ten years (2026-10-06)?** Its bootstrap wave to Blue, the Ala system's only volatiles world, stalled alive in the system from war day 159 to 3579 (dump: kind WAVE, 737 FP, eta 0); `tryBuildLink` builds no fuel plant without `groupHasVolatiles`, so fuel stayed 0 and no seed, scout or strike could sail, while the stock plan read "fuel 0, demand 0, holds". Fixed by the stall rule in `checkWaveArrivals` (`seedingWaveStallDays`): re-steered once, then a bootstrap wave is moved to its planet. `hive-stocks-and-upkeep.md` "A wave that stalls".
- **Does trade through the war move supplies (hw35, 2026-10-06)?** Barely: 32 allied convoys in three runs, 26 of them fuel. `ThreatFactionStock.surplus` needs banking >= trailing demand and four months in stock, and no war faction's supplies get there while the war runs (16-51k a faction at the end); fuel, hoarded at 250k-2.9M, trades freely across standings. The human bind stays supplies production - yards at 5,000 behind a metals gate against a forge on every hive. `game-runs-2.md` 35.
- **What do hulls at home cost (2026-10-06, final)?** Vanilla's patrols nothing from the war stocks, either side - their Military Base / Nexus demand and shortages are vanilla's (fleet size and quality fall, and the standing figure reads that). Only the navy a faction's yards built beyond the table pays: `ThreatHulls.builtAtHome` (min(built, free)) x its measured maintenance per FP x `standingUpkeepMult` a month from the reserves above floor and hold; unpaid / rate FP of built hulls lost, the unpaid booked as demand; yards bank built hulls only when the surplus keeps them (`suppliesKeepFP`). The hive's garrison is not charged and not supplies-gated (both tried and cut the same evening, `game-runs-2.md` 36-37). Log: `Hulls: … standing upkeep paid X of Y supplies, Z FP starved`. `docs/hull-pool.md` 2.
- **Did standing upkeep settle the navies (hw36-37)?** The charge alone did not (hw36a: the garrison grew on forge FP to the want, the charge took every supply, the strikes starved, no war); with growth bound by the supplies surplus (hw37) both navies sit under the line - upkeep paid 98-100%, growth waits logged hundreds of times, humans 4-15k FP, swarm 29-57k FP spending all it makes - and the humans won all three on sieges (84-106 a game, 25-35 hives eradicated). `game-runs-2.md` 36-37.
- **Does the cut-back (innate patrols, tangible actions) give a war that goes either way (hw38-39)?** Yes: six games on the second sector, swarm 2 / humans 2 / open 2, strikes 25-114 and sieges 129-244 a game (hw37: 9-24 strikes, 3-0 humans). `game-runs-2.md` 38-39.
- **Is the cut-back balanced across sectors (hw40-41)?** No: the first sector (hw4, `AmaruDugas`) goes swarm 6-0 on spread - founded 112-167 a game against 25-86 eradicated; hives fall only to ground fronts and the daily sieges are beaten by innate garrisons (188 of 255 expeditions aborted with 0 landings in hw40a). Pending the user: founding pace, what a siege does to a hive, yard output. `game-runs-2.md` 40-41.
- **Never launch a batch while the last one is still finishing (2026-10-08, 01:52).** `sbs.ps1` ends a batch by copying dumps and restoring the user's game settings ("user settings restored" in `sbs-status.txt`, 1-2 minutes after the last game's REACHED line). hw63 was launched in that window: hw63a loaded under the restored settings, ran 918 game days unlogged (its log opened at war day 3102, Aug 30 c214, with the hive starved and the forges rebuilding) and hw63b's clock never started. Wait for `done` in the status file, then launch.
- **`MiscAcademyFleetCreator.createData` NullPointerException ("from" is null) in a late war (hw63a, war day ~4300, 88 hives)?** Vanilla's: `pickSourceMarket` found no market to send an academy fleet from once the swarm had taken most of the core, and vanilla dereferences it unguarded. It is a fatal: hw84b (2026-10-08, two human worlds left, both on the send timeout) crashed from `CampaignEngine.advance` at war day 4704, the moment of the swarm's victory. Guarded since 2026-10-08 by `ThreatAcademyFleetGuard.install` (onGameLoad): a subclass under vanilla's id returns no route when no market can send, as vanilla's pilgrim creator does.
- **Why do the humans found forward bases faster than they lose them, and what binds them now (hw63, 2026-10-08)?** hw63b/c founded 111 / 145 links and lost 126 / 132, 70 of b's to Threat strikes of ~650 FP, while every faction's plan read `hulls 0 free of 1,300-1,900, 1,000-1,800 to rebuild at 0-300/mo` and fuel for 24-226 months: a link's founding was gated on fuel and supplies (`canFund`, `canPayVoyage`) and a rear link needed no guard, so the surplus bought paper bases the swarm's score counted as worlds destroyed. Since hw65 (BASES BOUND BY HULLS - confirmed by hw65: bases founded 3 / 29 / 84 against hw64's 136 / 61 / 109, lost to strikes 3 / 18 / 40 against 64 / 47 / 70, foundings held for hulls 84-167): `ThreatFrontlines.planFor` founds a link only while the faction's free hulls (`ThreatHulls.freeFP`) cover the site's guard need (`guardNeed`), logging `Frontline: F founds no link at S - N FP of hulls free, its guard needs M`. The humans' other binder is unbuilt: their hull answer is a Heavy Industry at a size-3+ *link* (`buildStep`), never at a core world, so yards razed in the core are never replaced (0-500 FP/mo at every hw63 end). -> `frontlines.md` "Garrisons: no paper bases"
- **Where do the humans' hulls go once their yards are gone (hw65, 2026-10-08)?** Into relief fed in piecemeal: `sendRelief` built whatever the base could pay each ground-front poll - 4-10 FP with the pool at 0 free - and sailed it at armies of 1-6k FP. hw65a/b/c: 1,881 / 2,888 / 3,356 sends, 34k / 61k / 60k FP a game, 696 / 1,482 / 1,691 relief fleets destroyed (698 sends from Ailmar to Eldfell alone over 670 days). Every hull the yards made went that way; bases bound by hulls (a46ad657) could not help while that drain ran. -> Since hw66 (RELIEF ONLY IF ENOUGH - confirmed by hw66: sends 127 / 266 / 390, relief fleets lost 5 / 62 / 95, the humans end with 19.6k / 9 / 16.1k FP of hulls free and the sector contested in two of three): `reliefEnough` - a relief sails only when what the base can field with the guards bound there outweighs the army (`reliefNeed`), the swarm's rally rule under the same `systemDefenceOnlyIfEnough` switch; `reliefOwed` counts only a relief that would sail. `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Only if enough".
- **Why does a player colony with no industry get +3,000 fuel / +1,500 supplies a month (2026-10-11)?** Vanilla's Waystation: on a PLAYER-owned market it adds a stockpiling bonus of size x `stockpileMultExcess` (0.5) units per commodity to the local-resources submarket (`Waystation.apply`, player-only branch), x the econ unit (fuel 1,500, supplies 750), capped at `stockpileMaxMonths` (5) and filled at a fifth a month. The board reads vanilla's figure (`ThreatReserves.vanillaStockpilePer30`). NPC colonies have no such submarket: their ledger banks surplus only (`accrualPer30`); for them a Waystation is the depot gate plus fuel/supplies demand.
- **Why was an order held with fuel and deposit paid and the hold 57-98% full (hw157)?** The hold is priced per point at the faction's measured supplies/FP (`ThreatReach.sortieHold`) but drawn for the built hulls' own maintenance (`ThreatFrontlines.maintenancePerMonth`), because vanilla's fleet factory builds 109-114% of the points asked (hw158's draw log: built 111% of asked, the hulls' rate 1.12 vs 1.10 measured - the rate was never the gap; hw157's "short on the hold alone" was the quiet log's one-line-per-base artefact). Since 2026-10-11 14:40 `buildTaskForce` trims the smallest warships to the hold the base paid (floor `TRIM_FLOOR` 75%, log `Order trimmed at`; the fuel and deposit want shrinks with the hulls - hw158's 728 trimmed fleets were all held against the untrimmed want, fixed 15:20) and the rate is measured on the faction's task forces alone (fleets with `MEM_HOLD`). The draw log shows `N FP of M asked, x supplies/FP/mo vs y measured`.
- **Why did a relief force stand down "supplies low, 0 aboard" (hw157c)?** `ThreatFleetOrders.absorb` merged the relief's fleets without their holds (`MEM_HOLD` not in its key list); fixed 2026-10-11 14:40. 265 of 278 relief stand-downs had supplies aboard - the relief stays with no term (`reliefStays`) on a hold sized for `sortieStayDays`, so it rotates home when low, by rule 4.
- **Why did a forward base launch a hunt with an empty hold (gs1, hw156)?** It cannot now: `buildTaskForce` sails an NPC fleet only fully provisioned from its own base, else `Order held at` (`stagedLaunch`, `fleet-provisions.md` rule 7). Before 2026-10-11 14:30 `ThreatSoftening.payableFP` sized the force on the base plus every market reaching it (`huntDonors`), `buildTaskForce` drew from the base alone and launched whatever it got, and `payFromDonors` topped only the deposit (`MEM_FUEL`/`MEM_SUPPLIES`) afterwards, never the hold.
- **Fleets carry their provisions (the user, 2026-10-11: "a navy needs to take lump sum supplies before heading out ... or are you saying they teleport supplies from base to the navy while its 10LY away").** Both sides. Beside the hull deposit a fleet draws a HOLD at launch - its ships' maintenance for the voyage there and back plus `sortieStayDays` (90; hunts and task forces were out 65-90 days at the median in hw10a-c, sieges 45-80 draw to return) or a siege's `siegeStayDays` - burns it per pass (`ThreatUpkeep`, `ThreatReturns.MEM_HOLD`), restocks to its launch load from a base of its faction in its system when below the leg home (`ThreatBases.inSystem`, knob `fleetResupply`), and with none to restock from stands down "supplies low" / `shortOfSupplies`. A siege's hold rides the expedition (`ThreatPurgeFGI.hold`), burned at `fightingFP` spawned or not. Nothing is billed at home any more; fleets and sieges from an older save keep the old billing until they relaunch. Player fleets refund the deposit by the NPC rule now. -> `fleet-provisions.md`
- **The player's built navy pays standing upkeep (2026-10-11).** `ThreatHulls.maintain` no longer skips the player: built hulls at home cost their maintenance a month from the colony stockpiles, a hull-month unpaid is a built hull struck off with a "Navy Unpaid" notice; the upkeep shows in the log (`Hulls: ... standing upkeep paid`); the row tooltip no longer lists it (the pool block was cut 2026-10-11). The Waystation stockpile income is small and capped; this and the flat sortie deposit were the real player advantage. -> `fleet-provisions.md`, `hull-pool.md`
- **hw165-hw171 overnight baseline (2026-10-13, 21 random games from day 0 on the hold-refund jar, six sectors repeated): the swarm's 12, contested 4, stalled or falling 5** (hw167b, hw170a, hw171a, hw171b, hw171c). The five share the fuel drain: the humans land on hives in volume (hw171a 975 hive siege slices in m101-112), the orbit bombardment of the landed fronts loads and reloads 10-day holds (hw171a 1.23M loaded + 1.0M rearmed of 3.8M income, 639k home, 922k fired to grind out 13 fronts = 71k a front), the stock sits at 0 from ~m112, founding stops (`sends held 46`) and the ground eradications outrun it (30 of hw171a's 32 after the stock emptied). One sector spans 59-126 hives on one jar; three games a sector reads a fix. Options O1 (bombard budget above a founding reserve, recommended) / O2 / O3 in `game-runs-2.md` 48.
- **DECISION 2026-10-13 07:10 (user, after the hw165-hw171 read): guns while the fuel is good, soldiers while it is not.** "Try O1 but rather let's make them only take fuel for bombard in the first place if reserves are good. Otherwise they can go with no fuel and just reinforce the front right by breaking up ships. Conversely a navy that is small but with lots of fuel doesn't break up ships but bombards a lot instead." Built 07:35: a hold loads only from the stock above `swarmBombardReserveMonths` (1) of the hive's fuel income plus the founding kit (`ThreatGroundFronts.bombardReserve`); a swarm fleet over its own besieged hive that the stock would not arm breaks up its ships into the hive's ground troops (`breakUpForHive`, `hiveTroops`, knob `threatinc_hiveBreakUpEnabled`), which stand in the hive's defence and counter-attack and bleed like marines; a fleet the stock arms keeps its hulls. -> `ground-war-orbit-control.md` "Guns while the fuel is good". First trial hw172 from hw171a / hw171b / hw167b's w2,850 snapshots.
- **hw172 (2026-10-13 08:05, the fuel-or-ships rule from the fallen games' w2,850 snapshots): a 80 hives (peak 91) vs hw171a 69, b 72 vs hw171b 59, c 87 vs hw167b 75.** Only a went under the reserve: stock pinned at 76-96k (never 0), 40 break-ups of 7,261 FP into 9,020 hive troops, Cores destroyed 19 vs 31, founding flowed (sends held 3 vs 46). b (1 break-up) and c (0) read the dice; hw173 repeats hw172 unchanged. The reserve depth (1 month of income) is my call, the user's to change. -> `game-runs-2.md` 49.
- **hw173 (2026-10-13 08:35, hw172 repeated unchanged): a 108 (climbing) / b 82 / c 87 - two games a snapshot, six of six above the source games (hw171a 69, hw171b 59, hw167b 75).** a, the game the rule touches, 80 / 108 with 40 / 64 break-ups (7.3k / 11.6k FP into 9.0k / 11.3k hive troops), stock pinned at the reserve and never 0, Cores destroyed 19 / 14 vs 31; b 72 / 82 and c 87 / 87 on 1-3 / 0 break-ups are the dice, consistently up. The three fallen games are the swarm's on this build; next read = random seeds from day 0, three games a sector, against the hw165-hw171 table. -> `game-runs-2.md` 50.
- **Why does a colony with 902 defenders look worse on the fronts table than one with 370 (user, 2026-10-17, Haven vs Arkoman in Zuan)?** Three columns read the attacker, not the defender. **Attrition** is the front's own headcount x `frontPushLossPer30Days` 0.30 / 30 x `threatPushLossMult` 2 x veterancy (`attritionPer30Days`): 433 troops lose 8.0 a day, 307 lose 5.7; defenders enter only through `pushLossFactor`, which is 1 at `groundStrengthExponent` 1.0. **Counter** is `frontCounterAttackDays` 40 / (stability / 10) / tempo (`Theatre.COLONY.counterAttackInterval`): Haven's 86 d is stability about 4 under the blockade, Arkoman's 44 d about 9 - the tooltip now says so ("Musters every N days at stability S"). The **counter-attack figure** is garrison plus armed marines x `marineCounterAttackMult` 0.25 (75 + 827 x 0.25 = 282), so 902 defenders go over the top as 282 and barely beat a 265 front. Where the defenders do count is the push: `pushDaysEstimate` = 20 days x defender / strength, clamped 0.5-3x, so Haven's 3.4:1 reads 60 days a district (the clamp) against Arkoman's 20, and Falls ~286 d against ~85 d; the push never stalls, only the counter-attack ends a front. -> `ground-war-defenders.md` 3b, `ground-war-fronts.md` "The front"
- **What happened to Haven (user, 2026-10-17: "one sat bomb" giving -9 stability and 164-day disruptions)?** No saturation: an unspawned 600 FP Threat strike resolved its whole siege in one frame on arrival (`ThreatStrikeFGI.abstractSiege`, the one-frame loop): 18 slices x 3 d = 54 d of tactical bombardment against 612 defence, condition 0.90 -> 0.11, unrest 10 x 0.89 = 9, clocks 0.89 x 180 wear days = 160 on Ground Defenses and the Military Base, then 61 FP broke up into 308 troops and 401 landed - all in one tick, with no time to answer. **DECISION (user, same day): the swarm strike runs a day at a time like the NPC sieges.** Built as `ThreatStrikeFGI.advanceDaily` under the same knob (`abstractSiegeDaily`), untested. -> `ground-war-code-paths.md` 8 "ThreatStrikeFGI runs its own copy"
- **DECISION 2026-10-18 17:30 (the user, after the audit: "ok try hive cover and size upkeep humans").** Both built: `ThreatHiveCover` (the hive's one stock covers a hive world's vanilla shortages as a human depot does, fuel from fuel, the rest from supplies at the base-price ratio; knob `threatinc_hiveCover`) and `ThreatCoreUpkeep` (the hive's size-upkeep table billed to every mobilised faction's core colony from its own depot, feeding convoys refill it, a size lost per starveDays unpaid, growth vanilla's; knobs `threatinc_coreSizeUpkeep`, `threatinc_coreSizeUpkeepPlayer` false). A per-world hive ledger was proposed and WITHDRAWN (the user: "sounds like an even bigger nerf to the threat") - the pool stays the hive's depot. -> `hive-stocks-and-upkeep.md` "Hive cover and core size upkeep". Trial hw200 (set A) after the hw199 read.
- **Why does the humans' reserve cover a shortage and the hive's stock not (user, 2026-10-18 17:15)?** Because the hive had no depot: `ThreatReserves` skips every Threat market, the hive holds one pooled stock the mod's ledger alone reads, and nothing ever wrote it into a hive world's vanilla commodity data - so a blockaded hive world's machinery shortage killed its guns while a human depot's cover kept the human guns up. Closed by `ThreatHiveCover` (above). Where do the hive's feeding swarms come from? The nearest hive with a spare tier-0 swarm lends the hull and pays the fuel; the cargo is debited from the pool at the target's allotment (`ThreatFeeding.shipHive`, `pickHiveDonor`).
- **DECISION 2026-10-18 17:35 (the user, reversing 17:30): NO size upkeep on human core colonies - "I want humans to stay true to vanilla".** `threatinc_coreSizeUpkeep` false (code kept, never trialled); hive cover's fate pending. A kit (5,500 supplies + 800 fuel + ~1,000-1,500 trip fuel + a 200 FP escort that sails home from a rear site) is ten days of hegemony's 15-20k/mo supplies income, so stock never gates founding; the count comes from the planner's shape: six factions each chain toward every hive they know (hw209a: 19 hives, 49 bases, Gamma Mu chained by 4 factions). Core builds: 80-120 MILITARY builds a game (Patrol HQ -> Military Base -> High Command, stations, batteries) and 10-18 producers (Heavy Industry / fuel plants answering a shortage), with 5-147 producer starts held on "supplies short" (the faction verdict: hegemony runs dry at 43k/mo spent on 20k income); yards output across the humans stays flat (4.4k -> 4.2k FP/mo in hw209a, new yards offsetting colonies lost), and the military builds raise the vanilla patrol table and the hull pool with it: all humans 10k FP at mobilisation -> 21-27k by month 40 in every game, bases or not. Monitoring: `Hulls:` (monthly per faction: pool, built, out, lost, free, yards FP/mo), `Reserve ledger:` / `Reserve budget:` (monthly per market) and `ThreatFactionStock.describe` (on each core-build answer) are all logged, but no digest or ledger section reads them - nothing summarises human navy or stock growth.** (game-runs-2.md s54)

The garrison leash (list) and the reinforcement leash (`REINFORCE_TARGET_KEY`) re-ordered it against each other every frame; it never moved. - **Why does a human faction sit on a free navy and invade so little (hw217a / hw218c / hw219a, 2026-10-19 12:30: the Hegemony 39k of 40k FP free, 13-19 council sieges a game)? MARINES.** The invade-pass line (hw219a, 739 passes): of the systems with no paying base, 594 were "marines", 67 "cannot pay", 22 "its front finishes first" - `ThreatPlays.payingBase` wants `siegeRaidStrNeeded` x `minMarinesFraction` (1.0, `npcSiegeFullStrength`) pooled at the base and its donors (`IncursionManager.siegeMarinesPooled`), 1,100-2,700 marines a landing, while the faction raises ~40-77 marines per market per 30 days (`ThreatReserves.accrualPer30`: vanilla surplus x `reserveTroopSurplusMult` 0.5 - halved 2026-09-30 when banking outran landings 13:1 - plus the militia), ~700 a month across 14 markets; the reserve fell 4,443 -> 2,052 over the game. The yards-first navy outgrew the marine trickle: a landing every 2-4 months a faction, whatever the navy. Bombardment cannot substitute (`bombardPlan` wears fortifications only; the sizing already takes that step). OPTIONS for the user (strategy before knobs): P1 marines follow the war footing - a marine-bound faction raises marines from its surplus supplies as it builds hulls from its yards (a `ThreatFactionStock` want like `yardWant`, priced, closed economy) - recommended; P2 `reserveTroopSurplusMult` back to 1.0 (the knob; banks vanilla's surplus, unconnected to the navy); P3 coalition marines pooled like hulls (everyone's trickle is small). CAVEAT: the humans already lean in 2 of 4 N1b games; more landings push toward a human win unattended (humans-must-not-win rule) unless the swarm answers. (game-runs-2.md s54, war-council-invasions.md 2)

(2) ALLIED STAGING: `ThreatCoalition.stagingHost(base, guestFid)` - a base (`isBase`) of another mobilised NPC faction at Welcoming or better (`willingness` 0.5), not hostile, never the player's; knob `threatinc_alliedStaging`. Such bases join the council's cluster bases (`ThreatWarCouncil`, so `inReach` counts them), every base pick takes them (`ThreatPlays.usable`: payingBase, baseOf, richestBase, the feint's base, `baseFor` no longer reads a hosted base as lost), the chain's `planFor` counts one within `frontlineReachLY` as reached (no own chain toward that hive). Stock rule A: the base's stock is the depot's, drawn by whoever launches there; the host's planner fills it and the guest's markets in reach join as donors (`ThreatConvoys.guestDonors`, convoys flagged aid and bound for the host, logged `Staging: <guest> sails X -> Y (<host>'s base): allied staging`). A base stages several plays now (`PlayStaging` keyed base#play with `factionId` / `baseId`, `playStagingsOf`, `siegeStock` sums the wants; old saves' entries still read). The siege's hull gates read the GUEST's pool (`ThreatHulls.freeFP(faction)` in `siegeCanPay` and the launch, was the base's). Logs name a host: `from Cruor (Sindrian Diktat's)`, `Staging: Y (hegemony) hosts tritachyon's play ... (allied staging)`. STILL POOLED (instant, haul fee) and open for the user: link upkeep (`ThreatFrontlines.pooled` / `payFromOthers`), founding kits, ordnance (`payOrdnance`), the shared Threat stock feeding hive worlds (facts "Do the Threat send physical convoys").
